    *   **Traditional Chinese (繁體中文):** 該公司在新的網路安全措施上投入巨資，以**降低數據洩露的風險**。
    *   *Explanation:* "Mitigate the risk" implies reducing the likelihood or severity of a negative event. 降低 (jiàngdī) is commonly used for "reducing" something like risk, cost, or level.

3.  **Humanitarian/Social Context:**
    *   **English:** Humanitarian organizations work tirelessly to **mitigate the suffering of refugees** in conflict zones.
    *   **Traditional Chinese (繁體中文):** 人道主義組織不懈努力，以**減輕衝突地區難民的痛苦**。
    *   *Explanation:* "Mitigate suffering" means to alleviate or lessen distress. 減輕 (jiǎnqīng) directly translates to "lighten" or "lessen" and is perfect for reducing burden or suffering.

4.  **Health/Medical Context:**
    *   **English:** Pain relievers can help **mitigate the discomfort** associated with a sprained ankle, but rest is also crucial.
    *   **Traditional Chinese (繁體中文):** 止痛藥可以幫助**緩解扭傷腳踝帶來的不適**，但休息同樣關鍵。
    *   *Explanation:* In a medical context, "mitigate discomfort" means to alleviate or ease pain/unpleasant sensations. 緩解 (huǎnjiě) means to "relieve" or "ease" and is frequently used for symptoms or tension.

5.  **Legal/General Context (using "mitigating" as an adjective):**
    *   **English:** The judge considered the defendant's difficult childhood as a **mitigating circumstance** when determining the sentence.
    *   **Traditional Chinese (繁體中文):** 法官在量刑時，將被告艱難的童年視為**減輕罪責的情節**。
    *   *Explanation:* "Mitigating circumstance" refers to a factor that makes an offense less serious or deserving of a lesser punishment. 減輕罪責的情節 (jiǎnqīng zuìzé de qíngjié) is a standard legal phrase meaning "circumstance that lessens culpability."

---

Remember, for the GRE, focus not just on the definition but also on how "mitigate" is used in various contexts and with what common nouns. Understanding these collocations will greatly help you comprehend complex sentences and use the word effectively in your essays.

## precarious

Let's break down the word "precarious" for your GRE prep!

---

## Word: **Precarious**

### Meaning / Definition

**Precarious** (adjective) means:

1.  **Not securely held or in position; dangerously likely to fall or collapse.** (e.g., a precarious balance, a precarious rock)
2.  **Dependent on chance circumstances or unknown conditions; uncertain; insecure; risky.** (e.g., a precarious livelihood, a precarious peace)

In essence, it describes something that is **unstable, uncertain, dangerous, or on the verge of collapse/failure.**

**Synonyms:** insecure, uncertain, unstable, shaky, wobbly, tottering, rocky, risky, hazardous, perilous, chancy, dicey.
**Antonyms:** secure, stable, safe, certain, firm, reliable.

### Common Collocations

"Precarious" often collocates with words describing:

*   **Positions/States:**
    *   **precarious position:** (physical or situational)
    *   **precarious balance:** (physical or metaphorical)
    *   **precarious state/condition:** (of health, economy, etc.)
    *   **precarious hold/grip:** (on something physical or abstract like power)
    *   **precarious perch:** (a high, unstable resting place)

*   **Livelihood/Future:**
    *   **precarious existence/life:** (living in poverty or instability)
    *   **precarious livelihood:** (an unstable way of earning money)
    *   **precarious future:** (an uncertain or risky future)

*   **Situations/Relationships:**
    *   **precarious situation:** (a dangerous or uncertain circumstance)
    *   **precarious peace:** (a fragile peace that could easily break)
    *   **precarious relationship:** (an unstable or easily broken relationship)

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples showcasing "precarious" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Physical Instability / Danger**
    *   **English:** The rock climber found himself in a **precarious position** on the cliff face, with only a tiny ledge for his feet.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 攀岩者發現自己身處懸崖邊一個**岌岌可危的位置**，腳下只有一小塊突出岩石。
        *   *(Note: 岌岌可危 (jíjí kě wēi) means "in imminent danger" or "on the verge of collapse.")*

2.  **Context: Financial / Economic Insecurity**
    *   **English:** After losing his job, his family's financial situation became **precarious**, as they struggled to pay their bills.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 失去工作後，他家庭的經濟狀況變得**岌岌可危**，他們努力支付帳單。
        *   *(Note: Here, 岌岌可危 also conveys the sense of being highly unstable and on the brink of collapse.)*

3.  **Context: Unstable Peace / Relationship**
    *   **English:** The peace treaty between the two nations remained **precarious**, threatened by simmering resentments on both sides.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 兩國之間的和平條約依然**不穩定**（或**岌岌可危**），因為雙方潛伏的不滿隨時可能使其破裂。
        *   *(Note: 不穩定 (bù wěn dìng) means "unstable." 岌岌可危 can also work if the threat is very immediate.)*

4.  **Context: Uncertain Livelihood / Existence**
    *   **English:** Many low-wage workers lead a **precarious existence**, always on the brink of financial hardship.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多低薪工人過著**朝不保夕的生活**，總是在經濟困難的邊緣掙扎。
        *   *(Note: 朝不保夕 (zhāo bù bǎo xī) is an idiom meaning "living from hand to mouth," or "not knowing where one's next meal is coming from," perfectly capturing a precarious existence.)*

5.  **Context: Maintaining a Fragile Balance / Control**
    *   **English:** The startup maintained a **precarious hold** on the market, constantly battling against larger, more established competitors.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這家新創公司在市場上保持著**不穩定的領先地位**（或**岌岌可危的市場佔有率**），不斷與更大、更成熟的競爭對手抗衡。
        *   *(Note: 不穩定的 (bù wěn dìng de) "unstable," or 岌岌可危的 (jíjí kě wēi de) "critical/perilous" are suitable here.)*

---

By understanding these nuances and seeing "precarious" in action across various contexts, you'll be well-prepared for its appearance on the GRE!

## forbearance

Okay, let's break down "forbearance" for your GRE studies. It's a fantastic word to know!

---

### Meaning of "Forbearance"

**Forbearance** (noun) has a core meaning of **patient self-control, restraint, or tolerance**. It implies refraining from exercising a right or enforcing something that is due.

It can be understood in a few key contexts:

1.  **General Patience/Self-Restraint:** The act of being patient, tolerant, or self-controlled, especially when faced with provocation, difficulty, or a challenging person. It's about holding back from reacting impulsively or harshly.
2.  **Leniency/Tolerance:** The quality of being lenient or tolerant towards others, even when they might deserve stricter treatment or when you have the power to impose it.
3.  **Financial/Legal Postponement:** (Most common in modern usage, especially in business/finance) The act of refraining from enforcing a debt, obligation, or right that is due. This often takes the form of an agreement to delay payments, repossessions, or legal action for a certain period. Think of a "forbearance agreement" on a mortgage.

**Key takeaway for GRE:** Think of it as a form of *restraint* or *postponement*, often with an underlying sense of patience or leniency.

---

### Common Collocations of "Forbearance"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing them helps you use the word naturally and understand it better in context.

*   **Verbs:**
    *   **show forbearance:** (e.g., "She showed great forbearance.")
    *   **exercise forbearance:** (e.g., "You must exercise forbearance in this situation.")
    *   **practice forbearance:** (e.g., "Practicing forbearance can lead to peace.")
    *   **grant forbearance:** (e.g., "The bank granted forbearance on the loan.")
    *   **request forbearance:** (e.g., "He requested forbearance from his landlord.")
    *   **extend forbearance:** (e.g., "They extended forbearance for another three months.")

*   **Adjectives:**
    *   **great forbearance:** (e.g., "It took great forbearance to remain calm.")
    *   **patient forbearance:** (e.g., "His patient forbearance was commendable.")
    *   **mutual forbearance:** (e.g., "Mutual forbearance is key to a good relationship.")
    *   **financial forbearance:** (e.g., "Many applied for financial forbearance during the crisis.")
    *   **legal forbearance:** (e.g., "The court offered legal forbearance.")

*   **Prepositions:**
    *   **forbearance toward(s) someone/something:** (e.g., "show forbearance towards newcomers.")
    *   **forbearance on a debt/loan/mortgage:** (e.g., "forbearance on their mortgage payments.")

*   **Nouns:**
    *   **period of forbearance:** (e.g., "They agreed to a six-month period of forbearance.")
    *   **forbearance agreement:** (e.g., "He signed a forbearance agreement.")

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: General Patience/Self-Restraint**
    *   **English:** It took great **forbearance** for him to remain calm and composed despite the constant provocations from his opponent.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管他的對手不斷挑釁，他仍能保持冷靜和鎮定，這需要極大的**忍耐力**。

2.  **Context: Financial Postponement (Mortgage)**
    *   **English:** Due to the economic downturn, many homeowners are requesting **forbearance** from their mortgage lenders to temporarily suspend payments.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 由於經濟衰退，許多房主正向他們的抵押貸款機構請求**延期還款**，以暫停支付款項。

3.  **Context: Interpersonal Leniency/Tolerance**
    *   **English:** Mutual **forbearance** is essential for a harmonious partnership, allowing both sides to overlook minor disagreements and focus on common goals.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 互相**寬容**對於和諧的夥伴關係至關重要，它能讓雙方忽略細微的分歧，並專注於共同目標。

4.  **Context: Legal Leniency (Judge)**
    *   **English:** The judge showed remarkable **forbearance** in dealing with the unruly defendant, opting for a stern warning rather than immediate contempt charges.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 法官在處理行為不端的被告時展現出非凡的**寬容**，選擇給予嚴厲警告而非立即指控藐視法庭。

5.  **Context: Principle/Virtue**
    *   **English:** Practicing **forbearance** in difficult situations can prevent escalation and lead to more peaceful resolutions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在困難情境中保持**忍耐**能防止事態升級，並帶來更和平的解決方案。

6.  **Context: Historical/Diplomatic**
    *   **English:** The elder statesman urged **forbearance** and dialogue between the warring factions, stressing that violence would only perpetuate suffering.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位資深政治家敦促交戰各方保持**克制**並進行對話，強調暴力只會讓苦難持續下去。

---

I hope this detailed explanation, with collocations and varied examples, helps you master "forbearance" for your GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## epitome

Understanding "epitome" is excellent for the GRE, as it's a word that conveys a very specific and nuanced meaning often tested.

---

### Meaning of "Epitome"

The word "epitome" (pronounced uh-PIT-uh-mee) refers to:

1.  **A perfect or typical example of a particular quality or type.** It's not just *an* example, but *the* *ultimate*, *quintessential*, or *most representative* example that embodies all the essential characteristics of something.
2.  **A summary or abstract of a written work, or a representation of something in a smaller or reduced form.** (This is a less common usage in everyday conversation but relevant to its etymology).

**In simpler terms:** When you say something is the "epitome of X," you mean it perfectly embodies, represents, or is the ultimate example of quality X.

---

### Common Collocations

The most common collocation for "epitome" is:

*   **the epitome of + [abstract noun/quality]**

Here are some common qualities or concepts that often follow "the epitome of":

*   **Positive qualities:**
    *   the epitome of **elegance**
    *   the epitome of **luxury**
    *   the epitome of **kindness**
    *   the epitome of **virtue**
    *   the epitome of **professionalism**
    *   the epitome of **success**
    *   the epitome of **perfection**
    *   the epitome of **courage**
    *   the epitome of **modernity**
    *   the epitome of **efficiency**
*   **Neutral/Negative qualities:**
    *   the epitome of **chaos**
    *   the epitome of **inefficiency**
    *   the epitome of **disorder**
    *   the epitome of **simplicity** (can be positive or neutral depending on context)

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts (with Traditional Chinese Translations)

Here are multiple examples showcasing "epitome" in different contexts:

1.  **Context: Describing a person embodying a positive character trait.**
    *   **English:** Dr. Anya Sharma, with her unwavering dedication to her patients and empathetic demeanor, is truly the **epitome of compassion** in the medical field.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 安雅·沙瑪醫生對病患的堅定奉獻和同理心，在醫學領域中實是**慈悲的典範**。
    *   *(Note: 典範 (diǎnfàn) means "model" or "exemplar," perfectly fitting "epitome" in a positive sense.)*

2.  **Context: Describing an object or design embodying a characteristic.**
    *   **English:** The newly unveiled luxury yacht, with its sleek design and state-of-the-art technology, is the **epitome of modern maritime engineering**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這艘新揭幕的豪華遊艇，以其流線型設計和最先進的技術，堪稱**現代海事工程的極致**。
    *   *(Note: 極致 (jízhì) means "ultimate" or "peak," conveying the sense of ultimate representation.)*

3.  **Context: Describing a situation or abstract concept, potentially negative.**
    *   **English:** The disorganized project meeting, lacking a clear agenda and effective leadership, was the **epitome of inefficiency**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那次雜亂無章的專案會議，既缺乏明確議程又缺乏有效領導，簡直是**效率低下的縮影**。
    *   *(Note: 縮影 (suōyǐng) means "miniature" or "epitome" in the sense of a smaller, concentrated representation of something larger or an abstract quality.)*

4.  **Context: Describing a historical figure or a cultural icon.**
    *   **English:** For many, Queen Elizabeth II was the **epitome of duty and steadfastness**, guiding her nation through decades of change.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 對許多人而言，伊莉莎白二世女王是**責任與堅毅的化身**，引領她的國家走過數十年的變革。
    *   *(Note: 化身 (huàshēn) means "incarnation" or "embodiment," suitable for describing a person who embodies abstract qualities.)*

5.  **Context: Describing a place or setting.**
    *   **English:** The serene lakeside cabin, nestled among ancient pines, was the **epitome of rustic tranquility**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那座靜謐的湖畔小屋，坐落在古老的松樹之間，是**鄉村寧靜的典範**。
    *   *(Note: Again, 典範 (diǎnfàn) works well here for a positive, perfect example.)*

---

**Key Takeaway for GRE:** When you encounter "epitome," think "the perfect example of" or "the ultimate representation of." It's almost always followed by "of" and then an abstract noun or quality. Mastering this word shows a sophisticated understanding of vocabulary.

## bombastic

That's a great word for GRE prep! Let's break down "bombastic."

---

## **Bombastic**

**Meaning:**

**Bombastic** (adjective) describes language that is **pompous, pretentious, and inflated**, often with the intention of impressing people, but ultimately lacking in substance or genuine meaning. It refers to speech or writing that sounds grand, important, or full of self-importance, but is actually empty, overly ornate, or exaggerated. It carries a negative connotation, suggesting insincerity or a desire to sound more profound than one actually is.

Think of it as language that is "full of hot air."

**Key characteristics:**

*   **Overblown/Inflated:** Uses excessively long words, complex sentences, or grand pronouncements.
*   **Pompous/Pretentious:** Aims to sound more intelligent, important, or authoritative than it is.
*   **Lacks Substance:** The impressive-sounding words often obscure a lack of real content, ideas, or practical solutions.
*   **Showy/Grandiose:** Intended to impress rather than inform or persuade genuinely.

---

**Common Collocations:**

When you see or use "bombastic," it often pairs with words related to speech, writing, or those who produce it:

*   **Bombastic rhetoric:** This is perhaps the most common collocation, referring to overly grand and empty political or persuasive speech.
*   **Bombastic speech / language:** General terms for inflated communication.
*   **Bombastic prose / writing:** Describes overly ornate or pretentious written work.
*   **Bombastic claims / pronouncements:** Exaggerated or self-important statements.
*   **Bombastic style / tone:** Refers to an overly dramatic or self-important manner of expression.
*   **Bombastic politician / speaker / orator:** A person known for using bombastic language.

---

**Example Sentences in Various Contexts & Traditional Chinese Translations:**

1.  **Context: Politics/Public Speaking**
    *   **English:** The politician's address, though filled with **bombastic rhetoric**, failed to convince voters of any actionable solutions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位政治家的演講儘管充滿了**誇大其詞的言辭**，卻未能說服選民提出任何可行的解決方案。

2.  **Context: Literature/Writing Style**
    *   **English:** Critics found the novelist's latest work to be **bombastic** and overwritten, prioritizing elaborate prose over genuine character development.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 評論家們認為這位小說家的最新作品**浮誇**且過於雕琢，將華麗的散文置於真正的人物塑造之上。

3.  **Context: Personal Communication/Claims**
    *   **English:** While his delivery was undeniably charismatic, the speaker's **bombastic tone** often overshadowed the message's true intent.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 雖然他的表達無可否認地很有魅力，但這位演講者**浮誇的語氣**常常掩蓋了訊息的真正意圖。

4.  **Context: Academic/Professional Advice**
    *   **English:** For a research paper, clarity and conciseness are key; avoid **bombastic language** that might obscure your arguments.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 對於研究論文而言，清晰和簡潔是關鍵；應避免使用**誇大其詞的語言**，以免模糊了你的論點。

5.  **Context: Describing a Person's Character (by their speech)**
    *   **English:** Despite his humble beginnings, the self-proclaimed guru's online videos were full of **bombastic claims** about his unique insights and miraculous cures.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管出身卑微，這位自稱大師的線上影片卻充滿了關於他獨特見解和神奇療法的**言過其實的宣稱**。

---

I hope this comprehensive explanation helps you master "bombastic" for the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## compliant

Okay, let's break down the word **"compliant"** for your GRE prep!

---

### Understanding "Compliant"

**Meaning:**

The adjective **"compliant"** primarily means **obedient, submissive, or conforming to a rule, standard, or request.** It describes something or someone that acts in accordance with, or yields to, a set of regulations, demands, or wishes.

Think of it in a few key ways:

1.  **Following Rules/Standards:** Adhering strictly to laws, policies, requirements, or specifications.
    *   *Example:* A product that meets all safety standards is "compliant."
2.  **Obedient/Submissive:** Willing to obey or agree to the wishes of others, sometimes without much resistance. This can have a slightly negative connotation if someone is *too* compliant (e.g., easily manipulated).
    *   *Example:* A child who always does what they're told is "compliant."
3.  **Amenable/Flexible (less common, but relevant in certain contexts):** Easily adapting or yielding to conditions.
    *   *Example:* A material that is "compliant" might easily bend or conform to a shape.

**Part of Speech:** Adjective
**Related Verb:** **Comply** (to act in accordance with a wish or command)

---

### Common Collocations

"Compliant" most frequently collocates with the preposition **"with"** and then a noun phrase indicating what is being conformed to.

*   **Compliant with:**
    *   ...**rules/regulations/laws:** *legally compliant, environmentally compliant*
    *   ...**standards/specifications:** *industry-compliant, technically compliant*
    *   ...**policies/guidelines/requirements:** *policy-compliant, safety-compliant*
    *   ...**terms/conditions:** *contract-compliant*
    *   ...**demands/requests:** (describing a person)
    *   ...**treatment/medication:** (describing a patient)

*   **Adjectives Modifying Compliant:**
    *   **Fully compliant**
    *   **Strictly compliant**
    *   **Legally compliant**
    *   **Environmentally compliant**
    *   **Technically compliant**

*   **Nouns Described by Compliant:**
    *   **Compliant system/software/product**
    *   **Compliant organization/company/business**
    *   **Compliant employee/student/patient**

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples with Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Business/Legal (Adhering to Regulations)**
    *   **English:** The new factory design must be **fully compliant with** all local environmental regulations before construction can begin.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 新工廠的設計在施工前必須**完全符合**所有當地環境法規。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "compliant" refers to meeting legal and environmental standards.

2.  **Context: Technology/Industry (Meeting Standards)**
    *   **English:** For the software to be widely adopted, it needs to be **compliant with** international data security standards.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 為了讓該軟體廣泛被採用，它需要**符合**國際資料安全標準。
    *   *Explanation:* This usage indicates that the software adheres to specific technical requirements.

3.  **Context: Healthcare/Medical (Patient Behavior)**
    *   **English:** A **compliant patient** who strictly follows the prescribed medication schedule generally experiences better recovery outcomes.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 一位**遵從醫囑的病人**若嚴格遵循規定的服藥時間表，通常會有更好的康復結果。
    *   *Explanation:* In medicine, "compliant" describes a patient who adheres to the doctor's instructions.

4.  **Context: Personal/Work (Obedient/Amenable Person)**
    *   **English:** She's a highly valued team member because she's always **compliant with** instructions and readily adapts to new tasks.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她是一位備受重視的團隊成員，因為她總是**服從**指示並樂於適應新任務。
    *   *Explanation:* This describes someone who is cooperative and follows directions well.

5.  **Context: Negative Connotation (Overly Submissive)**
    *   **English:** While generally good, his tendency to be overly **compliant** sometimes led him to agree to unreasonable demands without questioning.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 雖然通常是好的，但他過於**順從**的傾向有時會使他毫無疑問地同意不合理的要求。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "compliant" carries a slightly negative sense, suggesting a lack of assertiveness or critical thinking.

---

By understanding these nuances and common collocations, you'll be well-prepared to tackle "compliant" on the GRE!

## hypocritical

Let's break down the word "hypocritical" for your GRE studies.

---

## Understanding "Hypocritical"

**Meaning:**

The adjective **hypocritical** describes someone or something that acts or speaks in a way that suggests they have moral standards or beliefs that they do not actually possess or adhere to. It's about a discrepancy between what one preaches or claims, and what one actually does.

Essentially, it means being **insincere**, **deceptive**, or showing **double standards**.

*   **Core Idea:** Saying one thing but doing another, often with the implication of moral pretense or feigned virtue.
*   **Origin:** The word comes from the Greek "hypokrites," meaning an actor or a dissembler – someone who wears a mask or pretends to be someone they are not.

**Related Forms:**

*   **Noun:** **Hypocrite** (the person exhibiting hypocrisy)
*   **Noun:** **Hypocrisy** (the practice or state of being hypocritical)
*   **Adverb:** **Hypocritically** (in a hypocritical manner)

---

## Common Collocations

Collocations are words that often go together naturally. Knowing these can help you use "hypocritical" more fluently and score better on the GRE's "Sentence Equivalence" and "Text Completion" questions.

*   **Hypocritical behavior/actions:** What someone does that contradicts their stated beliefs.
*   **Hypocritical statement/remark/claim:** Something someone says that is insincere or goes against their true actions.
*   **Hypocritical attitude/stance:** A feigned moral position.
*   **Seem/sound/appear hypocritical:** How something comes across to others.
*   **Be utterly/deeply/highly/blatantly hypocritical:** Used to emphasize the degree of hypocrisy.
*   **It's hypocritical of someone to do something:** A common grammatical structure.

---

## Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its use, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Personal Morality/Behavior**
    *   **English:** His criticism of the senator's lavish spending seemed **hypocritical**, considering his own penchant for luxury cars.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他對這位參議員揮霍無度的批評顯得有些**偽善**，考慮到他自己也酷愛豪華汽車。

2.  **Context: Political/Social Commentary**
    *   **English:** The government's claim to support free speech seemed **hypocritical** given its recent crackdown on peaceful protests.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 政府聲稱支持言論自由，但最近卻鎮壓和平抗議，這顯得**虛偽**。

3.  **Context: Business/Corporate Ethics**
    *   **English:** Many consumers found the company's "green" advertising campaign **hypocritical**, as it continued to pollute local waterways.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多消費者認為這家公司的「綠色」廣告活動是**口是心非**的，因為它持續污染當地水道。

4.  **Context: Everyday Situations/Relationships**
    *   **English:** She found her friend's advice to "live within your means" rather **hypocritical**, as he was constantly racking up credit card debt.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她覺得朋友「量入為出」的建議相當**偽善**，因為他自己卻不斷累積信用卡債務。

5.  **Context: Emphasizing the Degree of Hypocrisy**
    *   **English:** It would be utterly **hypocritical** for us to demand austerity from others while enjoying excessive perks ourselves.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 如果我們在享受過度特權的同時卻要求別人緊縮開支，那將是**極度偽善**的。

6.  **Context: Religious/Moral Authority**
    *   **English:** The community was disillusioned by the minister's **hypocritical** behavior when his secret financial dealings were exposed.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當這位牧師的秘密財務交易被曝光後，社區對他的**虛偽行為**感到幻想破滅。

---

By understanding "hypocritical" and its common collocations, you'll be well-equipped to identify and use it effectively in your GRE vocabulary and reading comprehension sections!

## pristine

Okay, let's break down the word "pristine" for your GRE preparation.

## Pristine (adj.)

**Meaning:**
1.  **In its original condition; unspoiled.** This is the primary and most common meaning. It implies something is as it was when new or first created, untouched by human interference or the passage of time.
2.  **Clean and fresh as if new; spotless.** This meaning extends to cleanliness, suggesting something is impeccably clean, often to the point of appearing brand new.

**Think of it as:** Pure, perfect, immaculate, unblemished, untouched, original.

**Common Collocations:**

When studying for the GRE, paying attention to collocations (words that often go together) is very helpful. "Pristine" frequently collocates with:

*   **Pristine condition:** (e.g., an antique in pristine condition)
*   **Pristine beaches/waters/wilderness/forests/environment:** (referring to untouched natural beauty)
*   **Pristine white:** (e.g., a pristine white tablecloth)
*   **Pristine state:** (similar to condition)
*   **Pristine record/reputation:** (less common, but implies an unblemished or faultless history/image)
*   **Pristine beauty/appearance:** (referring to flawless aesthetic quality)

---

## Example Sentences in Various Contexts with Traditional Chinese Translations:

Here are multiple examples to illustrate "pristine" in different contexts:

1.  **Context: Nature/Environment**
    *   **English:** The island boasts **pristine beaches** with crystal-clear waters, attracting tourists seeking untouched natural beauty.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這個島嶼擁有清澈見底的**原始潔淨沙灘**，吸引著尋求未受破壞自然美的遊客。

2.  **Context: Objects/Condition (Antiques, Collectibles)**
    *   **English:** Despite its age, the vintage comic book was found in **pristine condition**, making it highly valuable to collectors.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管年代久遠，這本復古漫畫書被發現時仍保持著**完好如初的狀態**，對收藏家來說價值極高。

3.  **Context: Cleanliness/Orderliness**
    *   **English:** Her house is always kept in **pristine order**, with not a single item out of place.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她的房子總是保持著**一塵不染的整潔**，沒有任何物品錯位。

4.  **Context: Figurative/Abstract (Historical Record/Reputation)**
    *   **English:** The newly discovered ancient manuscript offered a **pristine glimpse** into the daily life of the early civilization.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 新發現的古代手稿提供了一個**原始、未受影響的視角**，讓我們得以一窺早期文明的日常生活。

5.  **Context: Science/Research**
    *   **English:** Scientists hope to study the **pristine ice cores** from Antarctica to learn about ancient climates before human impact.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 科學家希望能研究來自南極洲**原始未受污染的冰芯**，以了解人類影響之前的古代氣候。

6.  **Context: Appearance/Aesthetics**
    *   **English:** The bride looked stunning in her **pristine white** gown, radiating elegance and purity.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 新娘穿著她那件**潔白無瑕的**禮服看起來非常迷人，散發著優雅和純潔。

---

By understanding the core meaning, common collocations, and seeing "pristine" used in varied contexts, you'll be well-prepared to tackle it on the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## anomaly

Okay, let's break down the word "anomaly" for your GRE preparation.

---

### Understanding "Anomaly"

**Meaning:**

An **anomaly** (pronounced uh-NOM-uh-lee) refers to something that deviates from what is standard, normal, or expected. It's an irregularity, an inconsistency, or a peculiarity. When something is an anomaly, it stands out because it doesn't conform to the usual pattern or rule.

*   **Core Idea:** Deviation from the norm.
*   **Origin:** From Greek *anomalos* meaning "uneven, irregular."

**In simple terms:** It's an odd one out, a strange occurrence, or something that doesn't fit.

---

### Common Collocations for "Anomaly"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these will help you understand and use "anomaly" naturally in various contexts.

**Verbs often used with "anomaly":**

*   **Detect/Identify/Spot an anomaly:** To find or notice something unusual.
*   **Explain/Account for an anomaly:** To provide a reason or clarification for why something is unusual.
*   **Present an anomaly:** When something *is* an unusual case or situation.
*   **Address/Resolve an anomaly:** To deal with or fix an unusual problem.
*   **Treat an anomaly:** (Medical context) To manage or cure an abnormal condition.

**Adjectives often used with "anomaly":**

*   **Statistical anomaly:** A data point that falls outside the expected range.
*   **Climatic/Weather anomaly:** Unusual weather patterns.
*   **Genetic/Medical anomaly:** An unusual or abnormal biological condition.
*   **Behavioral anomaly:** Unusual or unexpected behavior.
*   **Historical anomaly:** An event or situation that is unique or doesn't fit the historical pattern.
*   **Significant/Notable anomaly:** A particularly important or obvious deviation.
*   **Rare/Unusual anomaly:** An infrequent deviation.
*   **Curious/Puzzling anomaly:** An anomaly that causes wonder or confusion.
*   **Blatant/Clear anomaly:** A very obvious deviation.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts (with Traditional Chinese Translations)

Here are multiple examples to solidify your understanding:

1.  **Context: Science/Data Analysis**
    *   **English:** The unexpected dip in temperature was a **climatic anomaly** that puzzled meteorologists, as it didn't fit the typical weather patterns for that region.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 氣溫意外的驟降是一個令氣象學家困惑的**氣候異常**，因為它不符合該地區的典型天氣模式。
    *   *(Note: 氣候異常 (qìhòu yìcháng) is a common translation for climatic anomaly.)*

2.  **Context: Behavior/Performance**
    *   **English:** For a student who consistently excelled, a failing grade on a major exam was a complete **anomaly**, prompting the teacher to check if something was wrong.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 對於一個成績一向優異的學生來說，在一次主要考試中不及格是一個完全的**反常現象**，促使老師去檢查是否出了什麼問題。
    *   *(Note: 反常現象 (fǎncháng xiànxiàng) is also good for an unusual phenomenon or occurrence.)*

3.  **Context: Economics/Market Trends**
    *   **English:** The sudden surge in stock prices for a struggling company was considered an **economic anomaly** that experts struggled to explain.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 一家陷入困境的公司股價突然飆升，被認為是一個經濟**異常現象**，專家們難以解釋。
    *   *(Note: 經濟異常 (jīngjì yìcháng) works well for economic anomaly.)*

4.  **Context: History/Culture**
    *   **English:** While most ancient civilizations were polytheistic, the early Jewish people's strict monotheism was a significant **historical anomaly**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 雖然大多數古代文明都是多神教的，但早期猶太人嚴格的一神教是一個顯著的**歷史特例**。
    *   *(Note: 歷史特例 (lìshǐ tèlì) can be used for a historical exception or unusual case. 歷史異常 (lìshǐ yìcháng) is also possible.)*

5.  **Context: Personal Traits/Routine**
    *   **English:** His calm demeanor during the crisis was an **anomaly**, as he was usually quite prone to panic under pressure.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他在危機中的冷靜舉止是一種**異常**，因為他在壓力下通常很容易恐慌。
    *   *(Note: 異常 (yìcháng) can stand alone here as "an anomaly" referring to the unusual quality.)*

6.  **Context: Medical/Biological**
    *   **English:** Doctors performed further tests to determine the cause of the **genetic anomaly** found in the patient's DNA.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 醫生進行了進一步的檢測，以確定在病人DNA中發現的**基因變異**的原因。
    *   *(Note: 基因變異 (jīyīn biànyì) is the specific term for genetic anomaly/variation.)*

---

By understanding the core meaning, common collocations, and seeing "anomaly" used in diverse contexts, you'll be well-prepared to tackle it on the GRE!

## precipitate

Okay, let's break down the word "precipitate" for your GRE studies. It's a versatile word with several meanings, and understanding its nuances, especially the metaphorical ones, is key for the test.

---

### Meaning of "Precipitate"

"Precipitate" can function as a **verb** or an **adjective**, and less commonly as a noun in specific contexts.

#### I. As a Verb (most common for GRE Verbal Reasoning)

1.  **To cause (an event or situation, typically an undesirable one) to happen suddenly, unexpectedly, or prematurely.**
    *   This is the most common metaphorical meaning on the GRE. It implies bringing something about swiftly, often with negative consequences, or before it's naturally due.
    *   **Synonyms:** trigger, instigate, provoke, spark, hasten, accelerate, bring about.

2.  **To cause a substance to be deposited in solid form from a solution.** (Chemistry context)
    *   *Example:* "The reaction caused a white **precipitate** to form at the bottom of the beaker."

3.  **To fall as rain, snow, etc.** (Meteorology context - often used as "precipitation," the noun)
    *   *Example:* "Heavy rains are expected to **precipitate** across the region tonight."

#### II. As an Adjective

1.  **Done or happening suddenly or without careful consideration; hasty, rash, impetuous.**
    *   This describes an action or decision that is abrupt and ill-considered.
    *   **Synonyms:** hasty, rash, impetuous, abrupt, sudden, quick.

---

### Common Collocations

**As a Verb:**
*   **precipitate a crisis:** 導致危機爆發
*   **precipitate a conflict/war:** 引發衝突／戰爭
*   **precipitate a decline:** 加速衰退
*   **precipitate an argument/outcry:** 激起爭論／抗議
*   **precipitate change:** 促成改變
*   **precipitate an economic downturn/recession:** 導致經濟衰退
*   **precipitate a chain reaction:** 引發連鎖反應

**As an Adjective:**
*   **precipitate action:** 倉促的行動
*   **precipitate decision:** 草率的決定
*   **precipitate conclusion:** 武斷的結論
*   **precipitate departure:** 倉促的離開

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences illustrating different meanings of "precipitate," along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Meaning: Verb - Cause something (often negative) to happen suddenly.**
    *   **English:** The sudden collapse of the housing market **precipitated** a severe economic recession.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 房市的突然崩盤**導致**了一場嚴重的經濟衰退。

2.  **Meaning: Verb - Cause something (often negative) to happen suddenly.**
    *   **English:** His rash accusations **precipitated** a bitter argument between the two colleagues.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他那魯莽的指控**引發**了兩位同事之間一場激烈的爭吵。

3.  **Meaning: Adjective - Hasty, rash, ill-considered.**
    *   **English:** Making such a **precipitate** decision without sufficient data proved to be a costly mistake for the company.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在沒有足夠數據的情況下做出如此**倉促的**決定，對公司來說是個代價高昂的錯誤。

4.  **Meaning: Verb - Chemistry (to form a solid).**
    *   **English:** When the two solutions were mixed, a cloudy white substance began to **precipitate** at the bottom of the flask.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當兩種溶液混合時，一種混濁的白色物質開始在燒瓶底部**沉澱**下來。

5.  **Meaning: Adjective - Hasty, sudden (describing an action/event).**
    *   **English:** The CEO's **precipitate** departure from the company raised many questions among investors.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 執行長**倉促地**離開公司，在投資者中引發了許多疑問。

6.  **Meaning: Verb - Cause a sequence of events.**
    *   **English:** A single misstep can **precipitate** a chain reaction of failures in complex systems.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 單一的失誤就可能在複雜的系統中**引發**一連串的故障。

---

By understanding these various uses and practicing with the examples, you'll be well-prepared for "precipitate" on the GRE!

## bellicose

That's an excellent word to know for the GRE! Let's break down "bellicose."

---

## **Bellicose**

**Meaning:**
*   **Definition:** (Adjective) Demonstrating aggression and willingness to fight; inclined or eager to fight; aggressively hostile.
*   **Etymology:** The word comes from the Latin word "bellum," meaning "war." So, "bellicose" literally means "full of war" or "warlike."
*   **Nuance:** It often describes a disposition or a ready inclination towards conflict, whether in speech, policy, or action, rather than just being angry. It suggests a desire to instigate or engage in a fight.

**Common Collocations:**
"Bellicose" frequently collocates with words related to:
*   **Speech/Communication:** **bellicose rhetoric**, **bellicose statements**, **bellicose tone**, **bellicose threats**
*   **People/Groups:** **bellicose leader**, **bellicose nation**, **bellicose regime**, **bellicose faction**, **bellicose opponent**
*   **Actions/Policies:** **bellicose foreign policy**, **bellicose stance**, **bellicose attitude**

---

**Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

1.  **Context: International Relations/Politics**
    *   **English:** The nation's new leader adopted a remarkably **bellicose tone** in his first address, threatening neighboring countries.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該國新任領導人在其首次演講中採取了驚人的**好戰姿態**，威脅鄰國。

2.  **Context: Personal Behavior/Disposition**
    *   **English:** After a few drinks, he became increasingly **bellicose**, challenging anyone who disagreed with him.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 幾杯酒下肚後，他變得越來越**好鬥**，不斷挑戰任何與他意見相左的人。

3.  **Context: Historical/Strategic**
    *   **English:** Throughout history, many empires have risen to power through **bellicose expansionism** rather than peaceful diplomacy.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 縱觀歷史，許多帝國都是透過**好戰的擴張主義**而非和平外交手段而崛起。

4.  **Context: Negotiation/Diplomacy**
    *   **English:** Despite the attempts at peaceful negotiation, the **bellicose demands** from one party ultimately led to the breakdown of talks.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管各方努力進行和平談判，但其中一方**好戰的要求**最終導致了談判破裂。

5.  **Context: Media/Public Discourse**
    *   **English:** The senator's **bellicose rhetoric** alienated many potential allies who favored a more diplomatic approach to the crisis.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該參議員**挑釁性**（或**好戰**）**的言論**疏遠了許多傾向於採取更外交手段來解決危機的潛在盟友。

---

Keep practicing with these types of words, and you'll be well-prepared for the GRE!

## acidulous

Let's break down "acidulous" for your GRE studies.

## Acidulous (adj.)

**Meaning:**

1.  **Literal:** Slightly sour or acidic in taste; tart.
    *   Think of a less intense version of "acidic." It's a mild, often pleasant sourness.
2.  **Figurative:** Slightly sarcastic, critical, or sharp in manner or tone; having a tart or caustic wit.
    *   When applied to people or their speech, it implies a subtle, sometimes understated, sharpness or criticism. It's not overtly harsh or aggressive, but rather incisive and perhaps a bit biting.

**Etymology:** The word comes from "acid" + the suffix "-ulous," which denotes a tendency or quality (like "credulous" or "scrupulous").

---

**Common Collocations:**

*   **Acidulous taste/flavor:** (Literal) Describing food or drink.
*   **Acidulous fruit/berries/wine:** (Literal) Describing specific items.
*   **Acidulous remark/comment:** (Figurative) A slightly critical or sarcastic statement.
*   **Acidulous wit/humor:** (Figurative) A sharp, often understated, and subtly biting sense of humor.
*   **Acidulous tone/voice:** (Figurative) A slightly sharp or critical way of speaking.
*   **Acidulous critique/review:** (Figurative) A mild yet pointed assessment.
*   **Acidulous expression/smile:** (Figurative) A look that conveys subtle disapproval or sarcasm.

---

**Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

1.  **Context: Describing Food/Taste (Literal)**
    *   **English:** The wild berries, though small, had an **acidulous** tang that made them refreshing on a hot day.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那些野莓雖然小，卻帶有一種微酸的滋味，在大熱天吃起來很清爽。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "acidulous" refers to a pleasant, slight sourness, not an overwhelming one.

2.  **Context: Describing a Person's Wit/Humor (Figurative)**
    *   **English:** She was known for her **acidulous** wit, often delivering sharp observations with a deceptively gentle smile.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她以其尖刻的機智而聞名，常常帶著欺騙性的溫和笑容，說出犀利的觀察。
    *   *Explanation:* This describes a type of humor that is intelligent, keen, and has a subtle edge, but isn't necessarily rude or offensive.

3.  **Context: Describing a Remark/Comment (Figurative)**
    *   **English:** His **acidulous** comment about the play's predictable plot left the director feeling rather deflated.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他對這齣戲預料之中的情節所做的帶有諷刺意味的評論，讓導演感到相當沮喪。
    *   *Explanation:* The comment was critical and biting, but perhaps delivered in a way that wasn't overtly aggressive, making it even more impactful.

4.  **Context: Describing a Written Critique/Review (Figurative)**
    *   **English:** The literary critic's **acidulous** review pointed out every minor flaw in the novel, though it acknowledged its overall ambition.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 文學評論家那篇略帶批判性的評論，指出了小說中的每一個小缺陷，儘管它也承認了其整體抱負。
    *   *Explanation:* The review was critical and detailed in its identification of faults, implying a keen, almost dissecting, scrutiny.

5.  **Context: Describing a Tone or Expression (Figurative)**
    *   **English:** Despite her outwardly polite demeanor, there was an **acidulous** tone in her voice when she questioned his credentials, betraying her skepticism.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管她表面上舉止禮貌，但在質疑他的資歷時，她的語氣卻略帶譏諷，暴露出她的懷疑。
    *   *Explanation:* This highlights a subtle, underlying critical or skeptical attitude expressed through vocal nuance rather than explicit words.

By understanding both the literal and figurative senses, and seeing it in various contexts, you'll be well-prepared for "acidulous" on the GRE!

## gossamer

That's a great word for GRE prep! Let's break down "gossamer."

## Gossamer

**Meaning:**

"Gossamer" primarily functions as an **adjective**, though it can also be a **noun**.

1.  **As an Adjective:**
    *   **Literal:** Refers to something that is **extremely light, thin, and delicate**, often translucent or almost invisible. Its origin comes from the fine, shimmery threads spun by spiders, especially those seen floating in the air or glistening on plants in autumn.
    *   **Figurative:** Extended to anything that shares these qualities: sheer, flimsy, insubstantial, tenuous, ethereal, or very fine. It often implies fragility or a lack of substance/solidity.

2.  **As a Noun:**
    *   The literal fine, often shimmering threads of a spider's web, especially when floating in the air or caught on vegetation.
    *   Figuratively, something incredibly light, thin, or delicate (e.g., a "gossamer of mist").

**Key Idea:** Think "spider web" or "fairy wings" – something incredibly delicate, sheer, and seemingly weightless.

---

### Common Collocations:

"Gossamer" frequently collocates with words that emphasize its qualities of lightness, delicacy, or transparency.

*   **Fabric/Material:** gossamer **veil**, gossamer **dress**, gossamer **fabric**, gossamer **silk**, gossamer **threads**, gossamer **wings**
*   **Nature/Atmosphere:** gossamer **mist**, gossamer **light**, gossamer **dew**, gossamer **film**
*   **Abstract/Figurative:** gossamer **dream**, gossamer **hope**, gossamer **illusion**, gossamer **connection**, gossamer **argument** (implying fragility or lack of substance), gossamer **touch**, gossamer **sound**

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts & Traditional Chinese Translations:

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its usage:

1.  **Literal (Spider Web):**
    *   **English:** The morning dew clung to the **gossamer threads** of the spider web, turning them into a shimmering network of jewels.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 清晨的露珠附著在蜘蛛網上**輕盈纖細的蛛絲**上，將它們變成了一張閃閃發光的珠寶網。

2.  **Clothing/Fabric:**
    *   **English:** Her wedding dress was made of a **gossamer fabric** that seemed to float as she walked down the aisle.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她的婚紗是用**輕薄如蟬翼的面料**製成的，當她走在紅毯上時，彷彿飄浮起來。

3.  **Nature (Mist/Light):**
    *   **English:** A **gossamer mist** hung over the valley at dawn, slowly dissipating as the sun rose.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 清晨，一層**輕薄的霧氣**籠罩著山谷，隨著太陽升起，慢慢消散。

4.  **Figurative (Fragile Idea/Argument):**
    *   **English:** The politician's argument, though elegantly phrased, was based on a **gossamer premise** that could easily be disproved by facts.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管言辭優雅，這位政治家的論點卻建立在一個**脆弱不堪的前提**上，很容易被事實駁倒。

5.  **Figurative (Tenuous Connection/Relationship):**
    *   **English:** Their friendship, initially strong, began to fray over time, becoming a **gossamer thread** barely holding them together.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他們的友誼起初很堅固，但隨著時間流逝開始磨損，變成了一條**若有若無、岌岌可危的線**，勉強維繫著彼此。

6.  **Sensory (Touch/Sound):**
    *   **English:** She felt a **gossamer touch** on her arm, like a butterfly's wing, and turned to see no one there.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她感到手臂上**輕如羽毛的觸感**，像蝴蝶的翅膀，轉過身卻沒看到任何人。
    *   **English:** The delicate, **gossamer sound** of the flute drifted through the silent hall.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 長笛**輕柔飄渺的聲音**飄蕩在寂靜的大廳裡。

7.  **Abstract (Dreams/Illusions):**
    *   **English:** His hopes for the future were often no more than **gossamer dreams**, easily shattered by reality.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他對未來的希望往往不過是**虛無縹緲的夢想**，很容易被現實擊碎。

By understanding "gossamer" through its literal origin (spider silk) and then applying its core qualities (light, thin, delicate, insubstantial) to various contexts, you'll be well-prepared for its appearance on the GRE!

## flagrant

Okay, let's break down the word "flagrant" for your GRE prep!

---

## **Flagrant**

**Meaning:**
"Flagrant" describes something (usually an offense, mistake, or bad action) that is **conspicuously or obviously offensive, wrong, or immoral**. It means something is so glaring, blatant, and unashamedly bad that it's impossible to ignore and often causes shock or scandal. It implies a complete and open disregard for rules, morality, or decency.

*   **Key aspects:**
    *   **Obvious/Blatant:** Not hidden; it's right out in the open.
    *   **Shockingly Bad:** Refers to something severely wrong or offensive.
    *   **Unashamed:** Done without any attempt to conceal or excuse.

**Synonyms often seen on the GRE:**
*   Blatant
*   Egregious
*   Glaring
*   Scandalous
*   Outrageous
*   Brazen
*   Unconscionable

---

**Common Collocations:**

"Flagrant" most commonly modifies nouns that refer to violations, abuses, or obvious errors.
*   **Flagrant violation:** (e.g., of rules, laws, human rights)
*   **Flagrant disregard:** (e.g., for safety, for the rules, for decency)
*   **Flagrant abuse:** (e.g., of power, of trust)
*   **Flagrant injustice:** (a very clear and obvious unfairness)
*   **Flagrant error/mistake:** (a very obvious and often serious error)
*   **Flagrant lie:** (an obvious and unashamed falsehood)
*   **Flagrant disrespect:** (very obvious and offensive lack of respect)

---

**Example Sentences in Various Contexts (with Traditional Chinese Translations):**

1.  **Context: Legal/Ethical Violation**
    *   **Sentence:** The company was fined heavily for a **flagrant violation** of environmental protection laws.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該公司因公然違反環境保護法規而被重罰。
        *   *(公然 gōngrán: openly, overtly, blatantly)*

2.  **Context: Disrespect/Disregard**
    *   **Sentence:** His **flagrant disregard** for the teacher's instructions led to him being suspended from class.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他公然無視老師的指示，導致他被停課。
        *   *(公然無視 gōngrán wúshì: blatant disregard)*

3.  **Context: Injustice**
    *   **Sentence:** Many civil rights activists viewed the court's decision as a **flagrant injustice** against the minority group.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多民權活動家認為法院的這項裁決是對少數族裔的公然不公。
        *   *(公然不公 gōngrán bùgōng: blatant injustice)*

4.  **Context: Behavior/Dishonesty**
    *   **Sentence:** The politician's **flagrant lies** during the election campaign shocked many voters.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位政客在競選期間公然撒謊，震驚了許多選民。
        *   *(公然撒謊 gōngrán sāhuǎng: blatant lying)*

5.  **Context: Abuse of Power**
    *   **Sentence:** Critics condemned the dictator's **flagrant abuse** of human rights, including arbitrary arrests and torture.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 批評者譴責這位獨裁者公然侵犯人權，包括任意逮捕和酷刑。
        *   *(公然侵犯 gōngrán qīnfàn: blatant violation/abuse)*

6.  **Context: Obvious Error/Mistake**
    *   **Sentence:** Despite the detailed instructions, the student made a **flagrant error** in the final calculation.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管有詳細的指示，這位學生在最終計算中還是犯了一個公然的錯誤。
        *   *(公然的錯誤 gōngrán de cuòwù: blatant error)*

---

Remember, "flagrant" carries a strong negative connotation and is used to emphasize the brazen and unmistakable nature of something bad. Good luck with your GRE studies!

## kinetic

Let's break down the word "kinetic" for your GRE preparation.

---

### Understanding "Kinetic"

**Meaning:**

"Kinetic" (adjective) primarily means **relating to or resulting from motion**. It describes something that is active, dynamic, or in the process of moving.

*   **Origin:** The word comes from the Greek word *kinētikos*, meaning "of motion," derived from *kinein*, "to move."

**Key Nuances:**

1.  **Physics/Science Context:** This is its most direct and common usage. It refers to the energy an object possesses due to its motion (kinetic energy), or phenomena related to movement at a molecular level (e.g., kinetic theory of gases).
2.  **Art/Design Context:** It describes art forms that incorporate actual movement or rely on the illusion of movement to create their effect (e.g., kinetic sculpture).
3.  **Figurative/Abstract Context:** It can be used to describe something that is highly active, dynamic, vibrant, or full of energy, even if not literally in physical motion. For example, a "kinetic personality" is someone very lively and energetic.

---

### Common Collocations of "Kinetic"

Here are some common and important collocations you'll encounter, especially in academic or scientific contexts:

*   **Kinetic energy:** (The most common one) The energy an object possesses due to its motion.
*   **Kinetic art/sculpture:** Art that incorporates movement as part of its design.
*   **Kinetic theory (of gases):** A model that explains the macroscopic properties of gases based on the constant, random motion of their constituent molecules.
*   **Kinetic chain:** In biomechanics, a series of body segments connected by joints, moving sequentially to perform a task.
*   **Kinetic reaction:** In chemistry, referring to the rate or mechanism of a chemical reaction.
*   **Kinetic friction:** The force that opposes the motion of two surfaces sliding against each other.
*   **Kinetic display/performance:** A dynamic and energetic show or presentation.
*   **Kinetic personality/style:** (Figurative) Describing someone or something that is lively, active, and full of energy.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences demonstrating the use of "kinetic" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Physics (Kinetic Energy):**
    *   **English:** The car's **kinetic energy** increases exponentially with its speed, making it much harder to stop at high velocities.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 汽車的**動能**會隨著速度的增加而呈指數級增長，這使得它在高速行駛時更難以停下。

2.  **Art/Design (Kinetic Art):**
    *   **English:** Alexander Calder's mobiles are classic examples of **kinetic art**, mesmerizing viewers with their subtle and unpredictable movements.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 亞歷山大·考爾德的活動雕塑是**動態藝術**的經典範例，它們細微且不可預測的移動令觀者著迷。

3.  **Science (Kinetic Theory):**
    *   **English:** The **kinetic theory of gases** posits that gas particles are in constant, random motion, colliding with each other and the walls of their container.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** **氣體分子運動論**提出，氣體粒子處於持續、隨機的運動狀態，並相互碰撞以及與容器壁碰撞。

4.  **Figurative (Personality/Style):**
    *   **English:** Her **kinetic** stage presence captivated the audience, filling the theater with an electrifying energy that few performers could match.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她**充滿動感**的舞台表現力吸引了所有觀眾，讓劇院充滿了鮮少有表演者能匹敵的振奮能量。

5.  **Abstract/Process (Dynamic Approach):**
    *   **English:** The new CEO brought a **kinetic** approach to the stagnant company, initiating rapid changes and fostering innovation across all departments.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 新任執行長為這家停滯不前的公司帶來了**充滿活力的策略**，啟動了快速變革並促進了所有部門的創新。

6.  **Specific Scientific (Kinetic Friction):**
    *   **English:** Understanding the principles of **kinetic friction** is crucial for designing braking systems and ensuring vehicle safety.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 理解**動摩擦**的原理對於設計煞車系統和確保車輛安全至關重要。

---

By understanding "kinetic" in these various contexts and practicing with its common collocations, you'll be well-prepared for its appearance on the GRE, whether in reading comprehension, text completion, or sentence equivalence questions. Good luck!

## unequivocal

Let's break down the word "unequivocal" for your GRE studies.

---

### Understanding "Unequivocal"

**Meaning:**
"Unequivocal" (pronounced /ʌnɪˈkwɪvəkəl/) means **leaving no doubt or misunderstanding; unambiguous; clear and precise.** It implies that something is expressed or understood in such a way that there is only one possible interpretation. It's a very strong form of clarity.

*   **Etymology Tip for GRE:** The word comes from "un-" (not) + "equivocal."
    *   "Equivocal" means open to more than one interpretation; ambiguous; uncertain.
    *   "Vocal" relates to voice, and "equi-" means equal. So, "equivocal" literally implies "equal voices" or "equal interpretations," leading to ambiguity.
    *   Therefore, "unequivocal" means the opposite: "not ambiguous," or "only one voice/interpretation."

**Synonyms:**
*   Unambiguous
*   Categorical
*   Definitive
*   Explicit
*   Absolute
*   Unmistakable
*   Indisputable
*   Resolute

**Antonyms:**
*   Equivocal
*   Ambiguous
*   Vague
*   Uncertain
*   Dubious

---

### Common Collocation

The most common collocations for "unequivocal" involve things that can be stated, shown, or expressed with absolute clarity and no room for doubt.

*   **Unequivocal support/commitment:** Expressing absolute, unwavering backing.
*   **Unequivocal statement/declaration/answer/response:** A clear, direct, and unambiguous communication.
*   **Unequivocal evidence/proof:** Data or facts that leave no room for alternative explanations.
*   **Unequivocal victory/success:** A win or achievement that is beyond dispute.

---

### Example Sentences and Traditional Chinese Translations

Here are multiple examples in various contexts:

1.  **Context: Politics/Diplomacy**
    *   **English:** The ambassador issued an **unequivocal declaration** of his country's commitment to peace in the region.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 大使發布了一項關於該國對地區和平承諾的**毫不含糊的聲明**。
    *   *Explanation:* This means the declaration was so clear and firm that no one could question their commitment.

2.  **Context: Science/Research**
    *   **English:** The scientific study provided **unequivocal evidence** that the new medication is effective with minimal side effects.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這項科學研究提供了新藥物有效且副作用極小的**確鑿證據**。
    *   *Explanation:* The evidence was so strong and conclusive that it left no doubt about the drug's efficacy.

3.  **Context: Business/Legal**
    *   **English:** The contract must use **unequivocal language** to ensure there is no room for misinterpretation of the terms and conditions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 合同必須使用**清晰無誤的措辭**，以確保條款和條件沒有任何被誤解的餘地。
    *   *Explanation:* The language used must be so precise that it cannot be understood in any way other than what was intended.

4.  **Context: Personal/Interpersonal Communication**
    *   **English:** When asked if she approved, her **unequivocal answer** was a firm and immediate "Yes!"
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當被問及是否同意時，她**斬釘截鐵的回答**是一個堅定而即刻的「是的！」
    *   *Explanation:* Her response was direct and left absolutely no doubt about her approval.

5.  **Context: Public Opinion/Reaction**
    *   **English:** The public's **unequivocal rejection** of the proposed tax increase forced the government to reconsider its policy.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 公眾對擬議增稅的**毫不含糊的拒絕**迫使政府重新考慮其政策。
    *   *Explanation:* The public's opposition was so strong and unified that it was impossible to ignore or misinterpret.

By understanding "unequivocal" and seeing it in these various contexts, you'll be well-prepared to encounter it on the GRE!

## neologism

Let's break down the word "neologism" for your GRE studies.

---

## Neologism (noun)

**Meaning:**

A **neologism** (pronounced /niːˈɒlədʒɪzəm/) is a **newly coined word or expression** that has not yet gained widespread usage or acceptance into the common language. It comes from the Greek words "neo-" (new) and "logos" (word, speech).

Neologisms are often created to describe new concepts, technologies, phenomena, or social trends for which no existing word accurately or sufficiently applies. While some neologisms become widely adopted and eventually enter standard dictionaries (like "google" as a verb, "blog," or "unfriend"), many are short-lived, remaining as jargon, slang within specific communities, or simply fading away.

**Common Collocations:**

Here are some words that frequently appear with "neologism":

*   **Verb + Neologism:**
    *   **coin a neologism:** (the most common and natural way to describe its creation)
    *   **introduce a neologism**
    *   **create a neologism**
    *   **popularize a neologism**
    *   **adopt a neologism**
    *   **encounter a neologism**

*   **Adjective + Neologism:**
    *   **a recent neologism**
    *   **a linguistic neologism**
    *   **a successful/failed neologism**
    *   **a trendy neologism**
    *   **an obscure neologism**

*   **Noun + of + Neologism:**
    *   **the coining of a neologism**
    *   **the spread of a neologism**
    *   **the adoption of a neologism**

---

**Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

1.  **Context: Technology & Digital Age**
    *   **English:** The internet age has spawned many **neologisms**, such as "blogging" and "unfriend," which quickly entered everyday vocabulary.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 互聯網時代催生了許多**新詞**，例如「部落格」（blogging）和「取消好友」（unfriend），這些詞彙很快就進入了日常詞彙。

2.  **Context: Academia & Specialized Fields**
    *   **English:** In her groundbreaking paper, the astrophysicist proposed a new **neologism**, 'chronosynclastic infundibulum,' to describe a theoretical spacetime phenomenon.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在她開創性的論文中，這位天體物理學家提出了一個**新創詞**——「時空漏斗」（chronosynclastic infundibulum），用來描述一種理論上的時空現象。

3.  **Context: Literature & Historical Impact**
    *   **English:** Shakespeare was famous for his prolific creation of **neologisms**, many of which, like 'eyeball' and 'swagger,' are now standard English.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 莎士比亞以大量創造**新詞**而聞名，其中許多詞彙，例如「眼球」（eyeball）和「大搖大擺」（swagger），現在已成為標準英語。

4.  **Context: Social Trends & Pop Culture**
    *   **English:** "Gaslighting" began as a psychological term but has become a popular **neologism** in social discourse, referring to manipulative behavior.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 「煤氣燈操縱」（Gaslighting）最初是一個心理學術語，但現在已成為社會討論中一個流行的**新詞**，指代操縱行為。

5.  **Context: General Linguistic Observation**
    *   **English:** Not every **neologism** manages to gain widespread acceptance; many fade away as quickly as they appear.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 並非每一個**新詞**都能獲得廣泛接受；許多新詞的消逝速度與其出現速度一樣快。

---

Understanding "neologism" is excellent for the GRE, as it not only tests your vocabulary but also your ability to understand how language evolves, a common theme in reading comprehension passages. Good luck with your studies!

## penchant

Okay, let's break down "penchant" for your GRE prep!

---

### Understanding "Penchant"

**Meaning:**
A **penchant** (pronounced PEN-chuhnt) is a **strong liking, tendency, or inclination for something.** It implies a natural or habitual preference, often for something specific. It's more than just a passing liking; it suggests a deep-seated affinity or a characteristic habit.

*   **Part of Speech:** Noun

**Synonyms (for GRE vocabulary):**
*   Fondness
*   Predilection
*   Proclivity
*   Inclination
*   Partiality
*   Leaning

**Antonyms:**
*   Aversion
*   Dislike
*   Disinclination
*   Repugnance

**Etymology Note:** The word "penchant" comes from the Old French word "pencher," meaning "to lean" or "to incline." This helps to remember its meaning as "leaning towards" something.

---

### Common Collocations

The most important and by far the most common collocation is:

*   **`penchant for`**

You almost always use "penchant *for* [something]" to express what someone has a strong liking or tendency towards.

**Other common collocations (adjectives that describe "penchant" or verbs used with it):**

*   **Adjectives:**
    *   `strong penchant`
    *   `marked penchant`
    *   `definite penchant`
    *   `unmistakable penchant`
    *   `curious penchant` (suggests an unusual or unexpected liking)
    *   `lifelong penchant`
    *   `growing penchant`
    *   `secret penchant`

*   **Verbs:**
    *   `have a penchant`
    *   `develop a penchant`
    *   `show a penchant`
    *   `display a penchant`

---

### Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are several examples to illustrate "penchant" in different situations, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Hobbies/Interests (Positive)**
    *   **English:** Ever since she was a child, Maria has had a strong **penchant for** collecting antique porcelain dolls.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 從小時候起，瑪麗亞就對收集古董瓷娃娃有著強烈的**癖好**。
    *   *(Note: 癖好 (pǐhào) can mean a hobby or a strong, sometimes unusual, liking.)*

2.  **Context: Personality/Behavioral Traits (Slightly Negative/Unusual)**
    *   **English:** Despite his brilliant mind, the professor had a curious **penchant for** arriving late to important meetings, much to the frustration of his colleagues.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管教授才華橫溢，但他卻有著一種奇怪的**癖好**，總是遲到重要的會議，讓他的同事們非常沮喪。
    *   *(Note: Here, 癖好 again works for an unusual, characteristic habit.)*

3.  **Context: Professional/Artistic Preferences**
    *   **English:** The director's latest film clearly demonstrated his unmistakable **penchant for** dark, psychological thrillers.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位導演的最新電影清楚地展現了他對黑暗心理驚悚片的無可置疑的**偏愛**。
    *   *(Note: 偏愛 (piān'ài) means "partiality" or "preference," fitting for artistic choices.)*

4.  **Context: General Lifestyle/Preference**
    *   **English:** Even in the bustling city, Sarah maintained her **penchant for** quiet evenings spent reading at home rather than going out.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 即使身處喧囂的城市，莎拉依然保持著她對在家閱讀的寧靜夜晚的**偏愛**，而非外出。
    *   *(Note: 偏愛 is suitable for a personal preference or a way of life.)*

5.  **Context: Abstract Tendency/Inclination**
    *   **English:** The politician's **penchant for** exaggeration often led to skepticism among voters, even when he was telling the truth.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位政治人物誇大其詞的**傾向**常讓選民產生懷疑，即使他說的是實話。
    *   *(Note: 傾向 (qīngxiàng) means "tendency" or "inclination," which fits well for a characteristic behavioral pattern.)*

6.  **Context: Developing a Liking**
    *   **English:** After years of cooking with spices, Mark developed a **penchant for** experimenting with exotic flavors from around the world.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經過多年與香料打交道後，馬克逐漸對實驗來自世界各地的異國風味產生了**興趣**。
    *   *(Note: Here, "develop a penchant" can be translated as 產生興趣 (chǎnshēng xìngqù - developed an interest/liking), or 產生偏愛 (chǎnshēng piān'ài - developed a preference/partiality).)*

---

By understanding "penchant" as a strong, characteristic liking or tendency, and remembering its crucial collocation "penchant for," you'll be well-equipped for the GRE!

## gregarious

It's great you're focusing on vocabulary for the GRE! Understanding nuances and common usage is key.

---

### Meaning of "Gregarious"

**Gregarious** (adjective) primarily has two main meanings:

1.  **For People:** Fond of company; sociable. Someone who is gregarious enjoys being with others and tends to seek out companionship or group settings. They thrive in social situations and often have many friends or acquaintances.
    *   **Etymology Tip for GRE:** The word comes from the Latin root *grex/gregis*, meaning "flock" or "herd." This helps link it to the animal meaning below.

2.  **For Animals:** Living in flocks or herds or in communities. This describes animals that naturally live in groups rather than being solitary.

---

### Common Collocations for "Gregarious"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these will make your writing and comprehension more natural.

*   **Common Nouns it Modifies:**
    *   **Gregarious person/individual/nature/personality/disposition:** (Referring to someone's social character)
    *   **Gregarious animal/species/bird/mammal/behavior:** (Referring to animals that live in groups)
    *   **Gregarious lifestyle:** (Referring to a way of living that involves much social interaction)

*   **Common Adverbs that Modify it:**
    *   **Highly/Extremely/Incredibly gregarious:** (Emphasizing the degree of sociability or group-living)
    *   **Naturally gregarious:** (Indicating it's an inherent trait)

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts with Traditional Chinese Translations

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its use:

1.  **Context: A person's social nature (positive trait)**
    *   **English:** John, a truly **gregarious person**, was always the life of the party, surrounded by friends and laughter.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 約翰是個真正**喜歡交際**的人，總是派對的靈魂人物，身邊圍繞著朋友和歡聲笑語。

2.  **Context: A professional setting (advantageous trait)**
    *   **English:** Her **gregarious nature** made her an excellent salesperson; she thrived on interacting with clients from diverse backgrounds.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她**合群的個性**使她成為一名優秀的銷售員；她樂於與來自不同背景的客戶互動。

3.  **Context: Contrasting personalities**
    *   **English:** Unlike his quiet, introverted sister, Mark was **incredibly gregarious**, always organizing social gatherings and trips for his friends.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 和他安靜內向的妹妹不同，馬克**非常愛交際**，總是為朋友組織各種社交聚會和旅行。

4.  **Context: Animal behavior (mammals)**
    *   **English:** Elephants are known for their **gregarious behavior**, living in close-knit family herds and showing strong social bonds.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 大象以其**群居行為**而聞名，牠們生活在緊密的家庭群體中，並展現出強烈的社會紐帶。

5.  **Context: Animal behavior (birds)**
    *   **English:** Many bird species are **gregarious**, flocking together for migration, foraging, and protection against predators.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多鳥類都是**群居性**的，為了遷徙、覓食和抵禦捕食者而聚集在一起。

6.  **Context: A change in lifestyle**
    *   **English:** After years of solitary research, the professor decided to adopt a more **gregarious lifestyle**, joining clubs and attending community events.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在多年的獨自研究後，這位教授決定採取一種**更為社交化**的生活方式，開始加入俱樂部並參加社區活動。

---

By understanding "gregarious" in both its human and animal contexts, and practicing with these collocations and example sentences, you'll be well-prepared for any GRE question involving this word! Good luck!

## prevaricate

Let's break down the word "prevaricate" for your GRE studies.

---

## **Prevaricate** (verb)

### **1. Meaning**

To **prevaricate** means to speak or act in an evasive way, to avoid telling the truth directly, or to deliberately mislead through vagueness, ambiguity, or changing the subject. It's not usually a blatant lie, but rather a deliberate attempt to sidestep the truth or avoid giving a direct answer, often to conceal something or to avoid taking a stand.

*   **Nuance:** The core idea is *evasion* and *indirectness* when confronted with a question that requires a direct answer. Someone who prevaricates is often trying to buy time, hide something, or avoid accountability.

*   **Etymology:** From Latin *praevaricari* "to walk with the legs apart," also "to deviate from the right path, to transgress," which perfectly captures the idea of deviating from the straight path of truth.

*   **Synonyms (helpful for GRE):**
    *   **Equivocate:** To use ambiguous language so as to conceal the truth or avoid committing oneself. (Very close in meaning)
    *   **Hedge:** To limit or qualify (something) by conditions or exceptions; to avoid making a direct statement or commitment.
    *   **Fudge:** To present or deal with (something) in a vague, misrepresentative, or prevaricating way.
    *   **Waffle:** To fail to make up one's mind; to speak or write at length without saying anything important or useful.
    *   **Beat around the bush:** To avoid getting to the point.
    *   **Tergiversate:** (More formal/less common) To make conflicting or evasive statements; equivocate.

*   **Antonyms:** Be forthright, be direct, be honest, state clearly.

### **2. Common Collocations**

"Prevaricate" often collocates with adverbs describing the manner or frequency of evasion, or the object of evasion:

*   **Adverbs:**
    *   **constantly prevaricate**
    *   **deliberately prevaricate**
    *   **habitually prevaricate**
    *   **repeatedly prevaricate**
    *   **openly prevaricate**
    *   **skillfully prevaricate**
    *   **tend to prevaricate**

*   **Verbs/Phrases indicating action:**
    *   **caught prevaricating**
    *   **begin to prevaricate**
    *   **refuse to prevaricate**

*   **Nouns (who/what prevaricates):**
    *   **a politician prevaricates**
    *   **a witness prevaricates**
    *   **a suspect prevaricates**
    *   **a spokesperson prevaricates**
    *   **the government prevaricates**

*   **Prepositions (about what):**
    *   **prevaricate about the facts**
    *   **prevaricate on the issue**
    *   **prevaricate over the details**

### **3. Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts**

Here are examples demonstrating "prevaricate" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

---

**Context 1: Politics/Public Statements**

*   **English:** When pressed by reporters, the government spokesperson continued to **prevaricate** about the true cost of the new infrastructure project, offering only vague estimates.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 在記者們的追問下，政府發言人對新基礎設施項目的真實成本繼續**支吾其詞**，只提供模糊的估計。

---

**Context 2: Legal/Witness Testimony**

*   **English:** The judge warned the witness not to **prevaricate** and to answer the prosecutor's questions directly and truthfully.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 法官警告證人不要**閃爍其詞**，要直接並誠實地回答檢察官的問題。

---

**Context 3: Personal/Interpersonal Communication**

*   **English:** Instead of directly addressing his partner's concerns, he began to **prevaricate**, changing the subject and avoiding eye contact.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 他沒有直接回應伴侶的擔憂，反而開始**含糊其辭**，轉移話題並避免眼神接觸。

---

**Context 4: Professional/Business Decisions**

*   **English:** Facing difficult questions from investors about the company's financial health, the CEO chose to **prevaricate**, offering reassurances without concrete data.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 面對投資者關於公司財務狀況的棘手問題，首席執行官選擇了**迴避搪塞**，只提供保證而沒有具體數據。

---

**Context 5: General Tendency/Character**

*   **English:** She has a tendency to **prevaricate** whenever she's asked to make a firm decision, which makes it hard to rely on her commitments.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 每當被要求做出明確決定時，她總有**拐彎抹角**的傾向，這使得很難信賴她的承諾。

---

**Context 6: Accusation/Observation**

*   **English:** Critics accused the minister of **prevaricating** on the climate change policy, arguing that his statements lacked clarity and commitment.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 批評者指責部長在氣候變遷政策上**推三阻四**，認為他的聲明缺乏明確性和承諾。

---

Remember, "prevaricate" is a great word for the GRE because it describes a specific type of untruthfulness or evasion that goes beyond a simple lie, often implying a deliberate and somewhat artful attempt to mislead without directly fabricating facts. Good luck with your studies!

## imperious

Let's break down the word "imperious" for your GRE studies.

### Meaning of "Imperious"

**Imperious** (adjective) describes someone or something that acts with an air of superiority and expects unquestioning obedience, often in a dictatorial, arrogant, or overbearing way. It suggests a commanding manner that can be perceived as haughty, tyrannical, or even unpleasantly authoritative.

Think of it as someone who behaves as if they are royalty or have absolute power, and everyone else should simply comply without question or challenge.

**Key nuances:**
*   It often carries a negative connotation, implying an abuse or excessive display of authority.
*   It's not just "bossy"; it implies a demanding nature coupled with an air of entitlement or superiority.

### Common Collocations

"Imperious" frequently modifies nouns related to manner, voice, commands, or even a person's general demeanor:

*   **Imperious tone:** A demanding, commanding way of speaking.
*   **Imperious manner/attitude:** An overbearing, arrogant way of behaving.
*   **Imperious voice:** A voice that sounds commanding and expects obedience.
*   **Imperious command/demand:** An order or request given with an air of absolute authority.
*   **Imperious gaze/look:** A look that is judgmental, authoritative, and expects compliance.
*   **Imperious gesture:** A hand movement or body language that demands attention or action.
*   **Imperious necessity/will:** (Less common, more figurative) An overwhelming, unavoidable need or a powerful, unyielding determination.

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences illustrating "imperious" in different contexts, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Describing a person's overall demeanor**
    *   **English:** The new manager's **imperious manner** quickly alienated many of his subordinates, who resented his dictatorial approach.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 新經理那種**傲慢專橫的態度**很快疏遠了他的許多下屬，他們對他獨裁的方式感到不滿。

2.  **Context: Describing a tone of voice or command**
    *   **English:** With an **imperious tone**, the queen commanded silence in the court, silencing all whispers immediately.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 女王以一種**盛氣凌人的語氣**命令法庭上所有人保持安靜，所有竊竊私語聲立刻停止了。

3.  **Context: Describing a look or gaze**
    *   **English:** He fixed me with an **imperious gaze**, as if daring me to challenge his authority or question his decision.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他以一種**威嚴的目光**盯著我，彷彿在挑戰我是否有膽量質疑他的權威或決定。

4.  **Context: Describing an abstract, powerful force or necessity**
    *   **English:** The **imperious necessity** of the moment demanded immediate action, even if it meant taking significant risks.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 形勢的**嚴峻性迫使我們**必須立即採取行動，即使這意味著要承擔巨大的風險。
    *   *(Note: "Imperious necessity" is a somewhat fixed phrase meaning an overwhelming, unavoidable need.)*

5.  **Context: Describing a historical figure's will or influence**
    *   **English:** Napoleon's **imperious will** shaped the course of European history for decades, bending nations to his ambition.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 拿破崙**專斷的意志**影響了歐洲歷史數十年，使各國都屈從於他的野心。

By understanding these contexts and collocations, you'll be well-prepared to encounter "imperious" on the GRE and use it effectively in your writing!

## sporadic

Okay, let's break down "sporadic" for your GRE studies!

---

## Sporadic

**Meaning:**

The word **sporadic** (pronounced /spəˈrædɪk/) describes something that happens **occasionally, irregularly, or in scattered, isolated instances rather than continuously or consistently.** It implies a lack of pattern, rhythm, or constant presence.

Think of it as:
*   **Not continuous:** It stops and starts.
*   **Not frequent:** It happens only sometimes.
*   **Not regular:** There's no predictable schedule.
*   **Scattered:** The occurrences are spread out and not concentrated.

**In the context of the GRE,** understanding "sporadic" is crucial for reading comprehension (e.g., describing historical events, natural phenomena, or scientific data) and for vocabulary questions (identifying synonyms like *intermittent, occasional, infrequent, irregular, isolated, scattered*, or antonyms like *constant, continuous, regular, frequent*).

---

### Common Collocations:

"Sporadic" most commonly modifies nouns that refer to events, activities, or occurrences.

*   **Sporadic outbreaks/incidents:** (e.g., of disease, violence, protests)
*   **Sporadic rainfall/showers:** (rain that isn't constant)
*   **Sporadic gunfire/clashes/fighting:** (isolated instances of conflict)
*   **Sporadic bursts/flashes:** (of light, energy, applause)
*   **Sporadic communication/visits:** (infrequent contact)
*   **Sporadic efforts/attempts:** (inconsistent trying)
*   **Sporadic attendance:** (irregular presence)
*   **Sporadic internet connection:** (unreliable, comes and goes)
*   **Sporadic symptoms/bouts:** (medical, e.g., of fever, pain)

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts with Traditional Chinese Translations:

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its use:

1.  **Context: Weather/Natural Phenomena**
    *   **English:** After days of heavy downpour, the rain became **sporadic**, with only light showers occurring now and then.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 連日暴雨後，雨勢變得**斷斷續續**，只剩下零星的陣雨。
    *   *(Note: 斷斷續續 (duàn duàn xù xù) conveys the "intermittent" or "on-and-off" nature.)*

2.  **Context: Conflict/Social Unrest**
    *   **English:** Despite the ceasefire agreement, **sporadic** clashes continued to be reported in the border region.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管已達成停火協議，邊境地區仍不斷傳出**零星的**衝突。
