    *   *(Note: 零星 (líng xīng) emphasizes the "isolated" and "scattered" nature of the clashes.)*

3.  **Context: Health/Medical Symptoms**
    *   **English:** The patient experienced **sporadic** bouts of dizziness throughout the day.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 病患全天都經歷著**斷斷續續的**頭暈。
    *   *(Note: 斷斷續續 again fits the "on-and-off" nature of the symptoms.)*

4.  **Context: Technology/Connectivity**
    *   **English:** The remote area suffered from **sporadic** internet connection, making online work challenging.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該偏遠地區網路連線**不穩定**（或 **間歇性**），導致線上工作困難重重。
    *   *(Note: 不穩定 (bù wěn dìng) means "unstable," which is a common way to describe unreliable internet. 間歇性 (jiān xiē xìng) more directly translates to "intermittent," which is a synonym for sporadic.)*

5.  **Context: Effort/Activity (e.g., Academic/Work)**
    *   **English:** His **sporadic** efforts in studying for the GRE ultimately resulted in a disappointing score.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他為準備GRE所做的**零星**努力，最終導致成績不理想。
    *   *(Note: 零星 (líng xīng) describes the "inconsistent" or "scattered" nature of his study efforts.)*

6.  **Context: Light/Sound (Sensory Experiences)**
    *   **English:** Through the dense fog, we could see only **sporadic** flashes of the lighthouse beam.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 穿過濃霧，我們只能看到燈塔光束**斷斷續續**的閃爍。
    *   *(Note: 斷斷續續 (duàn duàn xù xù) is perfect for describing something appearing and disappearing.)*

---

I hope this comprehensive explanation helps you master "sporadic" for your GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## peccadillo

Let's break down "peccadillo" for your GRE studies.

---

### **Meaning of "Peccadillo"**

**Peccadillo** (pronounced: pek-uh-DIL-oh) is a noun that refers to **a minor offense; a trivial fault or sin.**

*   It comes from the Spanish word "pecadillo," which is a diminutive of "pecado" (sin). The "-illo" ending signifies something small or unimportant.
*   It implies a small, relatively harmless transgression that is easily forgivable or overlooked, rather than a serious crime or moral failing. Think of it as a little "slip-up" or a character quirk that isn't a big deal.

**Key characteristics:**
*   **Minor:** It's not a major crime or grave sin.
*   **Forgivable:** Often seen as something that can be excused.
*   **Human:** Implies a common human weakness or slight imperfection.

**Synonyms:**
*   Minor fault, minor transgression, indiscretion, lapse, misdemeanor (in a general sense, not legal), venial sin, foible, failing.

---

### **Common Collocations for "Peccadillo"**

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these can help you use the word naturally and effectively in your writing and understand it better in reading.

*   **To commit a peccadillo:** To do something that is a minor fault.
*   **A harmless peccadillo:** Emphasizes that the fault causes no real damage.
*   **A minor peccadillo:** Redundant but sometimes used for emphasis.
*   **Overlook a peccadillo:** To ignore or forgive a small fault.
*   **Confess a peccadillo:** To admit to a minor fault.
*   **Forgive someone's peccadillos:** To excuse their small faults.
*   **His/her/their peccadillos:** Referring to someone's personal minor faults.
*   **An array/list of peccadillos:** Suggesting several minor faults.

---

### **Example Sentences in Various Contexts**

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its usage:

1.  **Context: Personal Habits/Character Flaws**
    *   **Sentence:** His occasional tardiness was considered a mere **peccadillo** by his colleagues, who admired his work ethic.
    *   **Translation (Traditional Chinese):** 他的偶爾遲到被同事們視為一個小過失，因為他們都欣賞他的職業道德。
    *   *(Note: Here, "小過失" (xiǎo guòshī) or "小毛病" (xiǎo máobìng) captures the essence of a minor fault.)*

2.  **Context: Literary/Artistic Criticism**
    *   **Sentence:** While the novel had a few stylistic **peccadillos**, its compelling plot and vivid characters made them easy to overlook.
    *   **Translation (Traditional Chinese):** 雖然這部小說有一些文體上的小缺陷，但其引人入勝的情節和生動的角色讓人很容易忽略它們。
    *   *(Note: "小缺陷" (xiǎo quēxiàn) fits well for minor flaws in a creative work.)*

3.  **Context: Public Figures/Politics**
    *   **Sentence:** The politician's indiscreet comments were initially dismissed as a **peccadillo**, but they quickly escalated into a full-blown controversy.
    *   **Translation (Traditional Chinese):** 這位政治人物的輕率言論最初被視為小過失，但很快就升級為一場全面的爭議。
    *   *(Note: "小過失" is again suitable for a minor misstep that might have larger consequences.)*

4.  **Context: Everyday Relationships/Tolerance**
    *   **Sentence:** She was willing to overlook her friend's **peccadillo** of leaving dishes in the sink, understanding that no one is perfect.
    *   **Translation (Traditional Chinese):** 她願意原諒朋友把碗盤留在水槽裡的這些小毛病，明白沒有人是完美的。
    *   *(Note: "小毛病" (xiǎo máobìng) is perfect here for a common, minor annoying habit.)*

5.  **Context: Self-deprecating Humor**
    *   **Sentence:** My greatest **peccadillo** is probably my inability to resist buying another book, even when my shelves are overflowing.
    *   **Translation (Traditional Chinese):** 我最大的小嗜好可能就是無法抗拒再買一本書，即使我的書架已經堆滿了。
    *   *(Note: "小嗜好" (xiǎo shìhào) or "小毛病" works well when referring to one's own minor, often endearing, flaws or indulgences.)*

6.  **Context: Historical/Biographical Accounts**
    *   **Sentence:** Historians often distinguish between a leader's strategic blunders and their personal **peccadillos**, which might include minor extravagance.
    *   **Translation (Traditional Chinese):** 歷史學家常區分一位領導人的戰略失誤和他們的個人小過失，後者可能包括一些小小的奢華。
    *   *(Note: "小過失" is used again, with "小小的奢華" (xiǎo xiǎo de shēhuá) illustrating a type of peccadillo.)*

---

By understanding its core meaning as a "small fault" and seeing it in varied contexts, you'll be well-equipped to handle "peccadillo" on the GRE!

## ebullient

That's a fantastic word for GRE prep! "Ebullient" is rich in meaning and often appears in contexts describing high energy and enthusiasm.

---

### Meaning of "Ebullient"

**Definition:**
"Ebullient" (adjective) means **overflowing with fervor, enthusiasm, or high spirits; vivacious; cheerful and full of energy.**

**Etymology Connection:**
The word comes from the Latin "ebullire," meaning "to boil over." This origin beautifully captures its meaning: imagine a liquid bubbling and boiling with excitement, overflowing with energy. It's not just happy; it's *visibly* and *energetically* happy, almost bursting with positive emotion.

**Synonyms:**
*   Exuberant
*   Effervescent
*   Buoyant
*   Vivacious
*   Spirited
*   Bubbly
*   Joyful
*   Lively
*   Enthusiastic

---

### Common Collocations for "Ebullient"

"Ebullient" often describes:

*   **People:** an **ebullient** personality/child/speaker/host/leader
*   **Emotions/States:** an **ebullient** mood/spirit/disposition
*   **Reactions/Atmospheres:** **ebullient** applause/cheers/laughter/welcome/celebration/atmosphere/crowd

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts (with Traditional Chinese Translations)

Here are multiple examples to help you grasp its usage:

1.  **Describing a Person's Spirit/Personality:**
    *   **English:** Despite the bad news, she maintained her **ebullient** spirit, always looking for the silver lining.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管有壞消息，她依然保持著她興高采烈的精神，總是尋找一線希望。
    *   *(Pinyin: Jǐnguǎn yǒu huài xiāoxī, tā yīrán bǎochízhe tā xìnggāocǎiliè de jīngshén, zǒng shì xúnzhǎo yī xiàn xīwàng.)*

2.  **Describing a Crowd's Reaction:**
    *   **English:** The pop star was greeted by an **ebullient** crowd, chanting his name and waving signs.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位流行歌星受到了一群興奮不已的群眾的歡迎，他們高喊著他的名字並揮舞著標語。
    *   *(Pinyin: Zhè wèi liúxíng gēxīng shòudào le yī qún xīngfènbùyǐ de qúnzhòng de huānyíng, tāmen gāohǎn zhe tā de míngzì bìng huīwǔ zhe biāoyǔ.)*

3.  **Describing a Performance or Presentation:**
    *   **English:** His **ebullient** presentation captivated the audience, who responded with enthusiastic applause.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他那熱情洋溢的演講吸引了觀眾，他們以熱烈的掌聲回應。
    *   *(Pinyin: Tā nà rèqíngyángyì de yǎnjiǎng xīyǐnle guānzhòng, tāmen yǐ rèliè de zhǎngshēng huíyīng.)*

4.  **Describing a Celebration or Joyous Occasion:**
    *   **English:** After winning the championship, the team celebrated with **ebullient** joy, spraying champagne everywhere.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 贏得冠軍後，球隊以歡騰的喜悅進行慶祝，到處噴灑香檳。
    *   *(Pinyin: Yíngdé guànjūn hòu, qiúduì yǐ huānténg de xǐyuè jìnxíng qìngzhù, dàochù pēnsǎ xiāngbīn.)*

5.  **Describing a Person's Temporary High Spirits:**
    *   **English:** Even typically reserved Mr. Henderson became quite **ebullient** after his favorite team scored the winning goal.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 即使是平常很內向的亨德森先生，在他最喜歡的球隊射入致勝球後也變得相當興高采烈。
    *   *(Pinyin: Jíshǐ shì píngcháng hěn nèixiàng de Hēngdésēn xiānshēng, zài tā zuì xǐhuān de qiúduì shèrù zhìshèng qiú hòu yě biàndé xiāngdāng xìnggāocǎiliè.)*

6.  **Describing an Overall Atmosphere:**
    *   **English:** The **ebullient** mood of the festival was infectious, spreading smiles to everyone present.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 節日歡快的氣氛很有感染力，將笑容傳染給在場的每個人。
    *   *(Pinyin: Jiérì huānkuài de qìfēn hěn yǒu gǎnrǎnlì, jiāng xiàoróng chuánrǎn gěi zàichǎng de měi gèrén.)*

---

Keep practicing these kinds of words, focusing on their nuances and how they fit into different contexts, and you'll be well on your way to mastering the GRE vocabulary! Good luck!

## benevolent

Okay, let's break down "benevolent" for your GRE prep!

---

### Understanding "Benevolent"

**Meaning:**
"Benevolent" (adjective) describes someone or something that is **well-meaning, kind, and generous.** It implies a desire to do good for others, often accompanied by active compassion and goodwill.

*   **Etymology:** The word comes from Latin: "bene" meaning "well" or "good," and "volo" meaning "to wish" or "to will." So, literally, it means "well-wishing" or "good-willed."
*   **Connotation:** It almost always carries a positive connotation, suggesting a warm, caring, and often philanthropic nature.

**Synonyms:**
Kind, compassionate, generous, philanthropic, benign, altruistic, magnanimous, clement.

**Antonyms:**
Malevolent, malicious, unkind, cruel, hostile.

---

### Common Collocations

Collocations are words that often go together naturally. Knowing these helps you use the word correctly and sound more fluent.

*   **Benevolent donor/patron:** Someone who gives generously to a cause.
*   **Benevolent organization/foundation:** A charity or group dedicated to helping others.
*   **Benevolent leader/ruler:** A leader who governs with kindness and concern for their people.
*   **Benevolent act/gesture:** A kind or charitable action.
*   **Benevolent smile/gaze:** A warm, kind expression.
*   **Benevolent spirit/nature/disposition:** A person's inherent kindness and good will.
*   **Benevolent influence/force:** A positive and helpful impact.
*   **Benevolent deity/God:** A god or higher power seen as kind and protective.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples showcasing "benevolent" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Describing a Person's Character**
    *   **English:** The elderly woman was known throughout the village for her **benevolent spirit**, always offering help and comfort to those in need.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位老婦人以她那**樂善好施的精神**聞名全村，總是向有需要的人提供幫助和慰藉。

2.  **Context: Philanthropy/Charity**
    *   **English:** A **benevolent donor** contributed a significant sum to fund the new community center, ensuring it would serve generations to come.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 一位**慈善的捐贈者**捐贈了巨額資金來資助新的社區中心，確保它能造福後代。

3.  **Context: Leadership/Authority**
    *   **English:** Despite his demanding role, the CEO adopted a **benevolent approach** to employee welfare, leading to high morale and productivity.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管職位要求嚴苛，這位執行長對員工福利採取了**仁慈的態度**，從而提升了士氣和生產力。

4.  **Context: Abstract Concept/Impersonal Force**
    *   **English:** Many cultures believe in a **benevolent deity** who watches over humanity and guides their destiny.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多文化都相信有一位**仁慈的神祇**看護著人類並引導他們的命運。

5.  **Context: A Specific Action or Expression**
    *   **English:** He offered a **benevolent smile** and a comforting word, instantly putting the nervous applicant at ease during the interview.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他露出**仁慈的微笑**並說了句安慰的話，立刻讓緊張的應徵者在面試中放鬆下來。

6.  **Context: Organizations/Institutions**
    *   **English:** The foundation was established with the **benevolent aim** of providing educational opportunities to underprivileged children.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這個基金會的成立宗旨是**以仁慈為目標**，為貧困兒童提供教育機會。

---

**GRE Tip:** When you encounter "benevolent" on the GRE, remember its core meaning of "good-willed" or "kind." It's a word with a strong positive connotation, and you'll often find it in contexts involving generosity, compassion, and a desire to help others.

## imperturbable

Let's break down "imperturbable" for your GRE studies.

---

### **Meaning of "Imperturbable"**

**Imperturbable** (adjective) means:

*   **Unable to be upset, excited, or flustered; calm.**
*   **Not easily disturbed or agitated; remarkably composed, especially under stress.**
*   It describes someone who maintains a steadfast calm and composure, no matter what challenges or difficulties they face. They are unruffled, unflappable, and serene.

**Etymology Note:** The word comes from Latin "imperturbabilis," meaning "not able to be disturbed." "Im-" means "not," and "perturb" means "to disturb or agitate."

---

### **Common Collocations for "Imperturbable"**

"Imperturbable" often describes:

*   **People:** an imperturbable leader, an imperturbable professional, an imperturbable individual.
*   **Qualities/States of Mind:**
    *   **Imperturbable calm/composure/demeanor/expression:** describes their outward appearance of tranquility.
    *   **Imperturbable nature/spirit/temperament:** describes an inherent quality.
    *   **Imperturbable resolve/courage:** describes their steadfastness in purpose or bravery.

You'll often see it used with verbs like:

*   **remain imperturbable**
*   **appear imperturbable**
*   **maintain an imperturbable front/expression**

---

### **Example Sentences in Various Contexts with Traditional Chinese Translations**

Here are multiple examples to help you grasp its usage:

1.  **Context: Professionalism/Leadership under pressure**
    *   **English:** Despite the chaos of the emergency room, the head surgeon remained **imperturbable**, calmly directing her team through the complex procedure.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管急診室內一片混亂，這位主治外科醫生卻依然**沉著冷靜**，有條不紊地指揮她的團隊進行複雜的手術。
    *   *Note:* Here, "沉著冷靜" (chénzhuó lěngjìng) perfectly captures the calm and collected nature.

2.  **Context: Personal Character/Stoicism**
    *   **English:** Her **imperturbable** nature made her an excellent therapist; nothing a client said seemed to faze her.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她**泰然自若**的性格使她成為一位優秀的治療師；客戶所說的任何話似乎都無法動搖她。
    *   *Note:* "泰然自若" (tàirán zìruò) emphasizes a serene and undisturbed composure, especially in difficult situations.

3.  **Context: Sports/Competition (e.g., poker, chess)**
    *   **English:** The grandmaster maintained an **imperturbable** expression throughout the chess match, giving away no hints of his strategy.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位特級大師在整個西洋棋比賽中始終保持著**不動聲色**的表情，沒有透露出任何戰略線索。
    *   *Note:* "不動聲色" (bù dòng shēng sè) literally means "not moving sound or color," implying no outward display of emotion or intention – perfect for a poker face or chess master.

4.  **Context: Facing Adversity/Resilience**
    *   **English:** Even after losing everything in the fire, he faced his new reality with **imperturbable** resolve.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 即使在大火中失去了一切，他仍以**堅定不移**的決心面對新的現實。
    *   *Note:* While "imperturbable" refers to the *calm* aspect, when paired with "resolve," "堅定不移" (jiāndìng bù yí) emphasizes the unshakeable, steady nature of his determination. One could also say 泰然的決心 but 堅定不移 is stronger with resolve.

5.  **Context: Everyday Annoyances**
    *   **English:** While everyone else was panicking about the missed flight, she remained **imperturbable**, calmly researching alternative routes on her phone.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當其他人都為錯過航班而恐慌時，她卻保持著**鎮定**，冷靜地在手機上研究替代路線。
    *   *Note:* "鎮定" (zhèndìng) is a direct and common translation for "calm" or "composed," fitting for an everyday scenario.

---

Understanding "imperturbable" is key for the GRE as it frequently appears in contexts describing ideal qualities for leaders, professionals, and individuals demonstrating emotional strength. Good luck with your studies!

## strident

Okay, let's break down the word "strident" for your GRE preparation.

## Strident

**Meaning:**

"Strident" primarily means **loud, harsh, and grating in sound**. When applied to non-sound contexts (like arguments, opinions, or policies), it means **forceful, insistent, or unpleasantly vehement, often conveying an aggressive or uncompromising tone.** It almost always carries a negative connotation, suggesting something unpleasant or jarring.

**Synonyms often seen on the GRE:**
*   **For sound:** harsh, shrill, piercing, grating, raucous, discordant.
*   **For tone/argument:** vehement, insistent, clamorous, vociferous, abrasive, aggressive, uncompromising.

---

**Common Collocations:**

"Strident" most commonly collocates with words related to **sound** or **expression/opinion**:

1.  **Sound:**
    *   **Strident voice/tone:** A loud, harsh, or shrill way of speaking.
    *   **Strident cry/shriek/whistle:** A piercing, unpleasant sound.
    *   **Strident noise/sound:** Any loud, grating sound.
    *   **Strident alarm/music:** Jarring or discordant sounds.

2.  **Expression/Opinion/Stance:**
    *   **Strident rhetoric/arguments:** Forceful, aggressive, and often uncompromising language used to persuade or oppose.
    *   **Strident demands/protests/criticism:** Loud, insistent, and often harsh expressions of what is wanted or disapproved of.
    *   **Strident views/stance:** Strong, uncompromising, and often unpleasantly expressed opinions or positions.
    *   **Strident opposition:** Forceful and vehement resistance.

---

**Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are examples with Traditional Chinese translations to help you grasp the nuances:

1.  **Context: Literal sound (Voice)**
    *   **English:** Her **strident voice** cut through the clamor of the marketplace, demanding attention.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她的**刺耳聲**穿透了市場的喧囂，要求人們注意。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "strident" describes a voice that is unpleasantly loud and sharp, like a screech or shriek.

2.  **Context: Literal sound (Object/Animal)**
    *   **English:** The **strident whine** of the circular saw echoed through the workshop.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 圓鋸**刺耳的嗚嗚聲**在工廠車間迴盪。
    *   *Explanation:* This refers to the unpleasant, high-pitched noise made by a machine.

3.  **Context: Political/Social Tone (Rhetoric)**
    *   **English:** The politician's **strident rhetoric** alienated many moderate voters who preferred a more conciliatory approach.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那位政治人物**尖銳的言辭**疏遠了許多喜歡採取溫和立場的選民。
    *   *Explanation:* "Strident rhetoric" means the language used was aggressive, overly forceful, and perhaps uncompromising, making it off-putting.

4.  **Context: Protest/Demands**
    *   **English:** Despite the activists' **strident demands** for immediate action, the city council decided to table the motion.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管激進份子**強烈的要求**立即採取行動，市議會仍決定擱置動議。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "strident demands" implies that the demands were loud, insistent, and perhaps unpleasantly forceful or uncompromising.

5.  **Context: Personal Opinion/Tone**
    *   **English:** He adopted a **strident tone** whenever discussing environmental policy, making it difficult for others to engage in calm debate.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 每當他討論環境政策時，他總是採用一種**刺耳的語氣**，讓其他人難以進行平靜的辯論。
    *   *Explanation:* This describes a manner of speaking that is aggressively insistent or unpleasantly forceful, making constructive dialogue challenging.

6.  **Context: Artistic/Literary (less common but possible)**
    *   **English:** The critic described the experimental play as having a **strident, almost jarring effect** on the audience, due to its unconventional soundscape.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 評論家形容這齣實驗劇因為其非傳統的音景，對觀眾產生了一種**刺耳、幾乎是令人不適的效果**。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "strident" refers to an unpleasant or harsh quality in an artistic work, like a discordant sound or an aggressively presented theme.

By understanding these various applications, you'll be well-prepared to encounter "strident" in its different forms on the GRE. Good luck with your studies!

## aggregate

That's an excellent word to master for the GRE! "Aggregate" is versatile and appears frequently in various contexts, especially in data analysis, economics, and general summaries.

Let's break it down thoroughly:

---

## Meaning of "Aggregate"

"Aggregate" fundamentally means **a whole or total amount formed by combining many separate elements.** It emphasizes the idea of collecting distinct parts into a single, larger unit.

It can function as a **noun, verb,** or **adjective**.

---

### 1. As a Noun: **`[ˈæɡrɪɡɪt]`**

*   **Meaning:** A total, sum, or collection of distinct parts, often referring to a collection of individual units that are considered together.
*   **Synonyms:** Total, sum, collection, compilation, mass, whole.

### 2. As a Verb: **`[ˈæɡrɪˌɡeɪt]`**

*   **Meaning:** To collect or combine separate elements into a single mass, sum, or total.
*   **Synonyms:** Accumulate, gather, collect, combine, compile, amass.

### 3. As an Adjective: **`[ˈæɡrɪɡɪt]`**

*   **Meaning:** Formed by the collection of many separate units or particles; total.
*   **Synonyms:** Total, combined, collective, overall.

---

## Common Collocations

Here are common collocations for "aggregate," categorized by its function:

**As an Adjective (most common in GRE-like contexts):**

*   **Aggregate data/information:** Collected or combined data/information.
*   **Aggregate score/total:** The total score or sum from multiple parts/games.
*   **Aggregate demand/supply:** (Economics) The total demand/supply for goods and services in an economy.
*   **Aggregate output/income:** (Economics) The total production/income in an economy.
*   **Aggregate effect:** The combined or total effect of multiple factors.
*   **Aggregate value/amount:** The total value or amount.
*   **Aggregate population:** The total number of individuals in a group/area.

**As a Verb:**

*   **To aggregate data/information/results:** To collect and combine.
*   **To aggregate resources:** To bring together various resources.
*   **To aggregate demand/supply:** To calculate the total demand/supply.

**As a Noun:**

*   **On aggregate:** (Often in sports) Referring to the total score over multiple matches.
*   **The aggregate of something:** The total sum or collection of something.
*   **An aggregate of diverse elements:** A collection made up of different parts.

---

## Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are examples illustrating its different uses, along with Traditional Chinese translations:

---

**1. Economics Context (Adjective/Noun)**

*   **English:** The government implemented policies to stimulate **aggregate demand** and boost the nation's economy.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 政府實施了刺激**總需求**的政策，以提振國家經濟。
    *(Here, "aggregate demand" refers to the total demand for goods and services in an economy.)*

*   **English:** Economists carefully track the **aggregate output** of all industries to measure economic growth.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 經濟學家密切追蹤所有產業的**總產出**，以衡量經濟成長。
    *(Here, "aggregate output" refers to the total production.)*

*   **English:** The report analyzed the **aggregate** of consumer spending across different demographics.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 該報告分析了不同客群的消費者支出**總和**。
    *(Here, "aggregate" is a noun, meaning the total sum or collection.)*

---

**2. Data/Information Context (Verb/Adjective)**

*   **English:** Researchers must **aggregate** data from various sources to form a comprehensive understanding of the phenomenon.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 研究人員必須將來自各個來源的數據**匯總**，才能對現象形成全面性的理解。
    *(Here, "aggregate" is a verb, meaning to collect and combine.)*

*   **English:** The dashboard provides an **aggregate view** of all customer feedback, making it easier to identify trends.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 該儀表板提供了所有客戶回饋的**總體視圖**，便於識別趨勢。
    *(Here, "aggregate view" means a combined/total view.)*

---

**3. Sports Context (Noun - "on aggregate")**

*   **English:** Despite losing the second match, Team A won the championship **on aggregate**, having scored more goals in total over the two legs.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管輸掉了第二場比賽，A隊還是憑藉**總比分**贏得了冠軍，他們在兩回合的比賽中總共進了更多球。
    *(Here, "on aggregate" refers to the total score accumulated over multiple games/legs.)*

---

**4. General Collection/Accumulation (Verb/Noun)**

*   **English:** Dust and debris tend to **aggregate** in the corners of the room if not regularly cleaned.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 如果不定期清潔，灰塵和碎屑會傾向於在房間角落**聚集**。
    *(Here, "aggregate" is an intransitive verb, meaning to gather or accumulate.)*

*   **English:** The final exam score is an **aggregate** of all quizzes and homework assignments throughout the semester.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 期末考試成績是學期內所有小考和家庭作業的**總和**。
    *(Here, "aggregate" is a noun, meaning the total sum.)*

---

**5. Business/Finance Context (Adjective)**

*   **English:** The company's **aggregate sales** for the quarter exceeded expectations, indicating strong market performance.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 該公司本季度的**總銷售額**超出預期，顯示出強勁的市場表現。
    *(Here, "aggregate sales" means total sales.)*

*   **English:** Investors look at the **aggregate returns** of a diverse portfolio rather than individual stock performance.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 投資者會查看多元化投資組合的**總體報酬**，而非單一股票的表現。
    *(Here, "aggregate returns" means total returns.)*

---

Mastering "aggregate" will definitely give you an edge on the GRE, especially in reading comprehension and analytical writing sections! Good luck with your studies!

## juxtapose

That's a great word for GRE prep! "Juxtapose" is a highly useful academic and analytical term.

---

### Understanding "Juxtapose"

**Meaning:**

The verb **"juxtapose"** (pronounced: JUHK-stuh-pohz) means to place two or more things (often ideas, images, or elements) side by side, especially in order to compare or contrast them, or to create an interesting or thought-provoking effect.

*   **Core Idea:** It's not just putting things next to each other randomly; there's always an *intention* or a *purpose* behind the placement. This purpose is usually to highlight similarities, emphasize differences, reveal an irony, create a particular mood, or deepen understanding.
*   **Part of Speech:** Verb.
*   **Noun Form:** The act of juxtaposing, or the state of being juxtaposed, is called **"juxtaposition"** (JUHK-stuh-puh-ZI-shun).

---

### Common Collocations for "Juxtapose"

Collocations are words that often go together naturally. Knowing these helps you use the word correctly and sound more native.

**Verbs:**
*   **Juxtapose X *with* Y:** This is the most common structure. (e.g., "juxtapose joy *with* sorrow")

**Nouns commonly juxtaposed:**
*   Ideas, concepts, themes
*   Images, visuals, scenes, frames
*   Colors, textures, shapes
*   Characters, personalities
*   Facts, figures, data
*   Words, phrases, lines
*   Past, present, future
*   Wealth, poverty
*   Light, shadow
*   Comedy, tragedy
*   Tradition, modernity
*   Theory, practice

**Adverbs that often describe *how* things are juxtaposed:**
*   **Cleverly, skillfully, artfully:** indicates deliberate and effective placement.
*   **Sharply, starkly:** highlights a strong contrast.
*   **Deliberately, intentionally:** emphasizes purpose.
*   **Ironically:** points to a contradictory effect.
*   **Effectively, powerfully:** describes the impact of the juxtaposition.

**Common phrases with the noun form "juxtaposition":**
*   **The juxtaposition *of* X *and* Y:** (e.g., "the juxtaposition of old and new")
*   **A sharp/stark/clever/ironic juxtaposition:** describing the nature of the contrast.
*   **The juxtaposition highlights/reveals/emphasizes/creates...**

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its use:

1.  **Context: Architecture/Urban Planning**
    *   **English:** The architect chose to **juxtapose** the sleek, modern glass tower with the historic stone church next door, creating a striking visual contrast.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 建築師選擇將流線型的現代玻璃大樓與隔壁的歷史石砌教堂**並置**，以形成強烈的視覺對比。
    *   (Jiànzhú shī xuǎnzé jiāng liúxiàn xíng de xiàndài bōlí dàlóu yǔ gébì de lìshǐ shíqì jiàotáng **bìngzhì**, yǐ xíngchéng qiángliè de shìjué duìbǐ.)

2.  **Context: Literature/Storytelling**
    *   **English:** In her latest novel, the author skillfully **juxtaposes** moments of profound joy with scenes of deep sorrow to explore the complexities of human emotion.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在她最新小說中，作者巧妙地將極度歡樂的時刻與深沉悲傷的場景**並列**，以探索人類情感的複雜性。
    *   (Zài tā zuìxīn xiǎoshuō zhōng, zuòzhě qiǎomiào de jiāng jídù huānlè de shíkè yǔ shēnchén bēishāng de chǎngjǐng **bìngliè**, yǐ tànsuǒ rénlèi qínggǎn de fùzá xìng.)

3.  **Context: Art/Photography (using the noun form)**
    *   **English:** The exhibition curator highlighted the powerful **juxtaposition** of vibrant abstract paintings against muted, minimalist sculptures, inviting a dialogue between different artistic expressions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 策展人強調了色彩鮮明的抽象畫與柔和簡約的雕塑之間強烈的**並置**（或「對照」），邀請觀眾在不同的藝術表達形式之間進行對話。
    *   (Cèzhǎnrén qiángdiàole cǎisè xiānmíng de chōuxiàng huà yǔ róuhé jiǎnyuē de diāosù zhī jiān qiángliè de **bìngzhì** (huò "duìzhào"), yāoqǐng guānzhòng zài bùtóng de yìshù biǎodá xíngshì zhī jiān jìnxíng duìhuà.)

4.  **Context: Social Commentary/Journalism**
    *   **English:** The article **juxtaposes** statistics on rising homelessness with reports of increased luxury development, underscoring a growing societal divide.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這篇文章將無家者人數不斷上升的數據與奢華開發項目增加的報導**對照**（或「並列」），突顯日益擴大的社會鴻溝。
    *   (Zhè piān wénzhāng jiāng wújiāzhě rénshù bùduàn shàngshēng de shùjù yǔ shēhuá kāifā xiàngmù zēngjiā de bàodǎo **duìzhào** (huò "bìngliè"), túxiǎn rìyì kuòdà de shèhuì hónggōu.)

5.  **Context: Rhetoric/Argumentation**
    *   **English:** During the debate, the politician **juxtaposed** his opponent's past statements with their current policy proposals to expose inconsistencies.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在辯論中，這位政治人物將對手過去的發言與其當前的政策提案**對照**（或「並置」），以揭露前後矛盾之處。
    *   (Zài biànlùn zhōng, zhè wèi zhèngzhì rénwù jiāng duìshǒu guòqù de fāyán yǔ qí dāngqián de zhèngcè tí'àn **duìzhào** (huò "bìngzhì"), yǐ jiēlù qiánhòu máodùn zhī chù.)

6.  **Context: Music/Performance**
    *   **English:** The composer deliberately **juxtaposed** a gentle, flowing melody with a sharp, dissonant chord progression to create a sense of unease.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 作曲家刻意將輕柔流暢的旋律與尖銳刺耳的和弦進行**並置**，以營造一種不安感。
    *   (Zuòqǔjiā kèyì jiāng qīngróu liúchàng de xuánlǜ yǔ jiānruì cì'ěr de héxián jìnxíng **bìngzhì**, yǐ yíngzào yī zhǒng bù'ān gǎn.)

---

By understanding its core meaning, common collocations, and seeing it in varied contexts, you'll be well-prepared to identify and use "juxtapose" effectively on the GRE and beyond!

## onerous

Okay, let's break down the word "onerous" for your GRE studies.

---

### **Onerous**

*   **Pronunciation:** ON-er-uhs

*   **Meaning:**
    *   **Burdensome; involving an amount of effort and difficulty that is oppressively heavy.**
    *   **Taxing; demanding a great deal of effort, especially financial or emotional.**
    *   **Imposing a significant obligation or responsibility.**

    Think of something that feels like a *heavy weight* on your shoulders, whether it's a task, a debt, a responsibility, or a condition. It's not just "difficult"; it implies a *burden* or a *hardship*.

*   **Etymology Tip:** The word comes from the Latin word "onus," which means "burden." This can help you remember its core meaning.

---

### **Common Collocations**

"Onerous" frequently describes:

*   **Tasks/Duties/Responsibilities/Obligations:**
    *   an **onerous task**
    *   an **onerous duty**
    *   an **onerous responsibility**
    *   **onerous obligations**
    *   an **onerous workload**

*   **Conditions/Terms/Regulations/Requirements:**
    *   **onerous conditions**
    *   **onerous terms** (especially in a contract)
    *   **onerous regulations**
    *   **onerous requirements**

*   **Financial/Legal aspects:**
    *   **onerous debt**
    *   **onerous contract**
    *   **financially onerous**

---

### **Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts**

Here are examples showing "onerous" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Work/Tasks**
    *   **English:** The CEO found the task of turning around the failing company to be an incredibly **onerous** one, requiring countless late nights and difficult decisions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 執行長發現扭轉這家經營不善公司的任務異常**繁重**，需要無數個深夜和艱難的決策。
    *   *(Note: 繁重 (fánzhòng) conveys "heavy, arduous, burdensome" for tasks.)*

2.  **Context: Legal/Contractual**
    *   **English:** Many small businesses find the new government regulations to be financially **onerous**, limiting their ability to grow and hire.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多小型企業認為新的政府法規在財務上非常**苛刻**，限制了他們成長和僱傭的能力。
    *   *(Note: 苛刻 (kēkè) conveys "harsh, exacting, severe" which fits the financial burden of regulations.)*

3.  **Context: Personal Responsibility/Burden**
    *   **English:** Taking care of his ailing parents became an **onerous** but deeply fulfilling responsibility for John.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 照顧他年邁的父母對約翰來說是一項**沉重**但卻意義深遠的責任。
    *   *(Note: 沉重 (chénzhòng) conveys "heavy, weighty" which fits a personal, emotional burden.)*

4.  **Context: Physical/Strenuous Activity (less common but possible)**
    *   **English:** The hike up the steep mountain with a heavy backpack proved to be an **onerous** challenge, testing the limits of their endurance.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 背著沉重背包攀登陡峭山脈的徒步旅行證明是一項**艱鉅**的挑戰，考驗著他們的耐力極限。
    *   *(Note: 艱鉅 (jiānjù) conveys "arduous, difficult, formidable" for a tough challenge.)*

5.  **Context: Financial Debt/Obligations**
    *   **English:** Student loan debt can feel particularly **onerous** for graduates trying to establish their careers and independence.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 對於那些試圖建立事業和獨立生活的畢業生來說，學生貸款債務尤其會感到**沉重**。
    *   *(Note: 沉重 (chénzhòng) again fits a "heavy" financial burden.)*

---

Keep practicing these words in context. Good luck with your GRE studies!

## succinct

That's a great word for GRE prep! "Succinct" is a favorite on standardized tests because it tests your understanding of precision and efficiency in language.

---

### Meaning of "Succinct"

**Succinct** (adjective) means:

1.  **Brief and clearly expressed.**
2.  **Using few words; to the point; concise.**

It implies that something is not only short but also gets its message across effectively, without unnecessary details or verbosity. It's about being both short *and* clear.

*   **Pronunciation:** suk-SINGKT
*   **Synonyms:** concise, terse, pithy, laconic, brief, short, crisp, epigrammatic.
*   **Antonyms:** verbose, wordy, rambling, long-winded, prolix.

---

### Common Collocations for "Succinct"

"Succinct" commonly modifies nouns related to communication, information, and expression. You'll often see it paired with:

**Adjective + Noun:**
*   **succinct answer/response/reply:** A short, clear answer.
*   **succinct summary/overview/explanation/description:** A brief and clear account of something.
*   **succinct style/prose/writing:** A writing manner that is concise and to the point.
*   **succinct report/brief:** A short, precise document.
*   **succinct argument/point:** A well-articulated, concise point or line of reasoning.
*   **succinct statement:** A short, precise declaration.

**Verb + Adverb (using "succinctly"):**
*   **explain succinctly:** To explain something briefly and clearly.
*   **state succinctly:** To say something in a few words.
*   **summarize succinctly:** To give a brief overview.
*   **respond succinctly:** To reply briefly and to the point.

---

### Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are several examples, showcasing different contexts and common collocations:

1.  **Context: Academic/Instructional**
    *   **English:** The professor asked for a **succinct answer**, not a lengthy essay, to the complex question.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 教授要求針對這個複雜的問題給出一個**簡潔的答案**，而不是一篇冗長的論文。
    *   (Jiàoshòu yāoqíu zhēnduì zhège fùzá de wèntí gěichū yīgè **jiǎnjié de dá'àn**, érbùshì yī piān rǒngcháng de lùnwén.)

2.  **Context: Professional/Business**
    *   **English:** Her presentation provided a **succinct overview** of the company's financial performance last quarter.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她的演講對公司上季度的財務表現提供了**簡明扼要的概述**。
    *   (Tā de yǎnjiǎng duì gōngsī shàng jìdù de cáiwù biǎoxiàn tígōngle **jiǎnmíng èyào de gàishù**.)

3.  **Context: Literature/Writing Style**
    *   **English:** Good technical writing often requires a **succinct style**, conveying information efficiently without unnecessary jargon.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 好的技術寫作通常需要**簡潔的風格**，有效地傳達信息而沒有不必要的術語。
    *   (Hǎo de jìshù xiězuò tōngcháng xūyào **jiǎnjié de fēnggé**, yǒuxiào de chuándá xìnxī ér méiyǒu búbìyào de shùyǔ.)

4.  **Context: Legal/Argumentation**
    *   **English:** The lawyer delivered a **succinct argument** that highlighted the key points of the defense case.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 律師提出了一個**簡潔有力的論點**，突出了辯護案件的關鍵點。
    *   (Lǜshī tíchūle yīgè **jiǎnjié yǒulì de lùndiǎn**, túchūle biànhù ànjiàn de guānjiàn diǎn.)

5.  **Context: Everyday Communication/Personal Traits**
    *   **English:** He's known for his ability to **explain complex ideas succinctly**, making them accessible to everyone.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他以其**簡潔地解釋複雜概念**的能力而聞名，使每個人都能理解。
    *   (Tā yǐ qí **jiǎnjié de jiěshì fùzá gàiniàn** de nénglì ér wénmíng, shǐ měi gè rén dōu néng lǐjiě.)

---

Mastering "succinct" and its collocations will definitely help you in the Verbal Reasoning section of the GRE, especially in Sentence Equivalence and Text Completion questions!

---

# GRE Vocabulary Explanations

## partisan

Let's break down the word "partisan" for your GRE preparation.

## Meaning of "Partisan"

**Core Meaning:** Showing strong and often uncritical support for a party, group, or cause. It implies a bias or prejudice towards one side and often against another.

**Detailed Explanation:**

1.  **As an Adjective:**
    *   **Definition:** Characterized by or exhibiting a strong, often unreasoning, allegiance to one party, faction, or cause.
    *   **Connotation:** Often negative, implying narrow-mindedness, an inability to compromise, or a preference for one's own group regardless of objective merit. It suggests a lack of objectivity or impartiality.
    *   **Example:** "a partisan debate" (a debate where each side strongly defends its own views and attacks the other, making compromise difficult).

2.  **As a Noun:**
    *   **Definition 1 (most common in modern usage):** A strong, often uncritical, supporter of a party, group, or cause.
    *   **Example:** "He was a staunch partisan of the new economic policy."
    *   **Definition 2 (less common in GRE context, more historical/military):** A member of an armed group engaged in irregular warfare, especially one fighting behind enemy lines. (Think guerrilla fighter). While this definition exists, it's less likely to appear on the GRE than the political/biased sense.

**Key Idea for GRE:** When you see "partisan," think **"biased," "one-sided," "loyal to a fault,"** especially in political or ideological contexts. The opposite would be **"impartial," "objective," "non-partisan."**

## Common Collocations

Here are some common words that "partisan" often goes with:

*   **Partisan politics / political divide:** Refers to the often hostile and uncooperative nature of political interactions.
*   **Partisan rhetoric:** Language used to promote one side's views and attack the other's.
*   **Partisan divide / split:** A deep division or disagreement between groups.
*   **Partisan views / approach:** Opinions or methods that are biased towards one side.
*   **Partisan attacks:** Criticisms or accusations made by one political side against another.
*   **Fiercely / Deeply / Highly partisan:** Intensifiers showing a very strong degree of bias.
*   **Partisan of [something]:** (Noun usage) A supporter of a specific cause or group.

## Example Sentences in Various Contexts

1.  **Political Debate/Legislation Context (Adjective):**
    *   **English:** The congressional debate quickly devolved into a series of **partisan attacks**, making genuine compromise impossible.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 國會辯論很快就演變成一連串的**黨派攻擊**，使得真正的妥協變得不可能。

2.  **Media/Journalism Context (Adjective):**
    *   **English:** Many worry that the 24-hour news cycle has become too **partisan**, presenting information through a political lens rather than objectively.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多人擔心二十四小時新聞循環變得過於**黨派化**，呈現資訊時透過政治視角而非客觀。

3.  **Supporter/Advocate Context (Noun):**
    *   **English:** As a lifelong **partisan of** the conservative movement, he consistently voted along party lines.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 作為保守運動的終生**黨派支持者**，他投票時始終與黨派路線保持一致。

4.  **Social/Non-Political Context (Adjective):**
    *   **English:** Even in a seemingly non-political discussion about sports, strong **partisan feelings** often emerge among rival fan bases.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 即使在看似非政治的體育討論中，強烈的**黨派情緒**也常在競爭對手的粉絲群體中浮現。

5.  **Action/Decision Context (Adjective):**
    *   **English:** The bill was passed purely on a **partisan vote**, with no support from the opposing party.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這項法案純粹是憑藉著**黨派投票**通過的，沒有得到對立黨派的任何支持。

6.  **Consequence/Outcome Context (Adjective):**
    *   **English:** The country's progress is often hampered by the deeply ingrained **partisan divide** between its main political factions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該國的進步常因主要政治派系之間根深蒂固的**黨派分歧**而受到阻礙。

Understanding "partisan" in these contexts, especially its negative connotations in political discourse, will be very helpful for the GRE Verbal Reasoning section.

## boorish

Let's break down the word "boorish" for your GRE preparation.

---

### Meaning of "Boorish"

**Boorish** (adjective) refers to someone who is **rude, unrefined, ill-mannered, and lacking in social graces.** It describes behavior that is coarse, crude, and often inconsiderate of others' feelings or social conventions.

*   **Etymology Tip:** The word "boor" originally referred to a peasant or farmer, and historically, such people were sometimes stereotyped as being uneducated or uncultured compared to city dwellers or nobility. This etymology helps reinforce the meaning of lacking refinement.

**Synonyms:** uncouth, crude, coarse, loutish, churlish, ill-bred, ungentlemanly/unladylike, unrefined, vulgar.

**Antonyms:** refined, polite, courteous, sophisticated, elegant, urbane, polished.

---

### Common Collocations for "Boorish"

Collocations are words that frequently go together. Knowing these can help you use "boorish" naturally and recognize it in GRE sentences.

*   **Boorish behavior/conduct:** Rude and ill-mannered actions.
*   **Boorish remarks/comments/jokes:** Offensive or inappropriate things said.
*   **Boorish manners:** Uncouth ways of acting, especially at social events.
*   **Boorish attitude:** A general disposition of being rude and inconsiderate.
*   **A boorish person/man:** Someone who consistently displays these qualities.
*   **His boorish ways:** Referring to an individual's habitual rude actions.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences using "boorish" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations.

1.  **Context: Social Gathering/Dinner Party**
    *   **English:** His **boorish behavior** at the dinner party, like burping loudly and interrupting others, made everyone uncomfortable.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他在晚宴上粗魯的舉止，像是大聲打嗝和打斷別人說話，讓所有人都感到不自在。
        *   (Tā zài wǎnyàn shàng cūlǔ de jǔzhǐ, xiàng shì dàshēng dágē hé dǎduàn biérén shuōhuà, ràng suǒyǒu rén dōu gǎndào bùzìzài.)

2.  **Context: Professional/Workplace Environment**
    *   **English:** Despite his talent, his **boorish remarks** in meetings often alienated colleagues and clients.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管他很有才華，但他會議中粗鄙的言論卻常常疏遠同事和客戶。
        *   (Jǐnguǎn tā hěn yǒu cáihuá, dàn tā huìyì zhōng cūpì de yánlùn què chángcháng shūyuǎn tóngshì hé kèhù.)

3.  **Context: Public Space/Travel**
    *   **English:** The tourist's **boorish conduct**, including littering and shouting, drew glares from the locals.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那位遊客粗野的行為，包括亂丟垃圾和叫喊，引來當地人的白眼。
        *   (Nà wèi yóukè cūyě de xíngwéi, bāokuò luàn diū lèsè hé jiàohǎn, yǐnlái dāngdì rén de báiyǎn.)

4.  **Context: Character Description/General Personality**
    *   **English:** He was known for his **boorish nature**, always preferring crude jokes over polite conversation.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他以其粗俗的本性聞名，總是喜歡說粗俗的笑話，而不是進行有禮貌的談話。
        *   (Tā yǐ qí cūsú de běnxìng wénmíng, zǒng shì xǐhuān shuō cūsú de xiàohuà, ér bùshì jìnxíng yǒu lǐmào de tánhuà.)

5.  **Context: Contrast/Comparison**
    *   **English:** Unlike his refined sister, his **boorish manners** often made him a difficult guest to invite to formal events.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 和他那文雅的妹妹不同，他粗魯的舉止讓他很難被邀請參加正式場合。
        *   (Hé tā nà wényǎ de mèimei bùtóng, tā cūlǔ de jǔzhǐ ràng tā hěn nán bèi yāoqǐng cānjiā zhèngshì chǎnghé.)

---

By understanding the definition, common collocations, and seeing "boorish" used in various contexts, you'll be well-prepared to encounter it on the GRE!

## amalgamate

Let's break down "amalgamate" for your GRE prep!

### **Amalgamate**

**Meaning:**
*   **Verb:** To combine or unite to form one organization or structure. It implies a thorough mixing or blending of distinct elements into a cohesive whole.
*   **Etymology Hint:** The word comes from "amalgam," which is a mixture (especially of mercury with another metal). This helps reinforce the idea of a thorough mixing.

**Common Collocations:**

"Amalgamate" often appears with prepositions and specific types of entities:

*   **Amalgamate with:** (e.g., "Company A amalgamated with Company B")
*   **Amalgamate into:** (e.g., "The two departments amalgamated into a single division")
*   **Amalgamate from:** (less common, but can describe the source, e.g., "The new entity was amalgamated from various smaller groups")
*   **Nouns it combines/is combined:** companies, organizations, departments, groups, entities, ideas, traditions, resources, data, elements, styles.
*   **Adverbs describing the process:** successfully, seamlessly, effectively, gradually, strategically.

**Synonyms (for GRE Vocabulary Building):**
*   Merge
*   Unite
*   Combine
*   Consolidate
*   Integrate
*   Blend
*   Fuse

**Antonyms:**
*   Separate
*   Divide
*   Disassociate
*   Fragment

### **Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are multiple examples showcasing "amalgamate" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Business/Corporate Context:**
    *   **English:** After months of negotiation, the two rival companies decided to **amalgamate** to form a stronger, unified entity in the market.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經過數月的談判，這兩家競爭對手公司決定**合併**，以在市場上形成一個更強大、統一的實體。

2.  **Cultural/Artistic Context:**
    *   **English:** The artist's distinctive style manages to **amalgamate** traditional Japanese aesthetics with contemporary Western influences, creating something truly unique.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位藝術家獨特的風格成功地將日本傳統美學與當代西方影響**融合**，創造出真正獨特的藝術作品。

3.  **Abstract/Ideas/Academic Context:**
    *   **English:** The new theoretical framework aims to **amalgamate** insights from psychology, sociology, and economics into a comprehensive understanding of human behavior.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這個新的理論框架旨在將心理學、社會學和經濟學的見解**融合成**對人類行為的全面理解。

4.  **Organizational/Government Context:**
    *   **English:** To improve efficiency and reduce bureaucracy, the city council voted to **amalgamate** several small municipal departments into one larger, more streamlined agency.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 為了提高效率和減少官僚作風，市議會投票決定將幾個小型市政部門**合併**成一個更大、更精簡的機構。

5.  **Social/Community Context (often intransitive use):**
    *   **English:** Over time, various immigrant communities in the city began to **amalgamate**, sharing traditions and creating a unique local culture.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 隨著時間的推移，城市中各個移民社區開始**融合**，分享傳統並創造出獨特的當地文化。

6.  **Scientific/Technical Context:**
    *   **English:** Researchers had to **amalgamate** data from various independent studies to get a statistically significant sample size for their meta-analysis.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 研究人員必須**整合**來自各種獨立研究的數據，才能為他們的統合分析獲得具有統計意義的樣本量。

**Key Takeaway for GRE:**
"Amalgamate" is a versatile and relatively formal verb. When you see it on the GRE, think "combine," "unite," or "merge," especially in a way that creates a new, unified whole from distinct parts. Its appearance often indicates a process of consolidation or integration.

## parity

"Parity" is a valuable word for the GRE, especially in Reading Comprehension passages and Sentence Equivalence/Text Completion questions that deal with social, economic, or political issues.

---

### Meaning of "Parity"

**Parity** (noun) refers to the state or condition of being **equal**, especially in status, value, amount, or rank. It signifies **equivalence** or **sameness**.

Think of it as achieving a balance or a level playing field between two or more groups, elements, or concepts. It's often used in contexts where there *were* differences, and an effort is being made to achieve equality.

*   **Etymology Tip:** The word comes from the Latin "par," meaning "equal." You might recognize this root in words like "peer" (someone of equal standing) or "disparate" (unequal, different).

---

### Common Collocations of "Parity"

Collocations are words that frequently appear together. Knowing these can help you understand and use "parity" naturally.

*   **Seek/Strive for parity:** To actively try to achieve equality.
*   **Achieve/Reach parity:** To successfully attain equality.
*   **Maintain parity:** To keep equality once it's been established.
*   **Lack of parity:** When equality is absent.
*   **Wage/Pay parity:** Equal pay for equal work.
*   **Gender parity:** Equality between genders, especially in representation or opportunity.
*   **Price parity:** Ensuring a product is sold at the same price across different channels or regions.
*   **Competitive parity:** When competitors are equally matched in strength or ability.
*   **Feature parity:** When different versions or products offer the same features.
*   **Parity with/between:** Often followed by the entities that are being compared. (e.g., *parity with* the previous model, *parity between* the two groups).

---

### Example Sentences and Traditional Chinese Translations

Here are multiple examples of "parity" in various contexts:

1.  **Context: Wages/Economics**
    *   **English:** The union is fighting to achieve **wage parity** for all employees, regardless of gender or race.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 工會正在努力為所有員工爭取「同工同酬」（工資上的平等），不論性別或種族。

2.  **Context: Social/Gender Equality**
    *   **English:** Many organizations are working towards greater **gender parity** in leadership roles within the company.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多組織正致力於在公司內部的領導職位上實現更大的性別平等。

3.  **Context: Sports/Competition**
    *   **English:** After years of dominance by a few teams, the new rules have created greater **competitive parity** in the league.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在少數幾支球隊主導多年後，新規則為聯盟帶來了更強的競技實力均等。

4.  **Context: Technology/Features**
    *   **English:** The development team is working hard to bring the mobile app to **feature parity** with the desktop version.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 開發團隊正努力讓手機應用程式的功能與桌面版本達到一致（功能對等）。

5.  **Context: International Relations/Military**
    *   **English:** The Cold War era was characterized by a delicate balance of nuclear **parity** between the superpowers.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 冷戰時期的一個特點是超級大國之間微妙的核子均勢。

6.  **Context: Education/Opportunity**
    *   **English:** Ensuring educational **parity** across all socio-economic strata remains a significant challenge for the government.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 確保所有社會經濟階層都能享有教育機會均等，仍然是政府的一項重大的挑戰。

7.  **Context: Prices/Markets**
    *   **English:** The company struggled to maintain **price parity** between its online store and physical retail locations.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該公司難以維持其線上商店和實體零售店之間的價格一致性（價格對等）。

---

Understanding "parity" and its common uses will definitely help you navigate complex GRE passages and choose the correct vocabulary in text completion questions. Good luck with your studies!

## placate

That's a great word for the GRE! Let's break down "placate."

---

### Meaning of "Placate"

**Placate** (verb) means to make someone less angry, hostile, or upset, often by doing something to please them or by making concessions. It implies an attempt to calm or soothe someone who is already agitated or resentful.

Think of it as:
*   To appease
*   To mollify
*   To pacify
*   To conciliate

**Key nuance:** The goal of placating is to alleviate *existing* anger or displeasure, not necessarily to resolve the root cause of the issue, but to stop the immediate negative reaction.

---

### Common Collocations for "Placate"

Common collocations for "placate" typically involve:

1.  **The object being placated:**
    *   **Placate an angry customer/client/employee:** (Most common)
    *   **Placate the public/critics/voters:** (In political or media contexts)
    *   **Placate a child/someone's feelings:** (In personal contexts)
    *   **Placate a disgruntled group/faction/nation:** (In diplomatic or organizational contexts)

2.  **The action taken to placate:**
    *   **Attempt to placate / Try to placate**
    *   **Seek to placate**
    *   **Efforts to placate**
    *   **Placate with [something]:** (e.g., *placate with concessions, placate with an apology, placate with a refund*)

---

### Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are several examples to illustrate its use, along with Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Business/Customer Service**
    *   **English:** The manager tried to **placate** the irate customer with a full refund and a discount voucher.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經理試圖用全額退款和折扣券來**安撫**這位憤怒的顧客。
    *   *(Explanation: The manager's actions are aimed at calming the customer's anger.)*

2.  **Context: Politics/Social Issues**
    *   **English:** In an effort to **placate** the protesting farmers, the government promised new subsidies.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 為了**平息**抗議農民的不滿，政府承諾提供新的補貼。
    *   *(Explanation: The government made a concession to soothe the farmers' discontent.)*

3.  **Context: Personal/Family Relations**
    *   **English:** She baked his favorite cake to **placate** him after their argument.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 爭吵過後，她烤了他最喜歡的蛋糕來**安撫**他。
    *   *(Explanation: The cake was a gesture to make him less upset.)*

4.  **Context: Workplace/Management**
    *   **English:** To **placate** the frustrated employees, the company announced more flexible working hours.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 為了**撫慰**感到沮喪的員工，公司宣布了更彈性的工時。
    *   *(Explanation: The company took action to alleviate the employees' frustration.)*

5.  **Context: Media/Public Relations**
    *   **English:** The celebrity issued a public apology to **placate** his critics after the controversial remarks.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位名人在發表爭議言論後，發布了公開道歉聲明，以**平息**批評者的怒氣。
    *   *(Explanation: The apology was an attempt to quiet down the negative public reaction.)*

6.  **Context: Diplomacy/International Relations**
    *   **English:** The diplomat worked tirelessly to **placate** the opposing factions and prevent further conflict.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位外交官不懈努力，**安撫**對立派系，以防止進一步的衝突。
    *   *(Explanation: The diplomat's efforts were directed at calming tensions between groups.)*

---

Keep practicing these words in context, and you'll master them for the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## aesthetic

"Aesthetic" is a fantastic word for the GRE because it has both an adjective and a noun form, and its meaning can be nuanced.

---

### Meaning of "Aesthetic"

**1. As an Adjective:**

*   **Primary Meaning:** Concerned with beauty or the appreciation of beauty. It relates to the visual or sensory pleasure derived from something.
    *   **Keywords:** Beautiful, artistic, visually pleasing, tasteful, graceful, pertaining to beauty or art.
*   **Secondary Meaning (more philosophical):** Pertaining to aesthetics (the branch of philosophy dealing with art, beauty, and taste).

**2. As a Noun:**

*   **Primary Meaning (countable):** A set of principles underlying the work of a particular artist or artistic movement; a particular taste for or approach to what is beautiful. It refers to the overall style, look, or "vibe" of something, often reflecting a specific philosophy or set of preferences.
    *   **Keywords:** Style, look, philosophy, design principles, artistic vision, vibe.
*   **Secondary Meaning (uncountable, usually "aesthetics"):** The branch of philosophy that deals with the principles of beauty and artistic taste. (Less common for the singular "an aesthetic" in this sense).

**In simpler terms:** "Aesthetic" is about how things *look and feel* in terms of beauty or art. It can describe something that *is* beautiful (adjective), or it can refer to a specific *style or philosophy* of beauty (noun).

---

### Common Collocations for "Aesthetic"

**As an Adjective:**

*   **aesthetic appeal:** How attractive something is to the senses.
*   **aesthetic value:** The worth something has purely based on its beauty or artistic quality.
*   **aesthetic design:** A design focused on visual appeal.
*   **aesthetic quality:** The standard of beauty or art in something.
*   **aesthetic considerations:** Factors related to beauty or visual appeal.
*   **aesthetic experience:** The feeling or pleasure derived from observing something beautiful.
*   **aesthetic principles:** The rules or theories governing beauty in art or design.
*   **aesthetic judgment:** An opinion based on artistic or visual merit.
*   **aesthetic sense/taste:** A person's ability to appreciate beauty.
*   **aesthetically pleasing:** Visually attractive.
*   **aesthetically driven:** Motivated by concerns for beauty or appearance.

**As a Noun:**

*   **minimalist aesthetic:** A style characterized by simplicity and lack of ornamentation.
*   **modern aesthetic:** A contemporary style or approach to design/art.
*   **vintage aesthetic:** A style that evokes a past era.
*   **industrial aesthetic:** A style inspired by factory or warehouse designs.
*   **rustic aesthetic:** A style that is simple, natural, and often rural.
*   **define an aesthetic:** To establish or describe a particular style.
*   **embrace an aesthetic:** To adopt a particular style or philosophy.
*   **maintain an aesthetic:** To keep a consistent style.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples showcasing the different uses of "aesthetic," with Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Visual Appeal/Design (Adjective)**
    *   **English:** The architect prioritized both functionality and **aesthetic appeal** in her latest building design.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 建築師在她最新的建築設計中，同時優先考慮了功能性與**美學吸引力**。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "aesthetic appeal" refers to how visually pleasing or beautiful the building looks.

2.  **Context: Artistic Quality/Standard (Adjective)**
    *   **English:** The film was lauded for its stunning cinematography and unique **aesthetic quality**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這部電影因其令人驚嘆的攝影技術和獨特的**美學品質**而備受讚譽。
    *   *Explanation:* "Aesthetic quality" describes the high standard of artistry and beauty in the film's visual presentation.

3.  **Context: Philosophical Principles (Adjective)**
    *   **English:** His lecture delved into the **aesthetic principles** underlying classical Greek sculpture.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他的講座深入探討了古典希臘雕塑所蘊含的**美學原則**。
    *   *Explanation:* "Aesthetic principles" refers to the theories and rules that govern beauty and art, particularly in a philosophical sense.

4.  **Context: A Specific Style (Noun - Countable)**
    *   **English:** They chose a minimalist **aesthetic** for their new apartment, favoring clean lines and neutral colors.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他們為新公寓選擇了極簡主義的**美學風格**，偏愛簡潔的線條和中性色調。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "a minimalist aesthetic" refers to a distinct style or design philosophy they adopted for their home.

5.  **Context: Broader Cultural Style/Taste (Noun - Countable)**
    *   **English:** The Victorian **aesthetic**, with its elaborate ornamentation and dark wood, reflects the era's social values.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 維多利亞時代的**美學風格**，以其精緻的裝飾和深色木材為特徵，反映了那個時代的社會價值觀。
    *   *Explanation:* "The Victorian aesthetic" describes the characteristic overall look and feel, and the underlying taste of that historical period.

6.  **Context: Personal Appreciation of Beauty (Adjective used with Noun)**
    *   **English:** Her refined **aesthetic sense** allows her to appreciate both traditional and modern **aesthetic designs**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她精緻的**美學品味**使她能夠欣賞傳統和現代的**美學設計**。
    *   *Explanation:* "Aesthetic sense" refers to her personal ability to discern and enjoy beauty, while "aesthetic designs" are designs focused on beauty.

---

Mastering "aesthetic" means understanding its flexibility to describe both the *quality* of beauty and a specific *style* of beauty. Good luck with your GRE!

## devious

Let's break down the word "devious" for your GRE studies.

---

### **Devious**

**Meaning:**

"Devious" primarily has two main senses, though the first is far more common in the context of the GRE and general usage:

1.  **Indirect, Dishonest, or Manipulative:** This is the most common and important meaning. It describes someone or something characterized by acting in a cunning, tricky, or underhanded way, often to deceive, gain an advantage, or achieve a goal by not being straightforward. It implies a lack of sincerity and often a hidden, potentially malicious, agenda.
    *   **Keywords:** cunning, sly, crafty, artful, wily, tricky, dishonest, deceitful, treacherous, manipulative, underhanded, indirect, circuitous.
    *   **Antonyms:** straightforward, honest, frank, direct, candid, sincere.

2.  **Winding or Indirect (Literal Sense):** Less common, but can describe a physical path or route that is not direct, but instead twists and turns.
    *   **Keywords:** winding, meandering, circuitous, rambling, indirect.

---

**Common Collocations:**

When studying for the GRE, understanding common collocations helps you use the word naturally and recognize its typical contexts.

*   **Devious plan/scheme/plot:** A dishonest or manipulative strategy.
*   **Devious tactics/methods:** Indirect and often unethical ways of doing something.
*   **Devious mind/character/person:** Someone who thinks or acts in a cunning, tricky, or dishonest way.
*   **Devious route/path:** (Literal sense) A winding, indirect way.

---

**Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts:**

Here are examples illustrating both senses of "devious" with Traditional Chinese translations:

**Context 1: Describing a manipulative person or their actions**
*   **English:** The politician was known for his **devious** tactics, always finding indirect ways to undermine his opponents without appearing overtly aggressive.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位政治人物以其**狡詐的**手段聞名，總是能找到間接的方式來暗中破壞對手，而表面上卻不顯得過於激進。

**Context 2: Referring to a dishonest plan or strategy**
*   **English:** She hatched a **devious** plan to discredit her rival, subtly spreading rumors rather than confronting her directly.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 她策劃了一個**陰險的**計畫來詆毀她的競爭對手，巧妙地散佈謠言而非直接與之對峙。

**Context 3: Describing a person's cunning nature**
*   **English:** His **devious** mind was always calculating how to gain an advantage, even if it meant misleading others.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 他那**詭計多端**的心思總是盤算著如何佔據上風，即使這意味著要誤導他人。

**Context 4: Using the literal sense for a physical path**
*   **English:** The hikers followed a **devious** trail through the dense forest, constantly turning and twisting to avoid obstacles.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 遠足者們沿著一條**崎嶇彎曲的**小徑穿過茂密的森林，不斷地轉彎和迂迴以避開障礙物。

**Context 5: Describing a complex and tricky situation/process**
*   **English:** Navigating the complex legal system can be a **devious** process for those unfamiliar with its intricacies.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 對於不熟悉其複雜性的外行人來說，在複雜的法律體系中穿梭可能是一個**錯綜複雜且難以應付的**過程。

---

**GRE Tip:**
On the GRE, "devious" almost always carries a negative connotation, implying dishonesty, manipulation, or a lack of straightforwardness. It's often used to describe characters, plans, or methods that are morally questionable.

## sensuous

Let's break down the word "sensuous" for your GRE preparation.

---

### Understanding "Sensuous"

**Meaning:**
"Sensuous" describes something that appeals to or gratifies the physical senses (sight, sound, smell, taste, touch), especially in an aesthetically pleasing or delightful way. It relates to experiencing the world through the senses, often evoking pleasure, beauty, or richness, but without a strong connotation of sexuality or lust.

**Key Distinction (Important for GRE!):**
It's crucial to differentiate "sensuous" from "sensual":

*   **Sensuous:** Primarily refers to the *aesthetic* pleasure derived from the senses – the beauty of a painting, the rich aroma of coffee, the smooth texture of silk, the vibrant colors of a sunset, the intricate flavors of a dish. It's often used in a more artistic, appreciative, or intellectual context.
*   **Sensual:** More often implies physical, bodily pleasures, especially those that are overtly sexual or involve gratification of physical appetites.

Think of it this way: A beautifully prepared meal with vibrant colors and aromatic spices is **sensuous**. The feeling of soft sheets against your skin might be **sensuous**. The experience of physical intimacy is **sensual**.

---

### Common Collocations for "Sensuous"

"Sensuous" frequently combines with words that relate to experiences, art, nature, or specific sensory attributes:

*   **Sensuous pleasure/delight:** The enjoyable feeling derived from the senses.
*   **Sensuous experience:** An event or situation that strongly engages the senses.
*   **Sensuous beauty:** Beauty that is perceived and appreciated through the senses.
*   **Sensuous colors/textures/aromas/flavors/sounds:** Describing specific sensory qualities that are pleasing.
*   **Sensuous art/music/dance:** Works of art that deeply engage the viewer's or listener's senses.
*   **Sensuous prose/description:** Writing that vividly describes sensory details.
*   **Sensuous curves/lines:** Often used to describe aesthetically pleasing shapes, especially in art or architecture.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences with their Traditional Chinese translations:

**1. Context: Art/Visuals (Emphasizing beauty and aesthetics)**
*   **English:** The artist's use of rich, vibrant hues created a truly **sensuous painting** that invited viewers to linger and admire its depth.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位藝術家運用豐富、鮮豔的色彩，創作出真正**訴諸感官的畫作**，讓觀賞者流連忘返，細細品味其深度。

**2. Context: Nature/Environment (Describing an immersive sensory experience)**
*   **English:** Walking through the botanical garden was a **sensuous experience**, with the intoxicating aroma of jasmine and the soft rustle of leaves underfoot.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 漫步在植物園是一次**感官愉悅的體驗**，有著令人陶醉的茉莉花香和腳下輕柔的樹葉沙沙聲。

**3. Context: Food/Taste (Focusing on the pleasure of flavor and texture)**
*   **English:** The chef crafted a truly **sensuous dish**, combining exotic spices and complex textures that delighted the palate with every bite.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位廚師烹製出真正**訴諸感官的菜餚**，結合異國香料和多樣口感，讓味蕾在每一口都感到無比愉悅。

**4. Context: Music/Sound (Highlighting an enveloping auditory experience)**
*   **English:** The jazz performance was a **sensuous blend** of smooth saxophone melodies and deep bass lines, enveloping the audience in its warm, rich sound.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 這場爵士樂表演是柔和薩克斯風旋律和低沉貝斯線條的**感官享受結合**，將觀眾包裹在其溫暖、豐富的音色之中。

**5. Context: Literature/Prose (Describing vivid and immersive writing)**
*   **English:** Her prose was celebrated for its **sensuous descriptions** of nature, making readers feel as if they were truly present in the depicted landscapes.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 她的散文以其**訴諸感官的自然描寫**而聞名，讓讀者感覺彷彿身臨其境於筆下的景致之中。

**6. Context: Design/Texture (Focusing on tactile and visual appeal)**
*   **English:** The interior designer chose materials with a **sensuous texture**, like velvet and polished wood, to create a luxurious and inviting space.
*   **Traditional Chinese:** 室內設計師選用了像天鵝絨和拋光木材等**觸感舒適的材質**，以打造一個奢華而溫馨的空間。

---

**GRE Tip:** When you encounter "sensuous" on the GRE, always remember its emphasis on *aesthetic* pleasure derived from the senses, rather than crude or overtly sexual gratification. This distinction is often tested. Practice identifying contexts where this word fits best.

## plausible

Okay, let's break down the word "plausible" for your GRE studies!

---

### Meaning of "Plausible"

**Plausible** (adjective) means that something seems **reasonable, believable, or likely to be true**, even if it hasn't been definitively proven. It suggests that an argument, statement, explanation, or scenario has a high degree of superficial credibility or probability.

Key aspects of "plausible":
*   **Credibility:** It has the appearance of being true or valid.
*   **Likelihood:** It could realistically happen or be the case.
*   **Not necessarily true:** Something can be plausible without actually *being* true. It just *seems* to make sense or fit the facts.
*   **Synonyms:** believable, credible, probable, conceivable, reasonable, tenable, likely, seeming true.
*   **Antonym:** implausible, unbelievable, improbable, far-fetched.

---

### Common Collocations of "Plausible"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these will help you understand and use "plausible" naturally.

**Nouns that are often plausible:**
*   **plausible explanation:** (合理的解釋)
*   **plausible theory:** (可信的理論)
*   **plausible argument:** (有道理的論點)
*   **plausible reason:** (合理的理由)
*   **plausible story:** (可信的故事)
*   **plausible excuse:** (貌似合理的藉口)
*   **plausible scenario:** (可想像的情境)
*   **plausible alternative:** (看似可行的替代方案)
*   **plausible outcome:** (合理的結果)
*   **plausible hypothesis:** (可信的假設)
*   **plausible claim:** (看似合理的說法)
*   **plausible solution:** (可行的解決方案)

**Adverbs that often modify "plausible":**
*   **highly plausible:** (非常可信的)
*   **entirely plausible:** (完全可信的)
*   **perfectly plausible:** (非常合理的)
*   **quite plausible:** (相當可信的)
*   **barely plausible:** (勉強可信的)
*   **not at all plausible:** (完全不可信的)

**Verbs often used with "plausible":**
*   **seem plausible:** (看起來合理)
*   **sound plausible:** (聽起來合理)
*   **appear plausible:** (似乎合理)
*   **find something plausible:** (認為某事合理)
*   **make something plausible:** (讓某事變得合理)

---

### Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are examples showcasing "plausible" in different situations, with Traditional Chinese translations.

1.  **Context: Everyday conversation / Justification**
    *   **English:** His excuse for being late sounded **plausible**, but I still had my doubts.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他遲到的藉口聽起來**很合理**，但我還是有些懷疑。
    *   *Explanation:* The excuse *seemed* believable on the surface, even if it wasn't necessarily true.

2.  **Context: Academic / Scientific Research**
    *   **English:** The new scientific theory provides a **plausible explanation** for the observed phenomena, though more research is needed to confirm it.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這項新科學理論為觀察到的現象提供了一個**合理的解釋**，儘管還需要更多研究來證實。
    *   *Explanation:* The explanation is credible and makes sense given the current data, even if it's not yet proven fact.

3.  **Context: Legal / Investigation**
    *   **English:** The detective found the suspect's alibi **plausible**, but decided to verify it with eyewitnesses.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 偵探認為嫌疑犯的不在場證明**可信**，但還是決定向目擊者求證。
    *   *Explanation:* The alibi seemed believable and consistent with the circumstances, leading the detective to consider it seriously.

4.  **Context: Business / Planning**
    *   **English:** Developing a sustainable energy source is a **plausible long-term solution** for the company's energy needs.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 開發永續能源是公司能源需求的一個**合理且可行的長期解決方案**。
    *   *Explanation:* The solution is realistic and appears to be a viable option for the future.

5.  **Context: Journalism / Critical Analysis**
    *   **English:** While the initial reports of the incident seemed **plausible**, further investigation revealed several inconsistencies.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管事件的初步報導**看似真實**，但進一步的調查揭示了多處不一致。
    *   *Explanation:* The reports initially appeared credible and believable, but deeper scrutiny showed they might not be accurate.

6.  **Context: Creative Writing / Storytelling**
    *   **English:** The plot twist was surprising, yet entirely **plausible** within the fantastical rules of the story's world.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 劇情轉折出人意料，但在那個奇幻世界的設定中卻**完全合情合理**。
    *   *Explanation:* Even though it was unexpected, the twist made sense and was consistent with the established logic of the fictional universe.

---

Remember for GRE: "plausible" often appears in reading comprehension passages or sentence equivalence questions. Focus on its nuance: it's about what *seems* true or *could* be true, rather than what *is* true. Good luck with your studies!

## predilection

Okay, let's break down "predilection" for your GRE prep!

---

## Predilection (n.)

### Meaning

**Predilection** refers to a **strong liking, preference, or partiality for something**. It suggests a natural or habitual tendency to favor one thing over others. It's more than just a casual like; it implies a deeper, often long-standing, inclination.

*   **Etymology Tip:** The "pre-" prefix means "before" and "dilection" comes from Latin *dilectio*, meaning "love" or "delight." So, it's like a "pre-existing love" or a "love beforehand."

**Synonyms (useful for GRE vocabulary):**
*   Preference
*   Partiality
*   Fondness
*   Penchant
*   Propensity
*   Inclination
*   Predisposition
*   Bias

**Antonyms:**
*   Aversion
*   Dislike
*   Disinclination
*   Antipathy
*   Repugnance

---

### Common Collocations

"Predilection" most commonly takes the preposition **"for"**:

*   **Predilection for:** This is the most frequent and natural pairing.
    *   *Example:* a predilection for classical music, a predilection for sweet foods, a predilection for solitude.

Other less common but possible collocations:

*   **Strong/Marked/Clear/Distinct/Pronounced predilection:** Adjectives that emphasize the intensity of the preference.
*   **Innate/Natural/Inherent predilection:** Adjectives suggesting the preference is inborn or deeply ingrained.
*   **Have/Show/Exhibit/Develop a predilection:** Verbs used to describe the possession or manifestation of a preference.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples with Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Hobbies & Interests**
    *   **English:** Despite growing up with pop music, she always showed a **strong predilection for** classical compositions and opera.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管她聽流行音樂長大，但她一直對古典樂曲和歌劇表現出**強烈的偏愛**。

2.  **Context: Professional/Academic**
    *   **English:** The renowned historian had a distinct **predilection for** uncovering obscure facts about the Roman Empire, often spending weeks in dusty archives.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位著名的歷史學家對發掘羅馬帝國鮮為人知的事實有著獨特的**偏好**，他經常在佈滿灰塵的檔案館裡待上數週。

3.  **Context: Personality & Behavior**
    *   **English:** His curious **predilection for** collecting antique maps meant his study was always overflowing with rolled-up scrolls and brittle charts.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他對收藏古董地圖有著**奇特的偏好**，這使得他的書房裡總是堆滿了捲起的捲軸和易碎的圖表。

4.  **Context: Abstract/Literary Style**
    *   **English:** The author's **predilection for** intricate metaphors and lengthy descriptions sometimes made his novels challenging for the casual reader.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位作者對複雜的隱喻和冗長的描述有著**偏愛**，有時這使得他的小說對普通讀者來說頗具挑戰性。

5.  **Context: Market/Consumer Behavior**
    *   **English:** Market research revealed a growing consumer **predilection for** sustainable and ethically sourced products, prompting many companies to re-evaluate their supply chains.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 市場研究顯示，消費者對永續和道德採購的產品日益**偏好**，促使許多公司重新評估其供應鏈。

---

Keep practicing your vocabulary in context, and you'll master these nuances for the GRE! Good luck!

## sedulous

That's an excellent word to learn for the GRE! "Sedulous" is a valuable vocabulary item, conveying a nuanced meaning beyond just "hardworking."

---

### Meaning of "Sedulous"

**Sedulous** (adjective) means showing **dedication, diligence, and painstaking effort** in a task or pursuit. It implies not just hard work, but also **persistence, thoroughness, and careful attention to detail**. Someone who is sedulous is not easily deterred and applies themselves with great care and commitment.

*   **Synonyms:** diligent, assiduous, painstaking, meticulous, thorough, industrious, indefatigable, laborious.
*   **Antonyms:** lazy, careless, negligent, desultory, lackadaisical.

**Think of it this way:** A student who merely *studies* is hardworking. A student who engages in *sedulous study* not only puts in long hours but also meticulously reviews notes, cross-references textbooks, practices every type of problem, and doesn't stop until they've mastered the material.

---

### Common Collocations for "Sedulous"

"Sedulous" often describes the *nature* of an effort, a person, or an action.

*   **Describing effort/action:**
    *   **Sedulous effort / work / study:** *e.g., "His sedulous effort paid off."*
    *   **Sedulous attention / care:** *e.g., "She paid sedulous attention to the tiny details."*
    *   **Sedulous pursuit:** *e.g., "The sedulous pursuit of knowledge."*
    *   **Sedulous research / analysis:** *e.g., "Years of sedulous research."*
    *   **Sedulous craftsmanship:** *e.g., "The sedulous craftsmanship of the artisan."*

*   **Describing a person:**
