    *   **Sedulous student / scholar / researcher / apprentice:** *e.g., "A sedulous student always asks questions."*
    *   **Sedulous individual / person:** *e.g., "He is a truly sedulous individual."*

*   **Adverbial form ("sedulously"):**
    *   **Sedulously applied:** *e.g., "He sedulously applied himself to the task."*
    *   **Sedulously maintained / cultivated:** *e.g., "The garden was sedulously maintained."*
    *   **Sedulously pursue:** *e.g., "They sedulously pursued their goals."*

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples with Traditional Chinese translations to help solidify your understanding:

1.  **Context: Academic / Study (GRE-relevant!)**
    *   **English:** To achieve a high score on the GRE, one must engage in **sedulous study** over many months, reviewing concepts and practicing problems relentlessly.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 要獲得GRE高分，必須數月如一日地進行**孜孜不倦的學習**，不斷複習概念並堅持不懈地練習題目。

2.  **Context: Professional / Research**
    *   **English:** Her **sedulous research** into ancient manuscripts uncovered previously unknown historical facts, leading to a groundbreaking new theory.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她對古代手稿**鍥而不捨的研究**發掘出先前未知的歷史事實，最終導向了一項開創性的新理論。

3.  **Context: Craft / Skill / Artistry**
    *   **English:** The intricate carving on the antique furniture was a testament to the artisan's **sedulous craftsmanship** and meticulous attention to detail.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 古董家具上錯綜複雜的雕刻，證明了這位工匠**精益求精、一絲不苟的技藝**和對細節的嚴謹關注。

4.  **Context: Personal Character Trait**
    *   **English:** It was his **sedulous nature**, always striving for perfection and refusing to cut corners, that eventually led to his success in the highly competitive field.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 正是他**勤奮嚴謹、力求完美**的性格，從不敷衍了事，最終使他在競爭激烈的領域中取得了成功。

5.  **Context: Maintenance / Cultivation (using the adverb "sedulously")**
    *   **English:** The beautiful public garden, **sedulously maintained** by a team of dedicated volunteers, bloomed vibrantly year after year.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這座美麗的公共花園在一群熱心志工的**悉心照料下**，年復一年地欣欣向榮。

6.  **Context: Goal Pursuit**
    *   **English:** Despite numerous setbacks, she **sedulously pursued** her dream of becoming a doctor, eventually graduating at the top of her class.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管遭遇了無數挫折，她仍**孜孜不倦地追求**成為醫生的夢想，最終以班級第一的成績畢業。

---

By understanding "sedulous" and its common collocations, you'll be well-equipped to tackle it on the GRE, whether in Sentence Equivalence, Text Completion, or Reading Comprehension. Keep up the great work – your dedication to vocabulary is truly sedulous!

## implicit

Let's break down the word "implicit" for your GRE preparation. Understanding its nuances and common uses is key.

---

### Understanding "Implicit"

**Meaning:**

"Implicit" means **suggested or understood without being directly stated.** It refers to something that is hinted at, inherent, or absolutely understood, even if it's not explicitly communicated.

It is the opposite of **"explicit,"** which means stated clearly and in detail, leaving no room for confusion or doubt.

**Etymology Note:** The word "implicit" comes from Latin "implicitus," meaning "entangled, involved," from "implicare" ("to fold into, involve"). Think of something "folded into" the meaning, rather than laid out flat.

---

### Common Collocations of "Implicit"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these will help you recognize and use "implicit" naturally.

1.  **Implicit trust:** Absolute or unquestioning trust.
2.  **Implicit agreement/consent:** An understanding or permission that is not formally stated but is understood or implied by actions or silence.
3.  **Implicit assumption/belief:** An underlying idea or conviction that is taken for granted without being consciously acknowledged or stated.
4.  **Implicit meaning/message:** The unstated or underlying significance of something.
5.  **Implicit threat:** A warning or danger that is suggested rather than directly expressed.
6.  **Implicit bias:** Unconscious attitudes or stereotypes that affect our understanding, actions, and decisions.
7.  **Implicit knowledge:** Knowledge that is not consciously accessible or easily articulated, often gained through experience (e.g., how to ride a bike).
8.  **Implicit criticism/approval:** Judgment or acceptance that is suggested rather than stated outright.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples of "implicit" in different situations, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Relationships/Trust**
    *   **English:** She had **implicit trust** in her colleague's judgment, even when he made unconventional decisions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她對同事的判斷有著**絕對的信任**，即使他做出非常規的決定。
        *   *(Note: "絕對的信任" (juéduì de xìnrèn) is a very common and natural translation for "implicit trust" in this context, emphasizing the absolute nature.)*

2.  **Context: Communication/Meaning**
    *   **English:** The poem's **implicit meaning** was a critique of consumerism, though it never directly mentioned the word.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這首詩的**隱含意義**是對消費主義的批判，儘管它從未直接提及這個詞。

3.  **Context: Agreements/Social Conduct**
    *   **English:** By staying silent, he gave his **implicit consent** to the plan.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他保持沉默，就等於**默許了**這個計畫。
        *   *(Note: "默許" (mòxǔ) is a perfect and concise translation for "implicit consent" or "tacit approval.")*

4.  **Context: Psychology/Social Issues**
    *   **English:** Training programs aim to make people aware of their **implicit biases** in hiring.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 培訓計畫旨在讓人們意識到他們在招聘中存在的**內隱偏見**。
        *   *(Note: "內隱偏見" (nèiyǐn piānjiàn) is the standard psychological term for "implicit bias.")*

5.  **Context: Logic/Assumptions**
    *   **English:** The entire argument rests on the **implicit assumption** that all people value economic growth above environmental protection.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 整個論點都基於一個**不言而喻的假設**，即所有人都將經濟成長置於環境保護之上。
        *   *(Note: "不言而喻的假設" (bù yán ér yù de jiǎshè) means "a self-evident or unspoken assumption.")*

6.  **Context: Power Dynamics/Warning**
    *   **English:** There was an **implicit threat** in his offer of "help" that suggested dire consequences if refused.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他提出「幫助」時帶有**隱性威脅**，暗示若拒絕將會有嚴重後果。
        *   *(Note: "隱性威脅" (yǐnxìng wēixié) is common for "implicit threat" or "covert threat.")*

7.  **Context: Skills/Learning**
    *   **English:** Much of an experienced craftsman's skill comes from **implicit knowledge** gained through years of practice, rather than explicit instructions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經驗豐富的工匠大部分技能來自於經年累月練習所獲得的**內隱知識**，而非明確的指示。
        *   *(Note: "內隱知識" (nèiyǐn zhīshì) is the standard term for "implicit knowledge" in fields like psychology or education.)*

---

By studying these examples and collocations, you'll be well-prepared to understand "implicit" on the GRE and beyond!

## panacea

Okay, let's break down the word "panacea" for your GRE studies.

---

### Understanding "Panacea"

*   **Meaning:** A panacea (pronounced /pænəˈsiːə/) is **a remedy for all ills or difficulties; a universal cure.**
    *   It comes from Greek roots: "pan-" meaning "all" and "akos" meaning "cure."
    *   **Key Nuance:** While it refers to a *supposed* universal solution, it's almost always used in a context that implies skepticism, doubt, or a rejection of the idea that such a simple, all-encompassing solution actually exists. It suggests an oversimplified or unrealistic expectation.

*   **Synonyms:** Universal remedy, cure-all, magic bullet, elixir, nostrum (often with a negative connotation, implying a quack remedy).

---

### Common Collocation

The most common and important collocation for "panacea" is:

*   **"Not a panacea"** or **"no panacea"**: This highlights the skeptical or critical way the word is almost always used. It emphasizes that something is *not* the perfect, all-encompassing solution it might be presented as.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples showcasing "panacea" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Technology/Social Issues**
    *   **English:** While technology offers many benefits, it's certainly **not a panacea** for all of society's problems, especially deep-seated economic inequality.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管科技帶來許多好處，但它絕不是解決所有社會問題的**萬靈丹**，尤其是根深蒂固的經濟不平等。
    *   *Explanation:* This sentence uses "not a panacea" to argue against the idea that technology alone can solve complex societal issues.

2.  **Context: Government Policy/Economics**
    *   **English:** The new tax policy was hailed by some as a **panacea** for the nation's financial woes, but critics warned it would create more problems than it solved.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 新的稅收政策被一些人譽為解決國家財政困境的**萬靈丹**，但批評者警告稱它會製造更多問題。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, a policy is *presented* as a panacea, but the subsequent "but critics warned" immediately introduces the skeptical counter-argument, aligning with the word's typical usage.

3.  **Context: Education/Learning**
    *   **English:** There's **no single teaching method** that serves as a **panacea** for all learning difficulties; effective education requires diverse approaches.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 沒有任何單一的教學方法能作為解決所有學習困難的**萬靈丹**；有效的教育需要多元的方法。
    *   *Explanation:* This example clarifies that a one-size-fits-all approach is unrealistic in education.

4.  **Context: Health/Well-being (Metaphorical)**
    *   **English:** Many people seek a quick fix, believing that a single diet or exercise routine will be a **panacea** for all their health concerns, but true well-being is multifaceted.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多人尋求速效解決方案，認為單一的飲食或運動習慣會是解決所有健康問題的**萬靈丹**，但真正的健康是多方面的。
    *   *Explanation:* This uses "panacea" to describe the misguided belief that a simple solution can address complex health issues.

5.  **Context: Business/Management**
    *   **English:** Implementing agile methodologies isn't a **panacea** for every business challenge; its effectiveness depends heavily on company culture and specific project needs.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 實施敏捷方法並非解決所有業務挑戰的**萬靈丹**；其有效性高度取決於公司文化和特定的專案需求。
    *   *Explanation:* In a business context, it highlights that a popular strategy might not be universally applicable or effective.

---

**Key Takeaway for GRE:** When you see "panacea" on the GRE, it's almost always in a context where someone is either arguing *against* the idea of a simple, universal solution, or describing someone's *mistaken belief* that such a solution exists. It rarely describes something that *is* genuinely effective for everything.

## malevolent

Okay, let's break down the word "malevolent" for your GRE prep!

---

### Understanding "Malevolent"

**Meaning:**
"Malevolent" (pronounced /məˈlɛvələnt/) is an adjective that describes something or someone having or showing a wish to do evil to others. It implies a malicious or hostile intent.

*   **Etymology Tip (helpful for GRE vocabulary):** The word comes from Latin:
    *   *malus* meaning "bad" or "evil"
    *   *velle* meaning "to wish"
    *   So, literally, "wishing bad/evil."

**Synonyms:** malicious, evil, wicked, hostile, sinister, baleful, ill-willed.
**Antonyms:** benevolent, benign, kind, good-willed, compassionate.

---

### Common Collocations for "Malevolent"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these can help you use the word naturally and effectively.

*   **Malevolent spirit/force/entity:** An evil ghost, supernatural power, or being.
*   **Malevolent glare/look/stare:** A hostile, menacing, or evil expression in someone's eyes.
*   **Malevolent intent/scheme/plot:** An evil or harmful purpose or plan.
*   **Malevolent dictator/villain/figure:** A leader or character who acts with evil intent.
*   **Malevolent influence/presence:** A harmful or sinister effect or atmosphere.
*   **Malevolent act/action:** A deed carried out with harmful or evil intentions.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts with Traditional Chinese Translations

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its usage:

1.  **Context: Character/Supernatural Entity**
    *   **English:** The villagers believed a **malevolent spirit** haunted the old mansion, causing strange occurrences.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 村民們相信一個**惡意的靈魂**纏繞著那座老宅邸，導致了奇怪的事件。
    *   *(Pinyin: Cūnmínmen xiāngxìn yī ge èyì de línghún chánrào zhe nà zuò lǎo zháidǐ, dǎozhì le qíguài de shìjiàn.)*

2.  **Context: Human Behavior/Expression**
    *   **English:** He cast a **malevolent glare** at his rival across the table, a clear sign of his animosity.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他朝著桌子對面的競爭對手投射出一個**惡毒的眼神**，明確地表達了他的敵意。
    *   *(Pinyin: Tā cháo zhe zhuōzi duìmiàn de jìngzhēng duìshǒu tóushè chū yī ge èdú de yǎnshén, míngquè de biǎodá le tā de díyì.)*

3.  **Context: Abstract/Intent**
    *   **English:** The villain's **malevolent intent** was clear: he wanted to destroy the entire city.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 反派的**邪惡意圖**很明顯：他想摧毀整個城市。
    *   *(Pinyin: Fǎnpài de xié'è yìtú hěn míngxiǎn: tā xiǎng cuīhuǐ zhěnggè chéngshì.)*

4.  **Context: Animal/Figurative**
    *   **English:** The ancient tales spoke of a dragon with **malevolent scales** that brought ruin to any kingdom it flew over.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 古老的傳說講述了一條擁有**惡煞般鱗片**的龍，牠飛過任何王國都會帶來毀滅。
    *   *(Pinyin: Gǔlǎo de chuánshuō jiǎngshù le yī tiáo yǒngyǒu èshà bān línpiàn de lóng, tā fēiguò rènhé wángguó dōu huì dàilái huǐmiè.)*
    *   *Note: "惡煞般" (èshà bān) implies an evil or demonic quality, suitable for a fantastical context.*

5.  **Context: System/Influence**
    *   **English:** Some philosophers argue that a **malevolent force** underlies certain societal ills, driving conflict and injustice.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 一些哲學家認為，一種**惡性力量**潛藏在某些社會弊病之下，驅動著衝突和不公。
    *   *(Pinyin: Yī xiē zhéxuéjiā rènwéi, yī zhǒng èxìng lìliàng qiáncáng zài mǒuxiē shèhuì bìbìng zhī xià, qūdòng zhe chōngtú hé bùgōng.)*
    *   *Note: "惡性" (èxìng) here implies something harmful, vicious, or destructive in nature.*

---

Keep practicing these words and their contexts! Good luck with your GRE!

## conjecture

That's an excellent word to master for the GRE! "Conjecture" frequently appears in reading comprehension passages and vocabulary sections.

---

### Meaning of "Conjecture"

**Conjecture** (pronounced "kun-JEK-cher") refers to:

1.  **Noun:** An opinion or conclusion formed on the basis of **incomplete information**, **guesswork**, or **speculation**, rather than on definite proof or certainty. It's an unproven idea or theory.
    *   Think of it as an educated guess, a hypothesis that hasn't been tested or confirmed, or a speculation.

2.  **Verb:** To form an opinion or conclusion about something without complete information; to guess or speculate.

**Key Idea:** The core meaning revolves around **uncertainty** and **lack of definitive proof**.

---

### Common Collocations

Here are common words that frequently appear with "conjecture":

#### As a Noun:

*   **Pure/Mere conjecture:** Emphasizes that it's entirely speculative, with no factual basis.
    *   *Example:* "His theory was dismissed as pure conjecture."
*   **Wild conjecture:** Implies a highly improbable or unfounded guess.
    *   *Example:* "The tabloids were full of wild conjecture about the celebrity's disappearance."
*   **Based on/Built on conjecture:** Indicates that something is founded on speculation rather than fact.
    *   *Example:* "The prosecution's case was based on conjecture, not concrete evidence."
*   **Form/Make a conjecture:** The act of creating such a guess.
    *   *Example:* "Scientists often form conjectures before designing experiments to test them."
*   **Support/Refute a conjecture:** To provide evidence for or against it.
    *   *Example:* "New data might support or refute the existing conjecture."
*   **Mathematical/Scientific conjecture:** Refers to unproven statements or hypotheses in those fields.
    *   *Example:* "The Goldbach Conjecture is one of the oldest unsolved problems in number theory."
*   **Matter of conjecture:** Indicates something that is open to speculation.
    *   *Example:* "The exact timing of the ancient migration is still a matter of conjecture."

#### As a Verb:

*   **Conjecture that...:** To speculate that something is true.
    *   *Example:* "We can only conjecture that he missed the flight."
*   **Conjecture about...:** To speculate concerning a particular topic.
    *   *Example:* "They conjectured about the future of the company."
*   **Conjecture from...:** To draw a conclusion based on limited information.
    *   *Example:* "From the faint tracks, he conjectured that a large animal had passed."

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples with Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: General Uncertainty/Lack of Information**
    *   **English:** "His sudden resignation from the company was a **matter of pure conjecture** among his colleagues, as no official reason was given."
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他突然從公司辭職，在同事間**純屬臆測**，因為沒有給出官方理由。
    *   *(Note: "純屬臆測" emphasizes the complete lack of basis.)*

2.  **Context: Science/Mathematics (Unproven Hypotheses)**
    *   **English:** "The **Goldbach Conjecture**, proposing that every even integer greater than 2 is the sum of two prime numbers, remains an unproven statement in number theory."
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 哥德巴赫**猜想**，即每個大於2的偶數都是兩個質數之和，在數論中仍然是一個未經證實的命題。
    *   *(Note: "猜想" is the standard term for a mathematical conjecture.)*

3.  **Context: Legal/Investigation (Lack of Evidence)**
    *   **English:** "The defense lawyer argued that the prosecution's case was **built on conjecture** and circumstantial evidence, lacking any concrete proof."
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 辯護律師爭辯說，檢方的案件**建立在推測**和間接證據之上，缺乏任何確鑿的證據。
    *   *(Note: "建立在推測" means built on speculation.)*

4.  **Context: Historical/Archaeological (Interpreting Limited Data)**
    *   **English:** "Based on the few artifacts found, archaeologists could only **conjecture about** the daily lives of the ancient civilization."
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 根據僅有的幾件出土文物，考古學家只能**推測**古文明的日常生活。
    *   *(Note: "推測" as a verb means to speculate/infer.)*

5.  **Context: Business/Economics (Future Prediction)**
    *   **English:** "The CEO admitted that predicting next quarter's sales figures was largely a **conjecture**, given the volatile market conditions."
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 執行長承認，鑑於市場波動，預測下一季度的銷售數字在很大程度上是一種**臆測**。
    *   *(Note: "臆測" here highlights the unreliability of the prediction.)*

6.  **Context: Everyday Speculation**
    *   **English:** "We can only **conjecture that** the delay was due to bad weather, as the airline provided no explanation."
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 我們只能**猜測**延誤是惡劣天氣造成的，因為航空公司沒有提供任何解釋。
    *   *(Note: "猜測" is a common way to say "guess" or "speculate" in everyday contexts.)*

7.  **Context: Debates/Discussions (Unsupported Claims)**
    *   **English:** "Without any supporting data, his claims remained nothing more than **wild conjecture**."
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 缺乏任何支持數據，他的說法僅僅是**不著邊際的推測**。
    *   *(Note: "不著邊際的推測" is a good translation for "wild conjecture.")*

---

Understanding "conjecture" is crucial for the GRE, as it tests your ability to differentiate between established facts, supported hypotheses, and mere speculation. Good luck with your studies!

## conflagration

Okay, let's break down "conflagration" for your GRE studies. It's a fantastic word to know for its power and versatility.

---

### Conflagration (noun)

**Meaning:**

*   **Primary Meaning (Literal):** A large, extensive, and destructive fire. It implies a fire that is difficult to control and causes widespread damage. Think beyond just "a fire" to something truly massive and devastating.
*   **Secondary Meaning (Metaphorical):** A large, destructive conflict or dispute. This can refer to social, political, or economic upheavals that are widespread and cause significant chaos or damage, akin to how a literal fire spreads.

**Etymology Note (for deeper understanding):** The word comes from Latin "conflagrare," meaning "to burn up" or "to be on fire." "Con-" means "together" or "thoroughly," and "flagrare" means "to burn." This helps to convey the idea of something burning completely and intensely.

---

### Common Collocations for "Conflagration":

Collocations are words that frequently go together. Knowing these helps you use the word naturally and effectively.

*   **Verbs often used with "conflagration":**
    *   **erupt into a conflagration:** (often for metaphorical use, or sudden, intense start)
    *   **spread a conflagration:** (implies it's growing)
    *   **contain a conflagration:** (to stop its spread)
    *   **battle/fight a conflagration:** (the effort to put it out)
    *   **be engulfed in a conflagration:** (what happens to something caught in it)
    *   **avert/prevent a conflagration:** (to stop it from happening)
    *   **ignite a conflagration:** (to start it, often metaphorically)

*   **Adjectives often used to describe "conflagration":**
    *   **raging conflagration:** (emphasizes intensity)
    *   **massive/widespread conflagration:** (emphasizes scale)
    *   **destructive conflagration:** (emphasizes impact)
    *   **fiery conflagration:** (a bit redundant, but emphasizes the fire aspect)
    *   **urban/forest conflagration:** (specifies the type/location)
    *   **political/social/economic conflagration:** (for metaphorical use)

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts with Traditional Chinese Translations:

Here are multiple examples to show its versatility:

1.  **Context: Literal, Large-scale Urban Fire**
    *   **English:** The Great Fire of London was not just a fire; it was a **conflagration** that reshaped the city's architecture and history.
    *   **Traditional Chinese (繁體中文):** 倫敦大火不只是一場火災；它是一場徹底改變了城市建築和歷史的**毀滅性大火（conflagration）**。

2.  **Context: Literal, Natural Disaster (Forest Fire)**
    *   **English:** The summer drought turned the vast forest into a tinderbox, setting the stage for a **conflagration** that burned for weeks.
    *   **Traditional Chinese (繁體中文):** 夏季乾旱使廣闊的森林變成火藥桶，為持續數週的**熊熊大火（conflagration）**埋下了伏筆。

3.  **Context: Metaphorical, Political/Regional Conflict**
    *   **English:** Tensions between the rival factions threatened to erupt into a regional **conflagration** if diplomacy failed.
    *   **Traditional Chinese (繁體中文):** 如果外交失敗，敵對派系之間的緊張關係恐將引發一場區域性的**衝突大火（conflagration）**。

4.  **Context: Metaphorical, Social/Economic Upheaval**
    *   **English:** The rapid spread of misinformation online created a social **conflagration**, igniting widespread panic and distrust.
    *   **Traditional Chinese (繁體中文):** 網路資訊的迅速傳播引發了一場社會性的**大動亂（conflagration）**，點燃了廣泛的恐慌和不信任感。

5.  **Context: Prevention/Containment**
    *   **English:** Firefighters worked tirelessly to contain the blaze, preventing what could have become a city-wide **conflagration**.
    *   **Traditional Chinese (繁體中文):** 消防員們不懈努力控制火勢，防止了一場可能蔓延全市的**特大火災（conflagration）**。

6.  **Context: Descriptive/Historical**
    *   **English:** Accounts from survivors described the ship's final moments as a roaring **conflagration**, visible from miles away.
    *   **Traditional Chinese (繁體中文):** 倖存者的敘述將該船最後的時刻描述為一場咆哮的**火災地獄（conflagration）**，數英里外都清晰可見。

---

By understanding "conflagration" in both its literal and metaphorical senses, and by practicing its collocations, you'll be well-prepared to encounter it on the GRE, whether in reading comprehension or sentence equivalence questions. Good luck with your studies!

## jargon

Let's break down the word "jargon" for your GRE preparation.

---

## Jargon

### Meaning of "Jargon"

**Jargon** refers to specialized words or expressions that are used by a particular profession or group and are difficult for others to understand.

*   **Key Characteristics:**
    *   **Specific to a field:** It's the unique vocabulary of a certain profession (e.g., medicine, law, technology), hobby, or social group.
    *   **Exclusionary:** Its primary effect is often to make communication efficient *within* the group but incomprehensible *to outsiders*.
    *   **Not necessarily negative:** While it can be used negatively to imply unnecessary complexity or exclusiveness, it's also a neutral term for the technical language essential to a field.
    *   **Synonyms:** Terminology, lingo, patois, argot (often implies secret or slang), specialized vocabulary.

### Common Collocations

Here are common words that frequently appear with "jargon":

**Adjectives often describing "jargon":**
*   **Technical jargon:** (most common)
*   **Legal jargon**
*   **Medical jargon**
*   **Academic jargon**
*   **Business jargon / Corporate jargon**
*   **Scientific jargon**
*   **Industry jargon**
*   **Unnecessary jargon**
*   **Obscure jargon**
*   **Confusing jargon**
*   **Complex jargon**
*   **Specialized jargon**

**Verbs often associated with "jargon":**
*   **Use jargon**
*   **Avoid jargon**
*   **Cut the jargon** (meaning to remove it)
*   **Explain jargon**
*   **Translate jargon**
*   **Full of jargon** (descriptive phrase)
*   **Sprinkle with jargon** (implying light use)

**Nouns often associated with "jargon":**
*   **Piece of jargon**
*   **Sea of jargon** (implying an overwhelming amount)
*   **Mixture of jargon**

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples showcasing "jargon" in different contexts, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: General criticism of clarity**
    *   **English:** The company's annual report was filled with so much **financial jargon** that it became incomprehensible to the average shareholder.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這家公司的年度報告充斥著大量的**金融術語**，以至於一般股東難以理解。

2.  **Context: Professional communication, with clarification**
    *   **English:** During the medical consultation, the doctor used some **medical jargon** that the patient didn't quite understand, but then quickly explained it in simpler terms.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在醫療諮詢中，醫生使用了一些病人不太理解的**醫學術語**，但隨後很快用更簡單的語言進行了解釋。

3.  **Context: Advice on effective communication**
    *   **English:** Effective communication requires presenters to avoid **technical jargon** when addressing a general audience, opting instead for clear, accessible language.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 有效的溝通要求演講者在面對一般聽眾時應避免使用**技術術語**，而選擇清晰易懂的語言。

4.  **Context: Academic writing**
    *   **English:** While academic papers often rely on **specialized jargon** to convey precise concepts, it can also create a barrier for interdisciplinary collaboration if not properly defined.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管學術論文常依賴**專業術語**來傳達精確的概念，但若未妥善定義，也可能為跨學科合作造成障礙。

5.  **Context: Humorous/Critical take on corporate language**
    *   **English:** The marketing meeting was a nightmare, packed with so much **corporate jargon** like "synergy," "paradigm shift," and "low-hanging fruit" that it felt like everyone was speaking a different language.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那場行銷會議簡直是一場惡夢，充斥著「協同作用」、「典範轉移」和「唾手可得的成果」等大量的**企業行話**，感覺每個人都在說不同的語言。

6.  **Context: Legal field**
    *   **English:** Understanding legal documents often requires familiarity with specific **legal jargon**, such as "habeas corpus" or "voir dire," which can be impenetrable to a layperson.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 理解法律文件通常需要熟悉特定的**法律術語**，例如「人身保護令」或「預選陪審員」，這對外行人來說可能難以理解。

7.  **Context: Removing jargon for clarity**
    *   **English:** The editor's main task was to strip away all the **unnecessary jargon** from the manuscript, making it more accessible to a wider readership.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 編輯的主要任務是去除手稿中所有**不必要的術語**，使其更容易被更廣泛的讀者群理解。

---

### GRE Tip:

When you encounter words like "jargon" on the GRE, pay attention to the **context**. Is it being used neutrally to describe specialized language, or negatively to imply obfuscation and lack of clarity? Understanding this nuance is key to answering reading comprehension and text completion questions correctly. Also, recognize its relationship with concepts like clarity, accessibility, and expertise.

## obscure

Let's break down the word "obscure" for your GRE preparation.

---

### Meaning of "Obscure"

"Obscure" is a versatile word that can function as both an **adjective** and a **verb**.

#### As an Adjective:

1.  **Not discovered or known about; uncertain:** This is the most common usage, referring to something that is not famous, widely recognized, or easy to find.
    *   **Synonyms:** unknown, little-known, unnoticed, forgotten, minor, remote, hidden.
    *   **Antonyms:** famous, well-known, prominent, celebrated, obvious.

2.  **Not clear, plain, or easily understood; ambiguous:** Referring to something that is difficult to comprehend or interpret.
    *   **Synonyms:** vague, ambiguous, cryptic, enigmatic, perplexing, abstruse, impenetrable, recondite.
    *   **Antonyms:** clear, plain, explicit, obvious, lucid, straightforward.

#### As a Verb:

1.  **To make something unclear, difficult to understand, or difficult to see.**
    *   **Synonyms:** hide, conceal, cloud, dim, eclipse, blur, muddle, confuse, camouflage.
    *   **Antonyms:** clarify, illuminate, reveal, expose, highlight.

---

### Common Collocations for "Obscure"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these helps you use the word naturally and recognize it in context.

#### As an Adjective:

*   **Obscure writer/artist/figure:** Someone who is not well-known or famous.
*   **Obscure village/town/corner:** A place that is remote, not well-visited, or hidden.
*   **Obscure reference/passage/text:** Something in a book, speech, or document that is difficult to understand or is not widely known.
*   **Obscure meaning/reason/motive:** Something that is unclear or mysterious.
*   **Obscure details/facts:** Minor or hard-to-find information.

#### As a Verb:

*   **Obscure the view/sun/light:** To block something from sight or to dim light.
*   **Obscure the truth/facts/issue:** To make something less clear, to hide, or to confuse.
*   **Obscure the meaning/distinction:** To make comprehension difficult.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple example sentences demonstrating "obscure" in different contexts, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Literature/Art (Adjective - not well-known)**
    *   **English:** He was an **obscure** poet whose work gained recognition only after his death.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他是一位**籍籍無名**的詩人，作品直到他去世後才獲得認可。
        *   *(Note: 籍籍無名 (jíjí wúmíng) directly translates to "without fame/reputation")*

2.  **Context: Geography/Travel (Adjective - remote place)**
    *   **English:** The ancient temple was hidden away in an **obscure** corner of the jungle.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這座古老的寺廟隱藏在叢林的一個**偏僻**角落。
        *   *(Note: 偏僻 (piānpì) means "remote" or "secluded")*

3.  **Context: Academia/Philosophy (Adjective - difficult to understand)**
    *   **English:** The philosopher's theories were so **obscure** that only a few scholars could truly grasp them.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位哲學家的理論過於**晦澀難懂**，只有少數學者才能真正理解。
        *   *(Note: 晦澀難懂 (huìsè nándǒng) means "abstruse and hard to understand")*

4.  **Context: Nature/Weather (Verb - to block light/view)**
    *   **English:** Heavy clouds **obscured** the sun, casting a gloomy shadow over the landscape.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 厚重的雲層**遮蔽**了太陽，給大地投下陰沉的陰影。
        *   *(Note: 遮蔽 (zhēbì) means "to cover" or "to block")*

5.  **Context: Politics/Current Events (Verb - to hide/confuse facts)**
    *   **English:** The politician tried to **obscure** the truth about the scandal with misleading statements.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位政客試圖用誤導性言論來**掩蓋**醜聞的真相。
        *   *(Note: 掩蓋 (yǎngài) means "to cover up" or "to conceal")*

6.  **Context: Business/Personal Decisions (Adjective - vague/unclear motive)**
    *   **English:** His motives for resigning remained **obscure**, fueling much speculation.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他辭職的動機依然**不明**，引發了許多猜測。
        *   *(Note: 不明 (bùmíng) means "unclear" or "unknown")*

---

Understanding "obscure" in these different contexts and recognizing its common collocations will be very helpful for both the Vocabulary and Reading Comprehension sections of the GRE. Good luck with your studies!

## shirk

For your GRE studies, understanding words like "shirk" deeply will be very beneficial!

---

### Meaning of "Shirk"

**Definition:**
To **shirk** means to avoid or neglect a duty, responsibility, or task, especially out of laziness, fear, or a desire to avoid an unpleasant situation. It implies an intentional evasion of something that one is obligated or expected to do.

**Part of Speech:** Verb

**Key Nuance:** It's not just *not doing* something, but actively *avoiding* doing something you *should* be doing.

---

### Common Collocations

The most common collocations for "shirk" involve the things people avoid doing:

*   **shirk responsibility**
*   **shirk one's duty**
*   **shirk work**
*   **shirk an obligation**
*   **shirk a task**
*   **shirk one's burden** (less common than the others, but still valid)

---

### Example Sentences and Traditional Chinese Translations

Here are multiple example sentences in various contexts:

1.  **Context: Professional/Work Ethic**
    *   **English:** A good leader never **shirks their responsibilities**, even when faced with difficult decisions.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 一位好的領導者即使面對艱難的抉擇，也絕不**逃避自己的責任**。

2.  **Context: Civic Duty/Societal Obligation**
    *   **English:** Citizens must not **shirk their civic duty** to vote, as it is essential for a healthy democracy.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 公民絕不可**逃避投票的公民義務**，因為這對健康的民主至關重要。

3.  **Context: Personal Commitment/Promise**
    *   **English:** He tried to **shirk his promise** to help move furniture, claiming he had a sudden headache.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他試圖**推卸幫忙搬運家具的承諾**，聲稱自己突然頭痛。

4.  **Context: Facing an Unpleasant Reality/Problem**
    *   **English:** The company could no longer **shirk the need** for a major overhaul of its outdated systems.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 該公司再也無法**迴避對其過時系統進行徹底檢修的必要性**。

5.  **Context: Consequences of Avoidance**
    *   **English:** If you **shirk your duties** now, you'll only create more problems for yourself in the future.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 如果你現在**逃避職責**，未來只會為自己製造更多問題。

---

**GRE Tip:** When you encounter "shirk" on the GRE, think about synonyms like *evade*, *dodge*, *avoid*, *neglect*, or *skirt*. It often implies a deliberate and perhaps morally questionable avoidance.

## morose

Let's break down the word "morose" for your GRE studies.

---

### Meaning of "Morose"

**Morose** (adjective) means:
*   **Sullen and ill-tempered.**
*   **Characterized by a gloomy disposition.**
*   **Melancholy, sad, or depressed in a quiet, withdrawn way.**

It's more than just "sad"; it implies a persistent, often uncommunicative gloominess, sometimes accompanied by irritability or a sour attitude. A morose person might brood, sulk, or simply refuse to engage.

**Synonyms often tested on the GRE:** gloomy, sullen, melancholy, despondent, glum, crestfallen, doleful, saturnine.

---

### Common Collocations for "Morose"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these can help you use the word naturally and understand it in context.

*   **People/Personalities:**
    *   **morose person/individual**
    *   **morose mood/temper/disposition**
    *   **morose character**
*   **Expressions/Demeanor:**
    *   **morose expression/face**
    *   **morose look**
    *   **morose silence**
    *   **morose demeanor**
*   **Verbs associated with "morose":**
    *   **become morose**
    *   **grow morose**
    *   **look morose**
    *   **feel morose**
    *   **remain morose**
*   **Adverbs modifying "morose":**
    *   **deeply morose**
    *   **unusually morose**
    *   **strangely morose**

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its use, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Describing a general mood or state after an event.**
    *   **English:** After losing the championship game, the entire team was **morose**, sitting in silence in the locker room.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 輸掉冠軍賽後，整個團隊都**悶悶不樂**，在更衣室裡沉默不語。

2.  **Context: Describing a person's typical disposition or reaction.**
    *   **English:** She tends to become quite **morose** whenever she has to deal with paperwork, finding it tedious and frustrating.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 每當她必須處理文書工作時，她就會變得相當**陰鬱**，覺得既乏味又令人沮喪。

3.  **Context: Describing a physical manifestation of the mood (e.g., facial expression).**
    *   **English:** His **morose** expression made it clear he was not enjoying the party, despite everyone's efforts to cheer him up.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他那**愁眉不展**的表情清楚地表明他不喜歡這個派對，儘管大家努力想讓他高興起來。

4.  **Context: Describing an atmosphere or setting.**
    *   **English:** The old, abandoned house, with its perpetually drawn curtains and overgrown garden, had a distinctly **morose** air about it.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那棟老舊廢棄的房子，窗簾總是拉著，花園雜草叢生，瀰漫著一種明顯**陰鬱**的氛圍。

5.  **Context: Showing a contrast or a surprising change in temperament.**
    *   **English:** Usually so outgoing and jovial, Sarah became surprisingly **morose** after receiving the bad news about her scholarship application.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 平常總是那麼開朗活潑的莎拉，在收到關於她獎學金申請的壞消息後，變得出奇地**沮喪**。

---

I hope this comprehensive explanation helps you master "morose" for your GRE test! Good luck with your studies!

## ignominious

Okay, let's break down "ignominious" for your GRE prep!

---

### Understanding "Ignominious"

**Meaning:**
**Ignominious** (adjective) means deserving or causing public disgrace or shame. It describes something that is deeply humiliating, dishonorable, or shameful, especially in a public context. It implies a loss of dignity and reputation.

*   **Etymology Tip:** The word comes from Latin *ignominia*, meaning "disgrace," which is derived from *in-* (not) + *nomen* (name). So, literally, it means "having no name" or "having lost one's good name," emphasizing the loss of reputation.

**Synonyms:** Shameful, disgraceful, dishonorable, humiliating, ignoble, discreditable, contemptible.

---

### Common Collocations for "Ignominious"

"Ignominious" very frequently modifies words related to defeat, failure, or a negative ending.

*   **Ignominious defeat/loss:** A very bad and humiliating loss.
*   **Ignominious end/exit/fall:** A shameful or disgraceful conclusion or departure.
*   **Ignominious failure:** A complete and embarrassing failure.
*   **Ignominious retreat:** A shameful withdrawal.
*   **Ignominious disgrace:** Intense public shame.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples to show how "ignominious" is used, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Military/Warfare**
    *   **English:** The once-mighty empire suffered an **ignominious defeat** at the hands of a much smaller rebel force.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 曾經強大的帝國在一支規模小得多的叛軍手裡蒙受了**恥辱的慘敗**。

2.  **Context: Sports**
    *   **English:** Despite being top seeds, the team faced an **ignominious exit** from the tournament after losing to the lowest-ranked competitor.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管是頭號種子，這支隊伍在輸給排名最低的對手後，卻迎來了**恥辱性的錦標賽出局**。

3.  **Context: Politics/Public Life**
    *   **English:** The corrupt official's career ended in **ignominious disgrace** after his embezzlement scheme was exposed.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位腐敗官員的職業生涯，在貪污計劃被揭露後，以**可恥的下場**告終。

4.  **Context: Business/Projects**
    *   **English:** After years of development and billions of dollars spent, the ambitious tech project met an **ignominious end** when its core technology proved unworkable.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 經過多年的開發和數十億美元的投入，這個雄心勃勃的科技項目因為其核心技術證明不可行而迎來了**可恥的結局**。

5.  **Context: Personal Failure/Dishonor**
    *   **English:** His attempt to cheat on the exam led to an **ignominious expulsion** from the university, forever tainting his academic record.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他在考試中作弊的企圖導致了從大學的**可恥開除**，永遠玷污了他的學業紀錄。

---

By understanding its core meaning, common collocations, and seeing it in varied contexts, you'll be well-prepared for "ignominious" on the GRE! Good luck!

## garrulous

Okay, let's break down "garrulous" for your GRE studies!

---

## Garrulous

### Meaning

**Garrulous** (adj.) means **excessively talkative, especially on trivial matters.**

It describes someone who talks a great deal, often rambling or going on about unimportant things. The connotation is usually slightly negative, implying that the person's talkativeness can be tedious, tiresome, or overwhelming to others, rather than simply being friendly or engaging (like "loquacious" or "voluble" can sometimes imply). It suggests a tendency to dominate conversations and a lack of conciseness.

*   **Synonyms:** talkative, loquacious, voluble, chatty, long-winded, rambling, effusive.
*   **Antonyms:** taciturn, reticent, concise, laconic, reserved.

### Common Collocations

Here are some common phrases where "garrulous" frequently appears:

*   **Garrulous person / man / woman:** The most common use, describing an individual.
*   **Garrulous old man / old woman:** A classic stereotype often used in literature.
*   **Garrulous nature / disposition:** Referring to someone's inherent tendency to talk a lot.
*   **Garrulous chatter / monologue / ramblings:** Describing the excessive talk itself.
*   **Garrulous companion / friend:** A friend who talks too much.
*   **Garrulous remarks / observations:** Specific comments that are overly talkative.

### Example Sentences and Translations

Here are multiple example sentences in various contexts, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Describing a personality trait**
    *   **English:** Despite her advanced age, my **garrulous** neighbor could talk for hours about her life story, often repeating the same anecdotes.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管年事已高，我那位**喋喋不休的**鄰居仍能連續說上好幾個小時自己的生平故事，而且經常重複相同的軼事。

2.  **Context: Professional/Meeting setting**
    *   **English:** His **garrulous** nature made team meetings unnecessarily long, as he tended to digress on every topic.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他那**話多的**性格讓團隊會議變得不必要地冗長，因為他總是習慣對每個話題都離題。

3.  **Context: Social interaction/Annoyance**
    *   **English:** The new guest quickly earned a reputation as being **garrulous**, monopolizing every conversation with tales of his own adventures.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這位新客人很快就贏得了**愛說話的**名聲，他用自己的冒險故事壟斷了所有對話。

4.  **Context: Character description (e.g., in literature)**
    *   **English:** The author portrayed the innkeeper as a **garrulous** but kind-hearted man, always ready with a story or local gossip.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 作者將這位客棧老闆描繪成一個**話雖多卻心地善良的**人，總是準備好分享故事或當地的八卦。

5.  **Context: Explaining a behavior**
    *   **English:** Nervous interviewees sometimes become **garrulous**, filling every silence with unnecessary details.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 緊張的受訪者有時會變得**多話**，用不必要的細節填滿每一個沉默的時刻。

6.  **Context: Focusing on the output of garrulousness**
    *   **English:** After an hour of his **garrulous ramblings** about stamp collecting, I politely excused myself.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在聽了他一個小時關於集郵的**喋喋不休的絮叨**後，我禮貌地找藉口離開了。

---

When you encounter "garrulous" on the GRE, remember its core meaning of *excessive* and often *unwelcome* talkativeness. Good luck with your studies!

## ameliorate

That's an excellent word for the GRE! Let's break down "ameliorate."

## Ameliorate (Verb)

**Meaning:**
To make something *better*, *more tolerable*, or *less bad*. It implies an act of improvement or mitigation, often concerning a difficult, negative, or problematic situation, condition, or feeling. It's about taking steps to lessen a burden or enhance a state.

*   **Synonyms (for GRE purposes):** improve, enhance, alleviate, mitigate, relieve, amend, rectify, better.
*   **Antonyms:** worsen, aggravate, exacerbate, deteriorate.

**Common Collocations:**

"Ameliorate" is often used with nouns that describe negative or challenging situations. You'll frequently see it paired with:

*   **Situations/Conditions:**
    *   ameliorate the **situation**
    *   ameliorate the **condition**
    *   ameliorate the **crisis**
    *   ameliorate the **impact/effects**
    *   ameliorate the **circumstances**
*   **Problems/Hardships:**
    *   ameliorate **hardship**
    *   ameliorate **suffering**
    *   ameliorate **pain**
    *   ameliorate **difficulties**
    *   ameliorate a **problem**
    *   ameliorate **tensions**
*   **Efforts/Measures:**
    *   **efforts** to ameliorate
    *   **measures** to ameliorate
    *   **policies** designed to ameliorate

---

## Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are several examples demonstrating "ameliorate" in different scenarios, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Social/Economic Policy**
    *   **English:** The government introduced new policies to **ameliorate** the economic hardships faced by low-income families.
    *   **Explanation:** The policies are designed to make the difficult economic situation *better* for these families.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 政府推出新政策以**改善**低收入家庭面臨的經濟困境。

2.  **Context: Medical/Health**
    *   **English:** While there's no cure, the new medication can significantly **ameliorate** the symptoms of the chronic illness.
    *   **Explanation:** The medication doesn't fix the illness, but it makes the symptoms *less severe* or *more bearable*.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 雖然無法治癒，但這種新藥可以顯著**改善**慢性病的症狀。

3.  **Context: Environmental Efforts**
    *   **English:** Community clean-up efforts are helping to **ameliorate** the pollution in the local river.
    *   **Explanation:** The clean-up is working to make the polluted state of the river *better*.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 社區清潔工作正有助於**改善**當地河流的污染狀況。

4.  **Context: Interpersonal Relationships**
    *   **English:** A simple apology can often **ameliorate** a tense situation between friends.
    *   **Explanation:** The apology serves to make the strained relationship *less tense* or *more peaceful*.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 一句簡單的道歉通常可以**改善**朋友之間緊張的局面。

5.  **Context: Business/Project Management**
    *   **English:** The project manager implemented a new workflow to **ameliorate** the delays in the development process.
    *   **Explanation:** The new workflow was put in place to make the problem of delays *less impactful* or to *reduce* them.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 專案經理實施了新的工作流程，以**改善**開發過程中的延誤。

6.  **Context: Abstract/Historical**
    *   **English:** Throughout history, activists have worked tirelessly to **ameliorate** social injustices.
    *   **Explanation:** Activists strive to make unfair social conditions *better* or *more just*.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 縱觀歷史，社會活動家們一直不懈努力，以**改善**社會不公。

Knowing "ameliorate" and its common contexts and collocations will certainly help you on the GRE, especially in Sentence Equivalence and Text Completion questions!

## abase

Let's break down the word "abase" for your GRE studies.

---

### **Meaning of "Abase"**

**Pronunciation:** /əˈbeɪs/ (uh-bays)

**Part of Speech:** Verb

**Definition:**
To **lower in rank, prestige, or esteem; to humble or degrade**. It often implies an act of diminishing someone's dignity, standing, or self-respect, either by oneself (self-abasement) or by others. It suggests a forceful or demeaning reduction to a lower state or position.

**Key Idea for GRE:** Think of "abase" as meaning "to bring low" or "to humiliate." It's more than just lowering; it carries a connotation of disgrace or loss of dignity.

**Synonyms:** Humble, demean, degrade, debase, mortify, humiliate.
**Antonyms:** Elevate, exalt, honor, dignify, uplift.

---

### **Common Collocation**

The most common and significant collocation for "abase" is:

*   **Abase oneself:** This phrase means to humble or demean oneself, often out of shame, submission, or a desire for forgiveness. It's about voluntarily (or involuntarily, due to circumstances) lowering one's own pride, dignity, or status.

While less common, you can also see:

*   **Abase [someone/something]:** This implies one person or thing bringing another person or thing down in status, dignity, or quality.
    *   *Example:* To abase one's dignity, to abase an art form.

---

### **Multiple Example Sentences in Various Contexts**

Here are examples to illustrate its usage in different contexts, with Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Self-abasement due to mistake or humility.**
    *   **English:** After his public mistake, he had to **abase himself** and apologize to the entire community.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他在公開場合犯錯後，不得不**自貶身分** (or **自貶**) 並向整個社區道歉。
    *   *Explanation:* Here, "abase himself" refers to him humbling his pride and dignity to express sincere remorse.

2.  **Context: Someone else degrading another person.**
    *   **English:** The cruel manager tried to **abase his employees** by constantly belittling their efforts.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 那位殘酷的經理不斷貶低員工的努力，試圖**貶抑他們**。
    *   *Explanation:* The manager is actively trying to lower the employees' self-esteem and standing.

3.  **Context: Lowering the quality or integrity of something abstract.**
    *   **English:** Many argued that the reality show would **abase the art form** by focusing on sensationalism rather than talent.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 許多人認為該實境秀只專注於譁眾取寵而非才能，會**貶損該藝術形式**。
    *   *Explanation:* The show is seen as reducing the value and dignity of the art form itself.

4.  **Context: Historical/Political submission.**
    *   **English:** In feudal times, vassals often had to **abase themselves** before their lords to secure protection or favors.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在封建時代，附庸們為了獲得保護或恩惠，常須在領主面前**自貶身份** (or **屈尊**)。
    *   *Explanation:* Vassals had to show extreme deference and humility, essentially lowering their own status, to their powerful lords.

5.  **Context: Feeling degraded (passive voice).**
    *   **English:** Even though she had done nothing wrong, the constant false accusations made her feel **abased** and unworthy.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 即使她沒有做錯任何事，但持續的誣告讓她感到**被貶低**且不值。
    *   *Explanation:* The accusations made her feel humiliated and diminished in her own eyes.

---

By understanding "abase" with its core meaning, common collocations, and varied examples, you'll be well-prepared to encounter it on the GRE! Good luck!

## cipher

That's a great word for GRE prep, as "cipher" has multiple distinct meanings, and the test often targets such words!

Let's break down "cipher" comprehensively.

---

### Understanding "Cipher"

"Cipher" (pronounced /ˈsaɪfər/) can function as both a **noun** and a **verb**, and its meanings vary significantly.

#### 1. As a Noun (Most Common Meanings)

*   **Meaning 1: A Secret Code or Encoded Message**
    *   **Definition:** A secret way of writing, especially one using a system of symbols or letters substituted for ordinary ones; a coded message.
    *   **Context:** Used in cryptography, espionage, or any situation requiring secure communication.
    *   **Analogy:** Think of it as the "language" of a secret message, or the message itself once it's been encoded.

*   **Meaning 2: A Person of No Importance or Influence; a Nonentity**
    *   **Definition:** A person or thing of no importance; a nonentity; a nobody.
    *   **Context:** Often used to describe someone who is titular but lacks real power, or whose contributions are negligible. This is a very common figurative use.
    *   **Analogy:** Like the number zero in a sequence – it holds a place, but has no intrinsic value of its own. This is a crucial meaning for the GRE.

*   **Meaning 3: The Numeral Zero (0)**
    *   **Definition:** The arithmetical symbol 0; naught; zero.
    *   **Context:** Less common in everyday use compared to "zero," but technically correct. More likely to appear in historical or mathematical contexts.

#### 2. As a Verb

*   **Meaning 1: To Encode or Decode**
    *   **Definition:** To write in or convert into cipher; to encrypt or decrypt.
    *   **Context:** Directly related to the noun meaning of a "secret code."

*   **Meaning 2: To Calculate or Compute (Archaic/Formal)**
    *   **Definition:** To perform arithmetic calculations; to compute.
    *   **Context:** This meaning is more archaic or formal, often implying working out numbers by hand or mentally. Less common in modern English.

---

### Common Collocations

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these helps you understand the nuance and natural usage.

**For "cipher" (Meaning 1: Secret Code):**

*   **break a cipher:** To solve or decode a secret message.
*   **decipher a cipher:** To interpret a coded message.
*   **encode/write in cipher:** To put a message into a secret code.
*   **complex cipher:** A difficult-to-solve code.
*   **unbreakable cipher:** A code that is theoretically impossible to crack.
*   **a key to the cipher:** The method or information needed to decode.

**For "cipher" (Meaning 2: Person of No Importance):**

*   **a mere cipher:** Emphasizes the insignificance of the person. (Very common!)
*   **become a cipher:** To lose influence or importance.
*   **treat someone as a cipher:** To disregard someone's opinions or contributions.

**For "cipher" (Meaning 3: Zero):**

*   **a string of ciphers:** Refers to multiple zeros in a number (e.g., 1,000,000 has six ciphers).

**For "cipher" (Verb: Encode/Decode):**

*   **cipher a message:** To encode or decode a message.

**For "cipher" (Verb: Calculate):**

*   **cipher figures/numbers:** To perform calculations.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples showcasing the different meanings and their Traditional Chinese translations:

---

**Meaning 1 (Noun): A Secret Code or Encoded Message**

1.  **English:** During the war, spies used **complex ciphers** to communicate sensitive information without detection.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 戰爭期間，間諜使用**複雜的密碼**來傳遞敏感資訊而不被偵測。

2.  **English:** The cryptographer spent weeks trying to **break the enemy's cipher**, which seemed impenetrable at first.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 密碼學家花了好幾個星期試圖**破解敵人的密碼**，這密碼起初看起來是無法攻破的。

3.  **English:** The old diary was written entirely in a personal **cipher**, making it impossible for outsiders to read.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這本舊日記完全是用個人**密碼**寫的，讓外人無法閱讀。

---

**Meaning 2 (Noun): A Person of No Importance or Influence; a Nonentity**

4.  **English:** Despite his impressive title, the vice-president was a **mere cipher** in the company, with no real power or influence.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管他有著令人印象深刻的頭銜，這位副總裁在公司裡卻是個**無足輕重的人物**，沒有實際的權力或影響力。

5.  **English:** She felt like a **cipher** in the large organization, her contributions going unnoticed and her opinions dismissed.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她在龐大的組織中感覺自己**微不足道**，她的貢獻被忽視，意見也被駁回。

6.  **English:** Historically, many monarchs were little more than **ciphers**, with the true power resting with their advisors or military leaders.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 歷史上，許多君主不過是**虛位元首**，真正的權力掌握在他們的顧問或軍事領袖手中。

---

**Meaning 3 (Noun): The Numeral Zero (0)**

7.  **English:** The number 10,000 contains four **ciphers** after the digit one.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 數字 10,000 在數字一之後包含四個**零**。

8.  **English:** In some older mathematical texts, the concept of a **cipher** (zero) was a revolutionary idea.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在一些較古老的數學文獻中，**零**（cipher）的概念是一個革命性的想法。

---

**Meaning 4 (Verb): To Encode or Decode**

9.  **English:** He learned to **cipher** the messages using a simple substitution method taught to him by his grandfather.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他學會了用祖父教他的簡單替換法來**編碼**訊息。

10. **English:** The ancient tablet was covered in symbols, and archaeologists worked for years to **cipher** its meaning.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 這塊古老的石碑上覆蓋著符號，考古學家們花了數年時間來**解讀**它的意義。

---

**Meaning 5 (Verb): To Calculate or Compute (Archaic/Formal)**

11. **English:** Before calculators, merchants often had to **cipher** complex sums quickly in their heads.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在計算機出現之前，商人常常必須在腦中快速**計算**複雜的總數。

12. **English:** He sat at his desk, **ciphering** out the quarterly profits with meticulous care.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他坐在書桌前，小心翼翼地**計算**著季度利潤。

---

By understanding these distinct meanings and their common uses, you'll be well-prepared for any "cipher" questions on the GRE! Good luck with your studies!

## rancor

Okay, let's break down "rancor" for your GRE prep!

---

## Rancor

**Meaning:**
**Rancor** (noun) refers to **bitter, deep-seated ill will or resentment, often long-lasting.** It's not just a momentary anger, but a profound and often smoldering feeling of hatred or animosity. Think of it as a grudge that has grown into a festering wound.

**Key characteristics:**
*   **Deep-seated:** It's not superficial.
*   **Bitter:** It carries a sense of harshness and unpleasantness.
*   **Long-lasting:** It persists over time, sometimes for years or even generations.
*   **Ill will/Resentment:** It implies a desire to harm or see someone suffer, or a profound sense of having been wronged.

**Common Collocations:**

Words that frequently appear with "rancor" help you understand its typical usage:

*   **Verbs:**
    *   **harbor rancor:** to hold or keep a feeling of rancor in one's mind.
    *   **feel rancor:** to experience the emotion.
    *   **express rancor:** to show or voice the bitterness.
    *   **foster/nurture rancor:** to encourage or allow resentment to grow.
    *   **dispel/overcome rancor:** to get rid of or conquer the bitterness.
    *   **be filled with rancor:** to be full of this feeling.

*   **Adjectives describing rancor:**
    *   **deep rancor:** very profound.
    *   **bitter rancor:** intensely unpleasant and hostile.
    *   **lingering rancor:** persisting for a long time.
    *   **old rancor:** from a past event or conflict.
    *   **smoldering/seething rancor:** hidden but intense and ready to erupt.
    *   **personal rancor:** related to an individual's specific feelings.

*   **Prepositions:**
    *   **rancor towards/against someone/something:** indicating the target of the resentment.
    *   **rancor between** (parties/groups).
    *   **without rancor:** doing something without ill will.

---

**Example Sentences in Various Contexts (with Traditional Chinese Translation):**

1.  **Context: Political/Historical Conflict**
    *   **English:** Years of political rivalry had fostered **deep rancor** between the two opposing parties, making compromise almost impossible.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 多年的政治競爭在兩個敵對政黨之間滋生了**深刻的積怨**，使得妥協幾乎不可能。

2.  **Context: Personal Relationships/Past Hurt**
    *   **English:** Despite his apology, a **lingering rancor** from the betrayal prevented her from truly forgiving him.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管他已經道歉，但由於過去的背叛，一種**揮之不去的怨恨**使她無法真正原諒他。

3.  **Context: Overcoming Adversity/Moving On**
    *   **English:** It's difficult to move forward and find peace when you **harbor so much rancor** in your heart against those who wronged you.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 當你心中對那些傷害你的人**懷有如此多的怨恨**時，很難向前邁進並找到平靜。

4.  **Context: Formal/Legal Setting**
    *   **English:** The judge noted the defendant's **evident rancor** and lack of remorse in his testimony against the victim.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 法官注意到被告在對受害者的證詞中表現出**明顯的怨恨**和缺乏悔意。

5.  **Context: Professional/Competitive Environment**
    *   **English:** Even after their fierce debate, the two scientists were able to discuss their findings **without rancor**, respecting each other's intellect.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 即使在激烈的辯論之後，這兩位科學家仍能**不帶怨恨地**討論他們的發現，並尊重彼此的才智。

6.  **Context: Social/Community Issues**
    *   **English:** The community leader urged everyone to set aside their **old rancor** and work together for the common good.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 社區領袖敦促大家放下**舊有的積怨**，為共同利益而努力。

---

**GRE Tip:**
On the GRE, "rancor" often appears in questions testing your understanding of negative emotions, conflicts, or the aftermath of disputes. Look for clues in the sentence that suggest a deep, persistent, and bitter negative feeling. Synonyms like "animosity," "enmity," "malice," and "bitterness" are good to keep in mind.

## vestige

Okay, let's break down the word "vestige" for your GRE preparation.

---

### Understanding "Vestige"

**Meaning:**
A **vestige** (pronounced **VES-tij**) is a trace or remnant of something that is disappearing or has almost completely disappeared. It refers to a small surviving part of something that once existed, but is now mostly gone, faded, or no longer holds its original importance or function.

*   **Key Idea:** It implies something *old*, *faded*, *minimal*, and often *without its original purpose*. It's not just "a piece," but a *ghost* or *echo* of what was.
*   **Part of Speech:** Noun

**Synonyms often seen on the GRE:**
*   **Trace:** The most direct synonym.
*   **Remnant:** Something left over.
*   **Relic:** An object surviving from an earlier time, especially one of historical or sentimental interest.
*   **Residue:** A small amount of something that remains after the main part has gone or been removed.

---

### Common Collocations of "Vestige"

Collocations are words that frequently go together. Knowing these will help you recognize and use "vestige" naturally.

1.  **Vestige of something:** (The most common structure)
    *   *Examples:* vestige of hope, vestige of an ancient civilization, vestige of a former glory, vestige of a tradition, vestige of doubt.
2.  **Faint vestige:** Emphasizes how minimal the trace is.
3.  **Only/Last vestige:** Implies it's the sole remaining part.
4.  **Not a vestige (left/remained):** Used to convey that absolutely nothing is left.
5.  **Scarcely a vestige:** Similar to "not a vestige," indicating almost nothing.
6.  **Vestigial (adjective form):** Often used in a biological context for organs that have lost their original function (e.g., a human appendix is a vestigial organ). While "vestigial" is an adjective, it directly relates to the noun "vestige."

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples to help you understand its usage in different scenarios:

1.  **Context: Historical/Physical Remains**
    *   **Sentence:** "Only a few crumbling walls remain as the last **vestiges** of the ancient Roman fort."
    *   **Explanation:** The walls are the minimal, surviving traces of a once grand structure.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 僅剩幾堵殘破的牆壁，作為古羅馬堡壘僅存的**遺跡**。
    *   (Jǐn shèng jǐ dǔ cánpò de qiángbì, zuòwéi gǔ Luómǎ bǎolěi jǐncún de **yíjì**.)

2.  **Context: Abstract/Emotional State**
    *   **Sentence:** "Despite the overwhelming odds, she held onto a faint **vestige** of hope that things would improve."
    *   **Explanation:** Her hope was very small, barely there, but still a trace of it remained.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管困難重重，她仍抱持著一絲微弱的**希望殘跡**，相信事情會好轉。
    *   (Jǐnguǎn kùnnán chóngchóng, tā réng bàochízhe yī sī wēiruò de **xīwàng cánjī**, xiāngxìn shìqíng huì hǎozhuǎn.)

3.  **Context: Biological/Evolutionary**
    *   **Sentence:** "The human appendix is often cited as a **vestige** of an organ that once played a more significant role in digestion for our ancestors."
    *   **Explanation:** The appendix is a remnant organ that has largely lost its original function.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 人類的闌尾常被視為一個器官的**殘跡**，這個器官在我們祖先的消化系統中曾扮演更重要的角色。
    *   (Rénlèi de lánwěi cháng bèi shìwéi yīgè qìguān de **cánjī**, zhège qìguān zài wǒmen zǔxiān de xiāohuà xìtǒng zhōng céng bànyǎn gèng zhòngyào de juésè.)

4.  **Context: Societal/Cultural Practices**
    *   **Sentence:** "In some remote villages, you can still find **vestiges** of ancient traditions that have long disappeared in urban areas."
    *   **Explanation:** These villages retain small, surviving parts of old customs that are mostly gone elsewhere.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 在一些偏遠的村莊，你仍能找到在城市地區早已消失的古老傳統的**殘餘**。
    *   (Zài yīxiē piānyuǎn de cūnzhuāng, nǐ réng néng zhǎodào zài chéngshì dìqū zǎoyǐ xiāoshī de gǔlǎo chuántǒng de **cányú**.)

5.  **Context: Complete Disappearance/Loss**
    *   **Sentence:** "After the devastating fire, not a **vestige** of the original structure remained; it was completely destroyed."
    *   **Explanation:** There was absolutely no trace or remnant left.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 毀滅性的大火之後，原始建築物連一點**遺跡**都沒有留下；它被徹底摧毀了。
    *   (Huǐmiè xìng de dàhuǒ zhīhòu, yuánshǐ jiànzhúwù lián yīdiǎn **yíjì** dōu méiyǒu liúxià; tā bèi chèdǐ cuīhuǐle.)

6.  **Context: Fading Influence/Power**
    *   **Sentence:** "His once immense power had dwindled to a mere **vestige**, with most of his former allies abandoning him."
    *   **Explanation:** His power was reduced to a very small, almost insignificant remainder.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他曾經巨大的權力已縮減到微不足道的**殘餘**，大多數以前的盟友都已離他而去。
    *   (Tā céngjīng jùdà de quánlì yǐ suōjiǎn dào wēibùzúdào de **cányú**, dà duōshù yǐqián de méngyǒu dōu yǐ lí tā ér qù.)

---

By understanding "vestige" as a *faint, small, or insignificant trace of something that was once much larger or more important*, and practicing with these collocations and example sentences, you'll be well-prepared for its appearance on the GRE. Good luck!

## quibble

Let's break down "quibble" for your GRE prep!

---

### Understanding "Quibble"

**Meaning:**

"Quibble" refers to **a slight objection or criticism, especially one based on a trivial detail or a minor point**. When used as a verb, it means to **argue or raise objections about such a trivial matter, often to avoid the main issue or to find fault unnecessarily.**

It carries a connotation of being petty, overly critical, or evasive.

**Parts of Speech:**

1.  **Verb:** To argue or object about something insignificant.
2.  **Noun:** A minor objection or criticism.

---

### Common Collocations

"Quibble" often appears with prepositions that indicate the subject of the trivial dispute:

*   **Quibble about:** "They quibbled about the wording."
*   **Quibble over:** "They quibbled over the price."
*   **Quibble with:** "I have no quibble with your main point." (This is usually when used as a noun or when expressing disagreement *with a person* regarding a minor point.)

As a noun, you might hear:

*   **A minor quibble:** Emphasizes its insignificance.
*   **A petty quibble:** Highlights the trivial nature and perhaps the annoyance it causes.
*   **No quibbles:** Indicates complete agreement or satisfaction.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its usage:

1.  **Context: Workplace/Decision-making (Verb)**
    *   **English:** Management spent too much time **quibbling over** minor details of the budget instead of focusing on the big picture.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 管理層花費了太多時間在預算的小細節上**斤斤計較**，而不是專注於大局。
        *   *(Explanation: "斤斤計較" (jīnjīn jìjiào) perfectly captures the idea of being overly concerned with trivial matters.)*

2.  **Context: Personal relationships/Everyday arguments (Verb)**
    *   **English:** They often **quibble about** trivial things like who left the cap off the toothpaste or whose turn it is to do the dishes.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他們經常為一些瑣碎的事情**爭論不休**，比如誰沒蓋好牙膏蓋子或輪到誰洗碗。
        *   *(Explanation: "爭論不休" (zhēnglùn bùxiū) means to argue endlessly, which fits the repetitive nature of petty arguments.)*

3.  **Context: Academic/Critical analysis (Verb)**
    *   **English:** While some critics might **quibble with** a few of the author's footnotes, the overall argument remains sound.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管有些評論家可能會對作者的幾個註腳**吹毛求疵**，但整體論點依然站得住腳。
        *   *(Explanation: "吹毛求疵" (chuī máo qiú cī) is a common idiom meaning to find fault by splitting hairs or nitpicking.)*

4.  **Context: Urgent situation/Dismissing trivialities (Verb)**
    *   **English:** There's no time to **quibble**; we need to make a decision immediately.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 沒有時間再**糾結於細枝末節**了；我們需要立即做出決定。
        *   *(Explanation: "糾結於細枝末節" (jiūjié yú xìzhīmòjié) means to get bogged down or entangled in minor, unimportant details.)*

5.  **Context: Expressing a minor objection (Noun)**
    *   **English:** My only **quibble** with the new policy is that it might be difficult to implement evenly across all departments.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 我對這項新政策**唯一的小異議**是，它可能很難在所有部門中均勻實施。
        *   *(Explanation: "小異議" (xiǎo yìyì) directly translates to "small objection," which is the noun form of "quibble.")*

6.  **Context: Indicating full approval (Noun)**
    *   **English:** After reviewing the contract, she had **no quibbles** and signed it right away.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 審閱合約後，她**沒有任何異議**，便立刻簽署了。
        *   *(Explanation: "沒有任何異議" (méiyǒu rènhé yìyì) means "had no objections whatsoever.")*

---

By understanding these nuances and seeing "quibble" in various contexts, you'll be well-prepared for its appearance on the GRE!

## decorum

Let's break down the word "decorum" for your GRE studies.

---

### Understanding "Decorum"

**Meaning:**
**Decorum** refers to behavior in keeping with good taste and propriety. It implies a sense of what is appropriate and polite, especially in a formal or serious setting. It's about maintaining dignity, respect, and adherence to social or professional norms. Think of it as the expected standard of conduct that shows respect for the situation, the people involved, and oneself.

**In simpler terms:** It's about acting properly and politely, especially when it really matters.

**Part of Speech:** Noun

---

### Common Collocations for "Decorum"

Collocations are words that often go together. Knowing these will help you use "decorum" naturally and understand it in context.

*   **Maintain decorum:** To keep up proper behavior.
*   **Observe decorum:** To follow rules of proper behavior.
*   **Preserve decorum:** To ensure proper behavior continues.
*   **Breach/Violate decorum:** To act in a way that breaks accepted standards of behavior.
*   **Lack of decorum:** The absence of proper behavior.
*   **Sense of decorum:** An innate understanding or feeling of what is proper.
*   **Strict decorum:** A very rigid adherence to proper behavior.
*   **Public decorum:** Proper behavior expected in public places.
*   **Professional decorum:** Expected conduct in a professional setting.
*   **Courtroom decorum:** The specific rules of behavior expected in a court of law.
*   **Parliamentary decorum:** The specific rules of behavior expected in a legislative body.

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples showcasing "decorum" in different situations, with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Formal/Legal Setting**
    *   **English:** The judge demanded **strict decorum** from everyone present in the courtroom, warning against any outbursts.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 法官要求法庭內所有在場人員保持**嚴格的莊重儀態**，並警告不得有任何情緒失控的行為。
    *   *(Pīnyīn: Fǎguān yāoqiú fǎtíng nèi suǒyǒu zàichǎng rényuán bǎochí **yángé de zhuāngzhòng yítài**, bìng jǐnggào bùdé yǒu rènhé qíngxù shīkòng de xíngwéi.)*

2.  **Context: Social Event/Etiquette**
    *   **English:** His sudden, loud laughter was considered a **breach of social decorum** at the solemn memorial service.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 他突然的大笑在莊嚴的追悼會上被視為**違反社交禮儀**的行為。
    *   *(Pīnyīn: Tā túrán de dàxiào zài zhuāngyán de zhuīdàohuì shàng bèi shìwéi **wéifǎn shèjiāo lǐyí** de xíngwéi.)*

3.  **Context: Political/Parliamentary Affairs**
    *   **English:** Despite the heated debate, the members of parliament managed to **maintain a degree of parliamentary decorum**, refraining from personal attacks.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 儘管辯論激烈，國會議員們仍設法**保持一定程度的議會禮節**，避免了人身攻擊。
    *   *(Pīnyīn: Jǐnguǎn biànlùn jīliè, guóhuì yìyuánmén réng shèfǎ **bǎochí yīdìng chéngdù de yìhuì lǐjié**, bìmiǎn le rénshēn gōngjí.)*

4.  **Context: Professional Environment**
    *   **English:** It is expected that all employees will **observe professional decorum** when interacting with clients, even under stressful circumstances.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 所有員工在與客戶互動時，即使在壓力之下，也應**遵守專業禮儀**。
    *   *(Pīnyīn: Suǒyǒu yuángōng zài yǔ kèhù hùdòng shí, jíshǐ zài yālì zhī xià, yě yīng **zūnshǒu zhuānyè lǐyí**.)*

5.  **Context: General Behavior/Lack Thereof**
    *   **English:** Her **lack of decorum** during the graduation ceremony, including talking loudly on her phone, was widely criticized.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 她在畢業典禮上**不得體**的行為，包括大聲講電話，受到了廣泛批評。
    *   *(Pīnyīn: Tā zài bìyè diǎnlǐ shàng **bùdétǐ** de xíngwéi, bāokuò dàshēng jiǎng diànhuà, shòudào le guǎngfàn pīpíng.)*
    *   *(Note: "不得體" (bùdétǐ) is a good, concise translation for "lack of decorum" in many contexts.)*

By understanding the core meaning, common collocations, and seeing it in varied contexts, you'll be well-prepared to tackle "decorum" on the GRE!

## desiccate

Let's break down the word "desiccate" for your GRE studies!

---

## Desiccate (Verb)

### Meaning

To **desiccate** means to remove the moisture from something, causing it to become completely dry. It often implies a process of extreme drying, to the point where the substance is preserved or, conversely, becomes brittle and lifeless.

**Key aspects of its meaning:**
1.  **To dry thoroughly:** To remove all the water or moisture from something.
2.  **To preserve by drying:** Often used in the context of food preservation or scientific samples.
3.  **To become dry:** (Intransitive) The substance itself dries up.
4.  **Figurative use:** To drain or dry up something non-physical, like emotions, inspiration, or spirit, leaving it barren or empty.

**Etymology:** From Latin *desiccare* "to dry up," from *de-* "down, away" + *siccare* "to dry."

### Common Collocations

"Desiccate" and its adjective form "desiccated" often appear with nouns that can be dried out, either intentionally or as a result of environmental conditions.

*   **Desiccated food/fruit/meat/vegetables:** (preserved by drying)
*   **Desiccated samples/specimens:** (in scientific contexts, for preservation)
*   **Desiccated soil/land/riverbeds:** (due to drought or extreme heat)
*   **Desiccated climate/environment:** (characterized by dryness)
*   **Desiccated body/remains/corpse:** (natural mummification)
*   **Desiccated leaves/plants:** (withered, dried up)
*   **Desiccated air:** (air from which moisture has been removed, often by a desiccant)
*   **To desiccate *by*... (e.g., heat, air, sun, chemical agents)**

---

### Example Sentences in Various Contexts

Here are multiple examples to illustrate its usage, along with their Traditional Chinese translations:

1.  **Context: Food Preservation**
    *   **English:** Ancient cultures would **desiccate** fruits and vegetables to preserve them for winter, a method still used today for dried apricots and raisins.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 古代文化會將水果和蔬菜**脫水**以保存過冬，這種方法至今仍用於製作杏乾和葡萄乾。
        *   (Gǔdài wénhuà huì jiāng shuǐguǒ hé shūcài **tuōshuǐ** yǐ bǎocún guòdōng, zhè zhǒng fāngfǎ zhìjīn réng yòng yú zhìzuò xìnggān hé pútáogān.)

2.  **Context: Natural Environment/Drought**
    *   **English:** The prolonged drought had completely **desiccated** the riverbeds and cracked the once-fertile soil, leading to widespread crop failure.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 長期的乾旱完全**乾枯**了河床，並使曾經肥沃的土壤龜裂，導致農作物大面積歉收。
        *   (Chángqí de gānhàn wánquán **gānkū** le héchuáng, bìng shǐ céngjīng féiwò de tǔrǎng guīliè, dǎozhì nóngzuòwù dà miànjī qiànshōu.)

3.  **Context: Scientific/Laboratory Use**
    *   **English:** For long-term storage, biological samples are often **desiccated** in a controlled environment to prevent microbial growth and decay.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 為了長期儲存，生物樣本通常會在受控環境中**脫水**，以防止微生物生長和腐爛。
        *   (Wèile chángqí chúchún, shēngwù yàngběn tōngcháng huì zài shòukòng huánjìng zhōng **tuōshuǐ**, yǐ fángzhǐ wēishēngwù shēngzhǎng hé fǔlàn.)

4.  **Context: Figurative/Emotional State**
    *   **English:** Years of unfulfilled dreams and relentless struggle can **desiccate** one's spirit, leaving a profound sense of emptiness and despair.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 多年未能實現的夢想和不懈的掙扎會**使人的精神枯竭**，留下深刻的空虛感和絕望。
        *   (Duōnián wèinéng shíxiàn de mèngxiǎng hé bùxiè de zhēngzhá huì **shǐ rén de jīngshén kūjié**, liú xià shēnkè de kōngxū gǎn hé juéwàng.)

5.  **Context: As an Adjective (Desiccated)**
    *   **English:** The archaeologists found a perfectly preserved, though **desiccated**, scroll in the desert tomb, its ancient script still legible.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 考古學家在沙漠墓穴中發現了一卷保存完好但**乾扁的**卷軸，其古老的文字仍然清晰可讀。
        *   (Kǎogǔ xuéjiā zài shāmò mùxué zhōng fāxiàn le yī juǎn bǎocún wánhǎo dàn **gānbiǎn de** juǎnzhou, qí gǔlǎo de wénzì réngrán qīngxī kědú.)

6.  **Context: Industrial/Chemical Process**
    *   **English:** Silica gel packets are commonly used to **desiccate** the air inside packaging, protecting sensitive electronics from moisture damage.
    *   **Traditional Chinese:** 矽膠包常被用於**吸乾**包裝內的空氣，保護敏感的電子產品免受濕氣損壞。
        *   (Xījiāo bāo cháng bèi yòng yú **xīgān** bāozhuāng nèi de kōngqì, bǎohù mǐngǎn de diànzǐ chǎnpǐn miǎn shòu shīqì sǔnhuài.)

---

Remember, for the GRE, pay attention to "desiccate" not just in its literal sense of drying, but also its metaphorical uses, as those are often tested to gauge your nuanced understanding of vocabulary. Good luck with your studies!
